Fresh-session rule: the substantive plan is merged, while the M0 amendment is usable only from a merged commit carrying its own exact-head approval artifact. The first implementation session begins with **M0P only in `popcre/ai-devops`**; each successor takes exactly the next open milestone, and no Jev schema migration starts until M0F's production double-run proof is recorded. External-repository outcomes M0P and M0Q are each followed by their own Oracle documentation-only `-C` closeout, which alone updates this STATUS table from immutable merge/release/install evidence; the external implementation session does not claim an Oracle status edit. Re-read the current milestone and downstream phases before editing, and use `fresh-session` at each natural cut. Whoever executes an Oracle step updates this table with a commit, test artifact, CI run, deployment ID, or exact rerunnable command.

Oracle handoff-count precondition: M0P and M0Q may proceed in `popcre/ai-devops`, but M0A cannot start until the oldest-first Oracle inventory is at or below five counted files after the standing legacy exception. The named owner is **Codex chat `01a0bf15-7842-7b73-a7f4-0a92b4138466` on `916-alien`**: immediately after this amendment merges, before this task closes, it must open and merge a separate current-main documentation-only closeout that verifies the amendment merge, confirms every implementation obligation is in this canonical plan and every review/dead-end remains recoverable from immutable Git/review artifacts, deletes only its now-finished `HANDOFF.d/2026-09-23T0641Z-916-codex-jev-plan-amendment.md`, and creates no replacement baton because the planning workstream is complete. That merge reduces the counted inventory from six to five; its merge SHA is M0A's required precondition evidence. If the closeout does not merge, this task remains unfinished and M0A stays blocked. A bounded milestone that completes and records its evidence in this STATUS table has no unfinished session state and therefore creates no handoff; this is the repository's existing “create a HANDOFF only for unfinished work” rule, not an exception. A milestone may start only when it can finish or roll back its isolated unmerged worktree in the owning session. If a genuine external blocker would require an unfinished baton while the inventory is five, it must stop before edits and assign a separate handoff-hygiene owner to make a legal slot; it may not create a sixth file or leave partial state. If a milestone actually carries an existing predecessor baton, the normal add-one/delete-one successor rule still applies.

---

## 1. The ultimate goal

POP Creations should spend expensive generative AI only where the Oracle actually needs writing, synthesis, or open-ended extraction. Fast, cheap, typed judgments should handle bounded questions such as “does this segment contain operational knowledge?”, “which known entity is named?”, “does this evidence support the claim?”, and “which approved capability best matches this request?”

When complete, the Oracle will:

- make selected classifications, routing decisions, rankings, and verification judgments at a fraction of the present generative-model cost;
- preserve or improve evidence quality by adding an independent semantic check where today the same model both creates and scores its own output;
- expose probability, confidence, latency, token, cost, and resolved-model data for every Jev decision;
- fall back safely when Jev is uncertain, unavailable, rate-limited, or not approved to receive the data;
- never let Jev weaken exact-quote validation, permissions, lifecycle rules, human approvals, or transactional safeguards.

**If a step conflicts with this goal, the goal wins — stop and flag it.** Lower token price is not success if recall, evidence quality, privacy, or employee trust gets worse.

## 2. What this application is

The Oracle is POP Creations / Spruce Line's evidence-backed enterprise knowledge system. Employees ask questions through the web and Teams, and administrators upload documents and review extracted claims, gaps, contradictions, taxonomy proposals, and synthesized Brain sections. Every important answer is intended to trace back to approved claims and quote-level evidence.

- Repository: `u2giants/theoracle`
- Target branch: `main`; the legacy branch-protection endpoint returned 404 on 2026-09-23, but that does not establish the absence of repository or organization rulesets. All work still uses a feature branch and pull request, and M0R must inventory the complete effective rule set, reconcile any overlap/conflict, and prove a no-bypass result before M0I grants workflow identity.
- Stack: TypeScript, Node 20+, pnpm/Turborepo, Next.js 16, Trigger.dev workers, Drizzle, Supabase
- Web production URL: `https://oracle.designflow.app`
- Vercel project: `prj_rP6Jlima7iK1paffEPhLqxlswGsC`
- Trigger.dev project: `proj_wgpzsvhmsopqhvwqaycn`
- Supabase production project: `eqccjfbyrywsqkxxpjvg` (`theoracle`, `us-east-1`)
- AI boundary today: `OracleAIClient` routes free-text or arbitrary structured generation through six direct providers: Anthropic, Vertex, Google, OpenAI, DeepSeek, and Qwen.

Jev is TypeSafe AI's text-only System One model. It does not write prose. It evaluates one text/JSON state against typed questions:

- `Choice`: one result from a known set, with probabilities and confidence;
- `Score`: a position on a defined ordered rubric, with probabilities and confidence;
- `Noul`: probability that a yes/no condition is true.

As documented on 2026-09-20, `jev-1.13.0` costs $0.042 per million input tokens, output tokens are free, the request limit is 64k tokens with a 32k state-plus-longest-question limit, and the primary training language is English. See [TypeSafe models](https://docs.typesafe.ai/models), [primitives](https://docs.typesafe.ai/primitives), and [known Jev 1.13 limitations](https://docs.typesafe.ai/model-jaggedness/jev-1.13).

## 3. What triggered this work

Albert requested a current review of TypeSafe AI / Jev and a whole-codebase audit to determine where its unusually low price could help the Oracle. The primary read-only audit ran against upstream `63cdcaa` on 2026-09-20 and covered `packages/ai`, workers, web/API/admin/MCP surfaces, Oracle engines, database/auth boundaries, documentation, deployment, and evaluation assets. The plan was subsequently rebased and re-audited against application code at `499d8b0`, including the merged connected-answer evidence reconciliation, canonical rendering, and independent semantic review path, then rebased onto documentation-only main `b7a7aca`.

The audit found real savings opportunities, but also a sharp architectural boundary: Jev is suitable for bounded semantic judgments, not generation. The Oracle currently pays generative models for several bounded decisions and sometimes trusts the generator's own confidence or classification. Other paths use brittle keyword/synonym heuristics that Jev could supplement. Conversely, the Oracle's highest-value work—claim discovery, exact evidence, workflow construction, chat, translation, and synthesis—cannot be moved to Jev without losing capability.

No Jev code, package, secret, setting, deployment, or production call exists yet. This plan is the build specification for issue #14.

## 4. Scope — in and out

### In scope

1. A separate TypeSafe decision client and typed question/result contract inside `@oracle/ai`.
2. Dedicated Oracle decision-run, subject, comparator, usage, cost, latency, health, and error observability for Jev calls, linked to existing generation model runs only when the current model supplies a comparator or retry.
3. Version-pinned, named, testable question packs with per-use-case thresholds and feature flags.
4. Shadow-first pilots, in this order:
   - MCP capability discovery;
   - bounded entity selection;
   - responsibility quote-candidate selection evaluation;
   - claim-extraction prefilter;
   - semantic claim/evidence support and auto-approval veto;
   - contradiction screening;
   - live Teams intervention screening;
   - retrieval reranking and chat-grounding verification;
   - the Jev decision/canary review and health workflow required by these pilots; broader admin advisory products remain deferred in Step 9C.
5. Privacy minimization, redaction where possible, version pinning, failure behavior, and rollback controls.
6. English and `zh-CN` evaluation where a use case can receive Chinese content.
7. Documentation and per-pilot live proof.

### Not in this plan

- Jev as a seventh `OracleProvider`, generative adapter, model-catalog entry, or auxiliary text-generation route.
- Replacing chat replies, claim discovery, claim summaries, reviewer questions, translations, transcript summaries, Brain prose, taxonomy naming, image transcription, workflow maps, responsibility records, embeddings, or JSON repair.
- Replacing exact quote/offset checks, source-pointer checks, schema validation, approved-status filters, permissions, authentication, direct-mention rules, safety confirmations, advisory locks, transactions, cooldowns, rate caps, or human approval.
- Automatically approving claims, merging entities, ingesting/dismissing meetings, assigning individual reviewers, or performing destructive actions from a Jev score.
- Reusing or overloading `model_runs` for Jev. Its required success boolean cannot represent a pending call, and its child tables have no durable policy-effect or subject-disposition contract. The foundation therefore includes the narrow decision-audit schema specified in Step 1.
- Changing the active R2 responsibility-reader prompt, matcher, budget, model, route, or production behavior. That workstream has its own plan and handoff.
- Moving every P2/P3 audit idea into production. Advisory pilots advance only when their measured value exceeds their complexity and privacy cost.

## 5. Current state of the code

### Repository and delivery state

- The plan baseline is clean `origin/main` at `b7a7acad0056acbc45c152fe1f2ac4730c107906`; PR #18 was documentation-only, so the audited application-code baseline remains `499d8b0dc3a726ea59dc93114cbb165e1f96f9d3`.
- There is no TypeSafe/Jev dependency or runtime integration.
- Issue #14 tracks this plan and is assigned to the originating Codex owner.
- This plan and its handoff are documentation-only; no code or production behavior is changed by landing them.

### Existing generation boundary

- `packages/ai/src/routes/types.ts:13` defines exactly six `OracleProvider` values.
- `packages/ai/src/providers/types.ts:153-184` defines the free-text/arbitrary-object provider adapter contract.
- `packages/ai/src/client/oracle-ai-client.ts:108-123` exposes `runText` and `runObject`; `runObject` accepts generated objects after schema validation, not semantic support validation.
- `packages/ai/src/routing/model-router.ts:107-140` selects the first successful configured generative route and uses fallbacks for provider failure, not task difficulty.
- `packages/db/src/schema.ts:1393-1417,1892-2020` stores generation-oriented model runs and usage. Those tables are useful dashboard precedents but cannot safely represent pending Jev decisions, behavior effects, source classifications, or replayable subject dispositions.
- `packages/db/src/schema.ts:1892-2020` provides usage detail, context-pack, and attempt records already used by production AI paths.

### Existing high-value seams

- Entity selection: `packages/ai/src/entity-planner-model.ts:55-154` asks a generative model to return known registry entities; `packages/ai/src/retrieval-plan.ts:227-276` still intersects the answer with allowed candidates.
- Domain routing: `packages/ai/src/retrieval-plan.ts:681-753,840-853` ranks domains with substring-hit counts; unmatched paraphrases fall back to broader search.
- Retrieval: `packages/ai/src/retrieval.ts:276-423` uses vector/text reciprocal-rank fusion plus a department bonus, then limits the result without a semantic reranker.
- MCP capability discovery: `apps/web/lib/mcp/registry.ts:67-98` uses token matching and maintained synonym groups.
- Chat output on `499d8b0`: ordinary evidence-backed, no-attachment requests reconcile every supplied claim into a typed ledger, render canonical summaries server-side, receive different-provider semantic review, permit one ledger repair, and publish nothing after a second failure. Attachments retain a separately reviewed generative path. Steps 9A/9B must preserve the 80-claim/character boundary, exception/handoff/relationship accounting, bounded-gap wording, translation rules, citation identity, and fail-closed review; PR #17 is locally verified but its second live acceptance is not assumed without a current production artifact.
- Claim extraction: `apps/workers/src/trigger/claim-extraction.ts:361-468` sends every non-empty user segment to the extraction model. An unused message-triage route exists at `packages/ai/src/routes/catalog.ts:163-181`.
- Document extraction: `apps/workers/src/trigger/document-ingestion.ts:1057-1361` validates quote occurrence and taxonomy membership, but kind/domain/impact/confidence/sensitivity originate from the same extractor.
- Auto-approval: `apps/workers/src/lib/document-claim-auto-approval.ts:26-76` combines deterministic gates with the extractor's own high confidence; there is no independent semantic evidence-support veto.
- Contradiction watcher: `apps/workers/src/trigger/contradiction-watcher.ts:155-232,450-452` invokes a generative adjudicator for every vector-shortlisted pair.
- Live Recall: `apps/workers/src/trigger/teams-live-recall-utterance.ts:292-318,450-558,756-900` uses a keyword/length pre-gate, then a generative model to decide and draft.
- Responsibility quote repair: `packages/ai/src/prompts/workflow-read.ts:71-80,242-273` already constrains selection to offered exact quote candidates, which is a direct `Choice` shape.
- Deterministic evidence: `packages/oracle-engines/src/extraction/quote-validator.ts:48-299`, taxonomy/promotion validators, synthesis support-ID checks, auth linking, and transaction paths are authoritative and must remain so.

### Existing evaluation and active-work constraints

- `evals/r2-responsibilities.md` and `apps/workers/src/__verify__/r2-responsibility-reader.ts` hold the pinned responsibility evidence and matcher history.
- `evals/bakeoffs/workflow-read.md:28-36` records a GPT-4.1 responsibility baseline of 22–23 calls, 55,620–58,923 input tokens, and $0.278–$0.295.
- The newest open R2 handoff, `HANDOFF.d/2026-08-27T1600Z-al8960ofc-claude-r2-reason-feedback-regressed.md`, says production is restored to a known-good 23/30 map and that continuing its reason-feedback work needs an owner decision. Jev evaluation may read immutable fixtures but must not edit or deploy the R2 reader path.

## 6. Key findings and root cause

1. **The cost opportunity is real but narrow.** Jev's price is roughly two orders of magnitude below typical generative input prices, but it cannot write strings. Savings come from replacing or prefiltering bounded judgments, not from moving Oracle's core generation.
2. **Several current calls are decision-shaped.** Entity selection, quote-candidate selection, contradiction category, extraction-worthiness, relevance, evidence support, and review routing all have finite answer spaces that map directly to Noul/Choice/Score.
3. **Some current safeguards are circular.** The extractor creates a claim and supplies its confidence, kind, domain, impact, and sensitivity. Deterministic validators prove quote presence and allowed IDs, but not always whether the quote semantically supports the summary. An independent cheap judge can add value without becoming authoritative.
4. **Heuristics create recall and maintenance pressure.** MCP capability search and domain inference use token/synonym/substring rules. Jev can add paraphrase understanding while code preserves an exact fallback.
5. **Jev probabilities are not permissions.** Type-safe output guarantees shape, not truth. Choice confidence measures distribution concentration; Noul has no separate confidence. Every use case needs its own threshold, labeled evaluation, and uncertainty behavior.
6. **Large/noisy state reduces accuracy.** Jev 1.13 is documented as literal, weak at arithmetic/date comparison, vulnerable to adversarial state, and less accurate when irrelevant context grows. The Oracle must shortlist/filter deterministically before asking it.
7. **Privacy is the first production gate.** TypeSafe says customer inputs are not used to train model weights, but normal retention is not equivalent to zero data retention; enterprise ZDR and contract/DPA terms must be approved before employee conversations, claims, evidence, or personnel information leave the current provider boundary.
8. **The integration belongs beside—not inside—the generation adapters.** Forcing Jev into `generateText`/`generateObject` would misrepresent its contract, contaminate route/model capability logic, and invite accidental use for generation.

## 7. Approaches considered and rejected

| Rejected approach | Why it looked attractive | Why it is rejected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Add `typesafe` to `OracleProvider` and implement another provider adapter | Reuses existing router and settings UI | Jev cannot satisfy `generateText` or arbitrary schema generation. It needs typed questions and probability results, not a fake generated object. |
| Replace claim extraction, workflow reading, chat, translation, or synthesis | Jev is extraordinarily cheap | These tasks must discover or write text/records. Chaining Choice calls to generate text is explicitly unsupported and would reduce capability. |
| Replace exact evidence/provenance checks with semantic probability | A semantic judge can catch unsupported claims | Exact occurrence, offsets, source IDs, approved status, permissions, and transactions are guarantees. Probability may add a veto/review signal but never replace them. |
| Enable a production skip gate immediately | The prefilter could save the most money quickly | A false negative silently loses knowledge. Shadow evaluation must first prove recall and threshold behavior on Oracle data. |
| Use one global confidence threshold | Simpler configuration | Risk differs by action. Routing an MCP search is recoverable; suppressing extraction or a severe contradiction is not. Each question pack needs independent thresholds. |
| Use `jev-latest` | Automatically receives improvements | Alias movement can invalidate tuned thresholds without a code change. Pin `jev-1.13.0`, log the resolved model, and upgrade through an explicit eval. |
| Send entire transcripts/documents as state | Maximizes available context | It increases privacy exposure, cost, and context rot. Deterministically shortlist the smallest sufficient text and metadata. |
| Ask Jev to count, calculate dates, enforce limits, or infer API capability | It understands text and returns scores | Jev 1.13 is weak at numeric/date precision; code and live API probes are authoritative and cheaper. |
| Start with live Teams or HR/sensitivity text | High call volume demonstrates savings | It is the most privacy-sensitive and operationally risky state. Start with synthetic/low-sensitivity capability routing, then graduate deliberately. |
| Modify the active R2 responsibility prompt while evaluating Jev | The pinned fixture is already available | The R2 workstream is separately governed and recently regressed. Jev evaluation must be additive and offline until that workstream is settled. |
| Treat TypeSafe marketing benchmarks as Oracle proof | Vendor reports strong speed/cost results | Oracle must measure accuracy, abstention, p50/p95 latency, total cost, and cost per correct decision on its own labeled cases. |

## 8. Design decisions already made

### Locked decisions — do not relitigate during implementation

1. **Separate decision boundary.** Add an `OracleDecisionClient` (name may vary only to match existing naming conventions) under `packages/ai/src/decisions/`. Do not extend the generation-provider adapter map.
2. **Official SDK and pinned model.** Use `@typesafe-ai/sdk`, pin `jev-1.13.0`, and make the fetch/client injectable for deterministic tests. Do not use community CLIs or an alias in production.
3. **Code owns policy.** Jev returns typed judgments and probabilities. Deterministic code decides whether to keep the current path, request review, or invoke a generative model.
4. **Shadow first.** Every use case initially records what Jev would have decided while preserving current behavior. Activation requires a written acceptance artifact and its own PR/session.
5. **Fail open to present behavior.** Timeout, overload, 429, malformed response, unavailable key, low confidence, or unsupported language must preserve the current code path. “Fail open” never means bypassing safety; it means do what Oracle already does today.
6. **No weakening of guarantees.** Jev may add a hold/veto/review signal. It may not bypass evidence, permissions, approvals, direct-mention rules, cooldowns, caps, or database guards.
7. **Per-use-case contracts.** Each question pack has a stable ID, version, exact instructions/criteria, state builder, threshold policy, feature flag, and test/eval artifact.
8. **Minimal state.** Send only the shortlisted text and metadata needed for that decision. Do not send binaries, full documents, raw attachments, or unrelated history.
9. **Complete observability.** Log provider `typesafe`, requested and resolved model, question-pack ID/version, input/output tokens, computed cost, latency, probabilities, confidence where applicable, outcome, threshold path, error class, and whether the result affected behavior.
10. **Dedicated decision-control schema.** Add the policy/run/subject/adjudication/control/canary tables specified below in the foundation migration; do not overload generation-oriented `model_runs`. No business claim, contradiction, message, or permission rule moves into these tables.
11. **No autonomous governance.** Claim approval, entity merge, meeting ingest/dismiss, reviewer assignment, and translation acceptance remain human/deterministic decisions.
12. **One production outcome per session.** MCP, entity selection, extraction prefilter, contradiction gate, live Recall gate, reranking, and grounding are separate implementation/live-proof sessions.
13. **Separated tuning and acceptance evidence.** Thresholds are chosen only on a versioned tuning set. Activation is judged on a sealed held-out set that was not used to write prompts, choose thresholds, or select examples. Each pilot records its minimum sample and class counts before the held-out run; material gates double-label at least 20% of cases, report agreement, and adjudicate disagreements before scoring.
14. **Audited effects only.** A Jev result may affect behavior only after its final dedicated decision row, typed subject links, usage, bounded answers, and policy outcome commit successfully. Any audit-persistence failure discards the Jev result and preserves the current path.
15. **Hard-negative canaries never stop.** Any active Jev decision that suppresses extraction, contradiction adjudication, or live-meeting analysis must atomically reserve the stated first-N ISO-week minimum (or all negatives when traffic is lower) and every tenth later negative into the unchanged current model path. Every disagreement plus at least 10 atomically reserved agreements per use case per ISO week (or all when fewer exist) enters human review, so shared model blind spots remain measurable. A human-confirmed safety miss or rolling error threshold automatically changes only that use case from `active` to `shadow` and alerts admins. Frozen acceptance is necessary but never substitutes for production drift detection.

### Open owner/business decisions

1. **Vendor/data approval:** approve a TypeSafe commercial account and the contract/DPA/security posture needed for Oracle data. Recommendation: require enterprise zero-data-retention terms before sending employee messages, company claims/evidence, licensed documents, or personnel data. This blocks Oracle-data pilots, not the synthetic client/harness work.
2. **Initial spend control:** approve an initial capped evaluation balance. Recommendation: $5 is ample for the planned shadow evaluations at the documented token price; increase only after measured value.

### Open implementation judgments with fixed criteria

- The exact class/file names may follow local conventions, but the separate decision boundary is locked.
- Direct SDK calls versus a tiny local wrapper are an implementation detail; the wrapper must expose injectable transport, redacted errors, and normalized Oracle usage.
- A use case may be dropped if its measured savings or quality gain does not justify added latency/privacy/maintenance. Record the negative result; do not lower the gate to force adoption.
- Unless a step states a stricter requirement, the held-out set has at least 100 cases and 25 cases in every safety-critical positive class. MCP and entity selection each use at least 100; extraction uses at least 200 with 50 high-value positives; claim support uses at least 150 with 50 unsupported/contradicted/qualified cases; contradiction uses at least 150 with 50 material contradictions. The immutable 30-case R2 fixture is held-out only; build a separate tuning set.

## 9. The plan — ordered, executable phases

### Phase A — Safe foundation

#### Step M0 prerequisite sequence — M0P/M0P-C, M0Q/M0Q-C, M0T/M0T-C, M0A, M0B–M0C, M0D1–M0D3, M0W, M0R/M0R-C, M0G/M0G-C, M0I1/I1-C through M0I5/I5-C, M0U/M0U-C, M0S/M0S-C, M0D4–M0D5, M0E/M0E-C, M0V0/V0-C, M0V1/V1-C, M0V2/V2-C, M0X0/X0-C, M0X1/X1-C, M0X2/X2-C, M0F/M0F-C

M0 is a dependency-ordered sequence, not one implementation session. **M0P** adds/publishes central `migration-release`, and Oracle **M0P-C** records that external outcome. Separate **M0Q** adds/publishes sealed-brief and neutral-root review support, and Oracle **M0Q-C** records it. **M0T/T-C** then proves and records, without reading any secret or changing any account/resource, that the current 1Password account/vendor contract can enforce the exact-field broker or isolated-vault route used later; absence stops the roadmap before Oracle migration infrastructure. Only then may **M0A** touch Oracle: the exact M0Q toolkit is installed locally, `ai-task-gates start --class migration-release` succeeds, and M0A pins that engine in CI, moves every CI/local/snapshot fixture to PostgreSQL 17, adds the repository-local six-class union, and delivers the later read-only production-version verifier. M0A never edits under reviewer-safety alone: its first and final checks use `migration-release`, and a changed-file manifest proves every native class is represented. **M0B/C/D1/D2/D3** build and prove only local/fake migration machinery and refuse production credentials or targets. **M0W** then merges four identity-only workflows; **M0R/R-C** applies and records repository governance plus the credential-free infrastructure-approval issuer. **M0G/G-C** separately creates and records the final WIF pool/provider, five exact-workflow identities/bindings, empty named secret resources, and two KMS keys from a clean Google-hosted administrator session; it installs the transfer workflow in identity-only form and introduces no credential value or production connection. **M0I1/I1-C** journals and delivers the six replicas; **M0I2/I2-C**, **M0I3/I3-C**, **M0I4/I4-C**, and **M0I5/I5-C** then separately rotate IAM trust to and prove the exact merged migration, reviewer, deployment, and settings workflow revisions. Each pair is its own reviewed live outcome and documentation closeout; no pair may borrow another's approval or artifact. Only after I5-C may **M0U/U-C** use the final migration read identity/resources to prove and record PostgreSQL 17+; a lower or ambiguous version records `blocked` and requires a separate quiesce/recovery implementation plan before any provider upgrade. **M0S/S-C** then merges the Vercel-settings workflow's tightly bounded mutation actions and uses its final identity to install/prove a provider-native constant automatic-build skip before any application-boundary source is merged. Only then may **D4** add restore/isolation/maintenance/app writer boundaries and **D5** compose the disabled system. **M0E/E-C** transitions review/rehearsal/deployment/canary routes while disabled and records rehearsal plus full timing. **M0V0/V0-C** installs/proves only the manifest-aware Vercel setting; **M0V1/V1-C** deploys/proves only Vercel/web; **M0V2/V2-C** then deploys/proves only Trigger/worker. **M0X0/X0-C** merges and records only the enable manifest and ignored-build proof; **M0X1/X1-C** deploys/re-proves only Vercel/web; **M0X2/X2-C** deploys/re-proves only Trigger/worker. **M0F/F-C** separately proves no drift, performs the one approved production database migration, and records closeout. Every milestone gets its own current-upstream worktree, exact-head review, PR/merge where applicable, STATUS transition, and no borrowed unmerged files; only genuinely unfinished state gets a successor handoff, subject to the capacity rule above.

**M0T read-only feasibility contract:** a named security administrator opens the current 1Password account on a separately reviewed clean hosted environment and inspects only product/tenant capability metadata—never any vault item or value. The evidence must identify an official, currently enabled provider mechanism that issues an expiring capability restricted to one named item/field and no vault listing, or an isolated-vault broker whose token can see only one synthetic one-field item and whose API returns only that field; it must also document issuance authority, maximum lifetime, revocation, audit event, automation/API support, and denial of sibling items. Public documentation alone is insufficient unless the current account exposes the feature; an account screenshot or assertion is insufficient without provider API/support attestation and schema/version. The content-free artifact contains only provider contract URLs/digests, feature/schema IDs, tenant capability booleans, scope/expiry/revocation/audit matrices, synthetic identifiers, timestamps, and no secret/item value. A neutral-root M0Q reviewer must APPROVE the exact artifact and chosen route. M0T-C records its digest/verdict and the selected route in STATUS; missing exact enforcement, unavailable automation, or a requirement for a vault-wide/GitHub-held credential records `blocked` and stops before M0A, M0U, or any Oracle migration/infrastructure work.

**Pre-D executable gate matrix**

| Milestone | Exact direct command | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0P | `bash tests/test-ai-task-gates.sh` | the central `migration-release` class, consumer reference, additive local requirements, unknown-class refusal, and stronger-production ordering all pass in `popcre/ai-devops` |
| M0P-C | `ai-codex-review plan-review --base <m0p-closeout-parent> --assert-head <m0p-closeout-head>` | Oracle documentation-only STATUS evidence matches the merged ai-devops SHA, published archive digest/version, installation proof, and green M0P gate; no toolkit or Oracle behavior changes |
| M0Q | `bash tests/test-ai-codex-review.sh --case sealed-brief && bash tests/test-ai-codex-review.sh --case untrusted-snapshot-root` | brief binding and neutral-root isolation both pass; either skipped/mismatched/prompt-injected path fails in `popcre/ai-devops` |
| M0Q-C | `ai-codex-review plan-review --base <m0q-closeout-parent> --assert-head <m0q-closeout-head>` | Oracle documentation-only STATUS evidence matches the merged ai-devops SHA, published archive digest/version, installation proof, and both green M0Q gates; no toolkit or Oracle behavior changes |
| M0T | `ai-codex-review final-check --base <current-main-parent> --assert-head <current-main-sha> --sealed-brief <m0t-feasibility-brief.json> --untrusted-snapshot-root` | neutral reviewer APPROVE binds the content-free provider/account capability artifact, exact-field or isolated-vault route, expiry/revocation/audit/automation contract, and sibling-denial evidence; no secret/item value or mutation occurred |
| M0T-C | `ai-codex-review plan-review --base <m0t-closeout-parent> --assert-head <m0t-closeout-head>` | documentation-only STATUS/decision exactly matches the sealed M0T artifact; any unavailable/broad-token route records blocked and M0A remains closed |
| M0A | `bash scripts/test-task-gates.sh && pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-postgres-runtime.ts` | the installed central release, pinned workflow SHA, six-class Oracle union, every planned-path native class, weakening-refusal fixtures, PostgreSQL 17 CI service, and effective `transaction_timeout` all pass |
| M0B | `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-pure.ts` | ordering, tokenizer, inventory, scanner, and side-effect-free import fixtures pass with no manifest/workflow/database access |

Each command is owned and introduced by its named milestone except that M0A deliberately introduces the verifier M0U executes. M0P and M0Q are separate `popcre/ai-devops` outcomes, each followed by a distinct Oracle documentation-only closeout; M0T/T-C is the intervening read-only transfer-feasibility outcome; M0A is the first Oracle implementation milestone; M0U/U-C is a separate read-only PostgreSQL prerequisite outcome; M0B–M0C run only after their successful predecessor is merged and installed.

**M0D ownership boundaries**

| Milestone | Owns | Direct green gate | Must not own |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M0D1 | raw baseline/postconditions, guarded Drizzle/raw execution behind direct test-only entrypoints, target guard, candidate/deployed drift split, expand-contract and old-app compatibility | `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-migration-engine.ts` | proof MAC/chain, package/legacy alias transition, authorization, snapshot/export, hosted runner, production credentials |
| M0D2 | immutable proof bootstrap v2, before/after manifests, database-side aggregate roots, injected fake-MAC adapter, permanent append-only state-version chain/key metadata, migration/seed alias transition after proof exists | `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-proof-chain.ts` | approval artifacts, leases, production KMS/IAM, snapshot/export, workflow wiring |
| M0D3 | immutable authorization bootstrap v3, approval schema/trust data structures, preparation/verifier/renewal helpers, authorization/action state, revocation cutoffs, leases, concurrency/crash recovery using fake authorities | `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-authorization.ts` | real reviewer workflow, GCP/KMS, production connection, snapshot/export, runner isolation |
| M0D4 | version preflight, digest-pinned local runtime, export/restore/dependency policies, maintenance boundary, isolation/egress/cleanup libraries, app writer boundaries, and synthetic fault fixtures | `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-rehearsal-engine.ts` | GitHub workflow, cloud identity, production read/write, activation flag; cannot start before M0S-C proves automatic builds suppressed |
| M0D5 | composition manifest and one fresh plus one filtered-snapshot synthetic end-to-end run across D1-D4 and the already-merged M0W workflow interfaces; final operator/docs scanner; frozen disabled contracts consumed by M0E+ | `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-disabled-integration.ts` | new subsystem design, production authority, workflow/cloud changes, live database action |

Each row is an independently accepted outcome: one branch, one exact-head review, one PR, one merged commit, and one STATUS update. A completed bounded row creates no handoff because no unfinished state remains; a handoff is permitted only under the capacity/predecessor rule above. A file or test that spans rows must be split at an injected interface; D2 receives D1 execution events, D3 receives D2 proof services, D4 receives D3 authorization services, and D5 only composes public interfaces. No D milestone may borrow an unmerged later file or defer its direct green command.

M0D3 has exactly one direct acceptance command: `pnpm exec tsx packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-authorization.ts`. It imports and executes the complete `production-authorization.ts` component suite plus its own milestone-boundary checks, reports both sub-results, and fails if either is skipped. `production-authorization.ts` remains runnable for diagnosis but is never an alternative milestone gate; any later legacy wording that calls it the direct green command is superseded by this aggregate contract.

The detailed bullets below use “M0D” only as the umbrella design name retained from the original plan. The ownership matrix is normative: migration execution/compatibility belongs to D1; manifests/proofs/checkpoints to D2; authorization/revocation/recovery to D3; snapshot/restore/isolation/maintenance to D4; and integration-only composition to D5. An implementation PR that crosses those boundaries fails its milestone gate even if the umbrella bullet describes the combined final behavior.

| M0I4 / I4-C | `pnpm verify:m0i4:deployment-boundary` / `pnpm verify:m0i4:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | exact-SHA IAM transition and two exact-project deploy-token fingerprint/denial proofs only; separate closeout |
| M0I5 / I5-C | `pnpm verify:m0i5:settings-boundary` / `pnpm verify:m0i5:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | exact-SHA IAM transition and one exact-project settings-token fingerprint/denial proof only; separate closeout |
| M0U | `pnpm exec tsx scripts/verify-postgres-17-prerequisite.ts --production` | the M0I2/I2-C-proven migration identity/resources produce authenticated read-only project/version/health proof showing PostgreSQL 17+; lower/unknown exits nonzero and performs no upgrade or traffic change |
| M0U-C | `pnpm exec tsx scripts/verify-postgres-17-prerequisite.ts --artifact <artifact>` | immutable read-only evidence is bound to project `eqccjfbyrywsqkxxpjvg`, current version/health, and current main; only a 17+ artifact unlocks M0S, while a lower-version closeout stays blocked |
| M0S | `pnpm verify:m0s:vercel-build-suppression -- --mode live-proof --artifact <path>` | exact project setting is the provider-native constant skip; the inert commit's deployment reaches documented canceled/skipped state with zero project-build execution and unchanged production alias; settings identity cannot deploy/read secrets/change any other setting |
| M0S-C | `pnpm verify:m0s:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | immutable setting/audit/canceled-deployment/unchanged-production artifact and issue record agree; no repository executable was needed to establish suppression |
| M0E | `pnpm verify:m0e:ci` | approval, deployment, signed-canary, rehearsal, full-root/end-to-end timing, and disabled activation routes pass; write guard remains false |
| M0E-C | `pnpm verify:m0e:live-proof -- --artifact <path>` | current rehearsal/timing artifact, approval, runner identity, KMS/public attestations, cleanup, and issue record agree |
| M0V0 / V0-C | `pnpm verify:m0v0:build-setting` / `pnpm verify:m0v0:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | ignore script/manifest plus one exact Vercel-setting transition only, then separate audit/readback/ignored-probe closeout |
| M0V1 / V1-C | `pnpm verify:m0v1:web-writer-fence` / `pnpm verify:m0v1:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | one Vercel deployment and web canary only, then separate proof closeout |
| M0V2 / V2-C | `pnpm verify:m0v2:worker-writer-fence` / `pnpm verify:m0v2:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | one Trigger deployment and worker canary only, then separate proof closeout |
| M0X0 / X0-C | `pnpm verify:m0x0` / `pnpm verify:m0x0:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | only enable/evidence fields changed; candidate and merged tree pass secretlessly and the closeout proves the merge was ignored |
| M0X1 / X1-C | `pnpm verify:m0x1:web-live` / `pnpm verify:m0x1:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | one exact-SHA Vercel deployment and enabled web canary only, then separate proof closeout |
| M0X2 / X2-C | `pnpm verify:m0x2:worker-live` / `pnpm verify:m0x2:closeout -- --artifact <path>` | one exact-SHA Trigger deployment and enabled worker canary only, then separate proof closeout |
| M0F | `pnpm verify:m0f:database-proof -- --deployment-artifact <path>` | current main and both live deployment/bundle IDs still match X2-C, then the separately approved bootstrap/idempotency database proof passes |
| M0F-C | `pnpm verify:m0f:live-proof -- --artifact <path>` | production proof chain, deployed drift result, authorization consumption, no-seed/idempotency result, and issue record agree |

Each command is a package alias committed by its named implementation milestone and runs in CI with fakes where live state is forbidden. A missing alias, skipped sub-suite, wrong milestone state, or artifact not bound to the expected merge SHA fails. The `-C` commands are read-only and may update no external state.

**Files/functions**

- **M0R credential-free infrastructure approval issuer:** M0R adds protected `.github/workflows/m0-infrastructure-approval.yml` and `pnpm verify:m0r:infrastructure-approval`. It does not call a model and needs no model token. A content-free approval-request PR contains one canonical manifest binding exact old/new merged workflow SHAs, workflow path/ref, one bounded IAM-condition replacement, administrator identity, expiry, rollback, and operation ID. The independent CODEOWNER named and verified by M0R—not the author, pusher, administrator, or Albert—reviews the exact last push and must submit the literal structured verdict `APPROVE <manifest-sha256>`; any prose, earlier-push review, changed digest, self-review, dismissal, or other verdict fails. A credential-free fresh attestation job uses only `contents: read`, `pull-requests: read`, `id-token: write`, and `attestations: write` to query GitHub's authenticated review/commit state, verify reviewer identity/independence, last-push SHA, manifest schema/digest, expiry and single-use operation ID, and emit the GitHub artifact attestation consumed by M0G and every later workflow/IAM transition. It receives no GCP, database, model, deployment, settings, KMS, Secret Manager, or 1Password authority. This issuer is live and tested at M0R-C; `.github/workflows/production-approval.yml` remains probe-only until M0E. The issuer itself is CODEOWNED, in the closure manifest, and cannot attest a PR that changes itself, its verifier, CODEOWNERS, or the manifest schema; such a change must merge first under the ordinary exact-head/code-owner gates, after which a new request PR may be reviewed.

- M0A updates the router-required `docs/verification/phase-3-task-gates-pilot.md` in the same PR as Oracle's task-gate policy/workflow pin. It records the new `migration-release` class, its rank and complete gate union, the exact merged M0P enforcement/toolkit SHA and archive digest, Oracle's pinned local policy/workflow SHA, rollout/rollback scope, and the commands/evidence proving unknown-class refusal and no weakening of production. `scripts/test-task-gates.sh` fails if that document's class/SHA/digest block differs from the installed engine, `.ai-devops/task-gates.json`, or workflow pin; documentation drift therefore blocks M0A before any Oracle edit or stronger action.
- Add side-effect-free `packages/db/src/raw-migration-order.ts` exporting the pure `orderRawMigrationNames(names)` parser/sorter; it imports no env, database client, seed, or runner and cannot call `main()`. It parses the leading numeric ordinal and sorts numerically, then by full filename only as a deterministic tie-breaker; it rejects any `.sql` file that does not match `^\d{2,}_[a-z0-9_]+\.sql$`. The repository already has one historical duplicate ordinal, so a checked exception permits exactly `56_employees_departments_array.sql` then `56_model_capabilities_more_providers.sql`; any other duplicate ordinal or changed/missing member aborts. Keep `01_extensions.sql` explicitly first before Drizzle and keep `99_vector_indexes.sql` explicitly opt-in after all normal raw migrations. Normal raw order at the audited baseline is therefore `10…98,100,101,102`, never lexical `100,101,102,11…`; future `103+` files run after every older normal prerequisite.
- M0P runs first in a new `popcre/ai-devops` worktree under reviewer-safety. It adds protected central class `migration-release` to `config/task-gates.json` at rank 75 (above infrastructure 70, below production 80), updates `bin/ai-task-gates`'s version and task-gate contract docs, and adds `tests/test-ai-task-gates.sh` fixtures proving consumers may reference the class, unknown classes still fail, local gates can only add requirements, and production remains stronger. It opens, reviews, merges, and installs that toolkit through the normal `codex-sync-dotfiles` route; its merge SHA and archive SHA-256 become M0P-C evidence. M0P changes no Oracle file and is a complete separate outcome. A fresh Oracle documentation-only M0P-C session verifies the exact ai-devops merge, release/archive digest, installed version, and test artifact, updates only this STATUS/evidence, receives exact-head plan review, and merges before M0Q starts.
- M0Q runs second in a new `popcre/ai-devops` worktree under reviewer-safety after M0P-C. It adds supported `ai-codex-review ... --sealed-brief <path> --untrusted-snapshot-root` handling. Sealed-brief mode canonicalizes/hashes caller JSON, inserts the full authorization instruction into the provider's top-level prompt, seals the brief/digest, and requires atomic explicit field binding. Untrusted-snapshot mode creates a fresh neutral working directory whose ancestor chain contains only toolkit-owned pinned instructions, mounts/copies the sealed repository snapshot read-only below a non-working-directory data path, passes source paths as untrusted evidence, and prevents repository `AGENTS.md`/overrides/config from becoming Codex startup instructions; complete source remains review-visible. The lifecycle report records neutral cwd, ancestor-instruction digests, snapshot mount/path/read-only proof, and absence of repository instruction loading. `bash tests/test-ai-codex-review.sh --case sealed-brief` rejects missing/mutated/partially bound briefs and generic APPROVE; `bash tests/test-ai-codex-review.sh --case untrusted-snapshot-root` plants malicious root/nested AGENTS/override/config instructions, symlink/path escapes, and source prompt injection, and proves they are treated as findings/data, never authority, while snapshot completeness and unchanged hashes remain intact. Either option is production-required and fail-closed. M0Q merges/publishes/installs through `codex-sync-dotfiles`; its exact commit/archive digest becomes M0Q-C evidence. M0Q changes no Oracle file and is separate from M0P. A fresh Oracle documentation-only M0Q-C session verifies that external evidence, updates only this STATUS/evidence, receives exact-head plan review, and merges before M0T or M0A.
- M0U is a separate read-only production prerequisite after M0I5-C and before M0S. It executes M0A's exact reviewed verifier from a fresh hosted runner through the M0I2-proven migration workflow identity, reads only `oracle-production-database-url` and `oracle-supabase-management-token`, and has no bootstrap principal or provider-write authority. The probe binds project `eqccjfbyrywsqkxxpjvg`, endpoint/region/status, `server_version_num`, installed extension versions (`vector`, `pgcrypto`, and every extension discovered in the production manifest), pooler mode, database, and current role. If `server_version_num>=170000`, M0U performs no mutation and emits a signed no-op artifact. If the version is lower, unknown, or health checks fail, M0U performs no provider action, records the exact blocker, exits nonzero, and requires a new implementation-plan-writer plan with an independently shipped Vercel maintenance gate, Trigger schedule/task drain, retry/queue fence, connection drain, provider rollback/restore path, concurrent-write tests, and separately reviewed upgrade authorization before any upgrade attempt. M0U-C may record either the successful 17+ proof or the blocker, but only a successful artifact unlocks M0S; this plan never restores traffic because it never quiesces or changes it.
- M0A first installs the exact merged M0Q toolkit outside Oracle, verifies its archive digest/version, then—in a fresh Oracle worktree before changing any file—runs `ai-task-gates start --class migration-release` and its initial check. Only under that already active composite gate does it update `.github/workflows/task-gates.yml` to M0Q and extend `.ai-devops/task-gates.json` with the six-class union and native path coverage. `scripts/test-task-gates.sh` proves the superset, every planned/negative-neighbor classification, workflow compliance, rulebook treatment, canonical runner, migration/release/drift/review/authorization/infrastructure gates, and weakening refusal. The final check must report the same M0Q engine and `migration-release`; a pre-edit declaration failure blocks without fallback to reviewer-safety or deployment.
- M0A also changes `.github/workflows/pr-check.yml` from its current PostgreSQL 16 service to a digest-pinned PostgreSQL 17 image and pins every local, fresh, snapshot, and compatibility runtime fixture to the same supported major. Add `config/m0-postgres-runtime.json`, `packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-postgres-runtime.ts`, and `scripts/verify-postgres-17-prerequisite.ts`; the runtime manifest accepts only `server_version_num>=170000` and rejects PostgreSQL 16, missing/unknown version, an absent `transaction_timeout`, or a session in which `SET LOCAL transaction_timeout` is ineffective. CI executes `SHOW server_version_num`, `SHOW transaction_timeout`, and an actual over-limit rollback fixture. The production verifier is unconditionally read-only and has no provider-upgrade action or credential; every production-capable M0S+ path additionally requires the merged successful M0U-C evidence digest. M0B/C/D1/D2/D3/W/R/G/I use only local/fake/provider-control targets as assigned and cannot connect to production. No PostgreSQL 16 lane is presented as supported or sufficient for M0.
- Add `packages/db/src/raw-migration-ledger.ts` and an admin-owned `oracle_raw_migrations` table containing ordinal, filename, SHA-256 file checksum, state (`deferred|applying|applied`), deferral reason/eligibility predicate, applied timestamp, runner version, and baseline-vs-executed provenance. Every discovered file has exactly one row. Normal files may never enter `deferred`; optional `99_vector_indexes.sql` starts `deferred` when its exact prerequisite/flag predicate is false and remains checksum-pinned and visible to completeness/drift checks. Only explicit guarded `--include-optional 99_vector_indexes.sql` may atomically verify its original checksum, target/prerequisites, transition `deferred -> applying -> applied`, execute it, and store a proof; removal, silent eligibility change, or automatic activation fails. Idempotency preserves a valid deferred row byte-for-byte. A static SQL tokenizer classifies every current file. Files without transaction control execute SQL plus `applied` in one transaction; the 22 legacy whole-file wrappers use `applying` recovery and complete postconditions. Crash fixtures cover every boundary and deferred activation. Every new `103+` file is forbidden from transaction control.
- The six control tables and mandatory writer fence arrive through four immutable, dependency-ordered bootstrap versions rather than one premature schema: M0C owns `packages/db/migrations/bootstrap/001_ledger_release_v1.sql` for only `oracle_raw_migrations` and `oracle_schema_releases`; M0D2 owns `002_proof_v1.sql` for only `oracle_migration_proofs` and `oracle_migration_proof_keys` after D1 execution events exist; M0D3 owns `003_authorization_v1.sql` for only `oracle_migration_authorizations` and `oracle_migration_authorization_actions` after D2 proof services exist; M0D4 owns `004_writer_fence_v1.sql`, containing only the exact owner functions plus four triggers on every manifest-enumerated writable application table: one `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE FOR EACH STATEMENT` fence trigger and three event-specific `AFTER ... FOR EACH STATEMENT` delta triggers whose `REFERENCING` clauses expose `NEW TABLE`, `OLD TABLE`, or both. Each file requires and hash-pins its exact predecessor, adds only its milestone-owned objects, has its own typed `control-schema-contract` revision and fresh/upgrade/downgrade/partial-effect tests, and is independently green at that merged head. The eventual M0F bootstrap action applies 001→002→003→004 inside its one authorized transaction and binds all four checksums; no earlier milestone references a later table/type. Bootstrap 004 is the sole narrow application-table DDL exception: it changes no business column, row, constraint, role, grant, or ownership and installs only the reviewed fail-closed fence triggers. These files are a formal **migration-control infrastructure exception** to `schema.ts`, not a second application-schema source. M0A updates the rule/task/architecture/development/deployment/migration README/decision docs with this versioned exception but no executable path; all other application schema remains owned by `schema.ts`/normal migrations.
- M0D4 also creates CODEOWNED `config/m0-writable-application-tables.json`, initially enumerating every current writable base/partitioned application table with its stable key and exact four-trigger definitions. The guarded schema scanner compares `pg_class` plus the Drizzle after-schema with that manifest and rejects every writable base/partitioned table that is absent, marked exempt, or lacks any exact fence/delta trigger. Every later generated/raw schema release that creates a writable table must update this manifest and install all four triggers in the **same guarded candidate transaction before any backfill or seed may touch it**; the after-manifest and clean/snapshot tests prove exact trigger function, timing, events, transition-table topology, owner, enabled state, and table coverage before commit. This explicitly includes all thirteen Step 1A decision-control tables, its four provenance/ingress tables, Step 8A-1's contradiction archive/source/reservation tables, and Step 8B-1's three Recall delivery tables plus any later table discovered by the scanner. Each owning schema milestone owns those trigger statements and tests; none may inherit M0's old-table snapshot or defer fencing to a later PR. Append-only/control tables may have narrower runtime grants, but are not trigger-exempt. Views and genuinely read-only relations remain separately classified and machine-proven non-writable. Fixtures add an unmanifested table, omit/disable/retarget each trigger, create a partition after the parent, and attempt same-transaction seed/backfill before trigger installation; all roll back the release.
- Later runs verify file checksum/state and skip `applied`; a changed checksum, missing historical file, order regression, or ledger/file disagreement fails before later SQL. Existing production is bootstrapped without replaying old SQL: the baseline manifest pins every current filename/checksum, transaction class, and named read-only postcondition verifier; only when every postcondition passes may one transaction create/populate exact `applied/baseline` rows. `01` and opt-in `99` have explicit ledger/postcondition treatment.
- `config/raw-migration-baseline.json` contains one reviewed entry for **every** current raw migration: filename/checksum, transaction class, complete-effect mode, verifier ID, canonical SQL/result encoding, and expected digest. Catalog migrations require exact object/definition/owner/ACL predicates; seed/data migrations require a closed affected-set query, exact desired-value predicate, and zero-row residual query proving no in-scope row is missing, stale, or partially transformed. Counts or “object exists” alone are forbidden. If a historical migration has no provable total predicate, bootstrap stops and requires a separately reviewed reconciliation migration—never guesses applied and never replays it. `packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-baseline-postconditions.ts` mutates/deletes one required effect for every manifest entry and proves bootstrap rejects each partial state; it also proves the untouched production-shaped fixture admits each baseline row exactly once.
- Drizzle keeps its journal format, but production never calls the SDK `migrate()` path that can commit separately. M0D adds `packages/db/src/apply-drizzle-guarded.ts`: it parses the exact generated journal/files, **accepts** the repository's normal journal `"breakpoints": true` format, verifies checksums and expected before/after manifests, and executes every pending generated statement plus its exact Drizzle journal insert through the caller's existing transaction/client. `breakpoints` is treated only as format metadata, never permission for a separate commit. A side-effect-free SQL lexer tracks normal text, single/double/E strings, dollar-quoted bodies, line comments, and nested block comments; only an exact `--> statement-breakpoint` marker encountered in normal lexical state splits a statement. Markers inside quoted/comment/dollar content remain data. `breakpoints:true` requires that canonical generated-marker grammar; `breakpoints:false` safely accepts one unsplit statement only. Empty segments, malformed/out-of-state markers, unsupported journal versions/fields, and transaction-control tokens in any parsed statement fail before connection. Compatibility fixtures consist only of every unmodified current migration plus a freshly generated Step 1A migration; for those generator-produced files, the lexer's ordered statements must match the pinned Drizzle `readMigrationFiles` result byte-for-byte and both migrators must produce identical journal/catalog state. Hostile-input fixtures are a separate rejection suite: literal/comment/dollar-quoted marker text, malformed/truncated markers, empty segments, and unsupported `breakpoints`/journal shapes must be rejected before connection whenever the guarded lexer and Drizzle's unconditional splitter disagree; the guarded runner intentionally supersedes Drizzle by refusing such a file, never by executing a different interpretation. LF/CRLF and ordinary generated dollar-quoted functions remain in the compatibility suite, while package-version drift blocks until both suites are reviewed again. Raw-file SHA-256, journal-entry digest, ordered per-statement digests, and parser/package version enter the release closure. For production releases, one locked `SERIALIZABLE` `apply_verify` transaction first requires PostgreSQL support for `transaction_timeout`, sets `SET LOCAL transaction_timeout='4min'` plus shorter per-statement/lock/idle-in-transaction limits, then executes guarded generated SQL and eligible future raw SQL, captures the runner-visible post-state plus every locked relation, validates expected catalog/data/role/grant deltas and candidate manifest within that boundary, inserts Drizzle/raw journals plus release/proof/action state, reruns live revocation, and only then commits; any exception, unexpected/missing in-boundary effect, cancellation, disconnect, or revocation observed before the defined cutoff rolls back all candidate DDL/DML and journal/proof state together. Existing production never replays legacy self-managed raw wrappers; every new `103+` raw file is transaction-safe by rule. Fresh/rehearsal paths compare the guarded executor's final journal/catalog with the standard Drizzle migrator on an isolated database so format drift blocks before production. `oracle_schema_releases` records `planned -> applying -> applied_pending_external_check` in the authorization transaction; recovery proves either that whole release/proof/journal commit exists or none does—there is no authorized `drizzle_applied` partial state. The separate read-only post-commit verification advances it to `verified` or `contaminated`. Fault tests inject before/after every parsed generated statement—including every current/new breakpoint shape—Drizzle journal insert, raw statement, delta check, proof/action insert, revocation check, commit, and post-commit check, including ambiguous disconnect and external privileged races, and require full rollback before commit or one fenced pending/verified/contaminated release with no false rollback claim.
- Add `packages/db/src/__verify__/m0-expand-contract.ts`, `scripts/verify-old-app-schema-compat.mjs`, and M0-owned `config/m0-old-app-db-smokes.json`. The static gate compares each generated/raw catalog delta with the current-main schema and rejects drops/renames, type narrowing, removed/default-changed columns, new `NOT NULL` without an additive default/backfill sequence, enum-value removal/reorder, destructive constraint replacement, and ownership/privilege removal; new constraints over populated objects must stage `NOT VALID` then validate in a later reviewed outcome. M0 builds its smoke manifest from an exhaustive current-main scan of all web/worker/package DB clients and top-level operational scripts, with stable operation IDs and source hashes; a completeness scanner rejects an unlisted query path. The dynamic gate creates a disposable checkout/build of exact `origin/main`, migrates a fresh database to the candidate schema, and runs every operation in this M0 manifest plus old-version web/worker schema smoke suites against it. Candidate code is not used for the probe. Step 1A later creates the broader role/grant-oriented `config/runtime-db-access.json` and imports every still-current M0 operation ID rather than being an M0 prerequisite. M0 and every later schema-first PR must pass both compatibility commands; intentional contract breaks require a separate expand/migrate/contract plan, never an exception flag.
- Add `config/database-targets.json`, side-effect-free `packages/db/src/database-target-guard.ts`, and `packages/db/src/supabase-project-target.ts`. The code-owned `production` profile accepts **only** the committed Supabase session-pooler host form for project `eqccjfbyrywsqkxxpjvg`, requires its project-ref-bearing pooler username, database name, TLS, and allowed migration identities, and explicitly rejects direct-host, transaction-pooler, custom, retired-project `vokucjpanhvqunimlvsp`, and unrelated-project forms. Snapshot/fresh profiles require a newly created `oracle_m0_*` database and deny both real project refs. Every bootstrap/apply path receives an unforgeable `VerifiedDatabaseTarget` from this guard on the **same connection immediately before its transaction**; it parses the protected URL without logging it and verifies the exact pooler hostname/username project ref, TLS, `current_database`, `session_user/current_user`, server version, and expected migration-role attributes. For production, `supabase-project-target.ts` uses a protected read-only management token to fetch that exact project's current endpoint/region/status and compare them before every write. Table tests cover the one accepted session-pooler form and reject direct-host usernames even when the host/project are otherwise valid. No URL, CLI flag, environment assertion, or local default can override the committed production ref; any mismatch exits before ledger or application-table writes.
- M0 does **not** rotate or retire the current production credential and does not claim to make historical binaries, dashboard SQL, or Supabase MCP technically powerless; doing that before the separately staged runtime-role cutovers in Steps 1A–1D could outage web/workers. It makes the guarded runner the documented/repository-enforced path and detects catalog/control drift at the next action. The later Steps 1A–1D own the new libpq SCRAM helper, schema-owner/migrator/runtime roles, protected credential delivery, and staged web/worker cutover before any old credential retirement. Until then, external administrator use is an acknowledged governance boundary, not a falsely enforced M0 guarantee.
- Split migration from seeding without breaking a merged intermediate head. M0C adds guarded importable cores and direct fresh-only wrappers beside existing entrypoints; it does **not** change `migrate.ts`, `seed.ts`, or any package alias. M0D1 exercises its guarded executor only through its direct verification entrypoint and likewise leaves every legacy/package alias unchanged. M0D2, only after its proof bootstrap and proof engine are green, turns `migrate.ts` into a backward-compatible thin wrapper over the proof-bearing guarded runner and keeps `seed.ts` as an executable local/fresh-only wrapper plus importable module; existing `db:migrate` and `db:seed` aliases continue their named action on an allowed local/fresh target and fail before writes on production. M0E's deployment-surface cutover atomically changes root/package manifests to `db:migrate:guarded`, `db:migrate-and-seed`, and `db:seed:local`, removes deprecated aliases, and updates workflows/operator commands; direct and old aliases are then rejection-tested. `migrate-guarded.ts` is the sole supported production CLI and composes target guard, release coordination, order, ledger, and proof without seeding. Intermediate-head tests at M0C, D1, and D2 invoke every then-existing alias and require its original local/fresh effect or explicit pre-write production refusal.
- Upgrade `packages/db/src/check-migration-drift.ts` with two explicit modes. Required pre-merge CI runs `--mode candidate` only against its just-provisioned fresh database: it applies/observes the candidate branch through the fresh guarded path and requires Drizzle journal parity, raw-file/baseline checksums, control-schema version, complete raw ledger, no `applying` rows, one verified candidate release head, and fresh idempotency; it neither reads nor requires production bootstrap, so M0 and later schema-first PRs can merge. Post-merge delivery runs `--mode deployed --target production --expect-sha <merged-sha> --artifact <protected-path>` after the production action and requires all of those checks plus full retained proof/checkpoint validation, exact merged SHA/release head, and catalog continuity; it writes a content-free signed artifact containing only target fingerprint, merged SHA, release/proof IDs/digests, result, and time. `M0_NOT_BOOTSTRAPPED` fails only this strict deployed mode. Add `packages/db/src/wait-migration-release.ts` and root `db:wait-migration-release`; it accepts expected SHA/proof ID, polls under the read-only target guard for at most ten minutes with five-second backoff, exits immediately on failed/mismatched/competing release state, and succeeds only by invoking deployed drift and returning its artifact. The existing `pr-check` step switches to candidate mode; post-merge migration delivery runs the named waiter command before completion. Tests prove candidate passes with a pending migration on a fresh target, deployed rejects the same pre-production state, waiter timeout/mismatch/failure are non-zero, and both modes fail each missing/mismatched component. Help/error text names only the guarded production path.
- Update every active operator/caller instruction—`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/development.md`, `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/1password.md`, `docs/macro-understanding-implementation-plan.md`, `packages/db/migrations/sql/README.md`, the admin taxonomy page notice, active instructions in `DECISIONS.md`, `china_imp.md`, `MACRO_FIRST_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `MACRO_FIRST_REDESIGN.md`, and `plan_repo_reliability_and_release_gaps.md`—to forbid routine production migration through Supabase MCP/dashboard/bare entrypoints and use the guarded command, or the explicitly non-production migrate-and-seed command. Update the active prevention rule at `AGENTS.md:1241`; preserve its incident commands at lines 1229/1238 only as historical evidence. Before enabling the scanner, inventory **every** current match and either update active guidance or add one exact line entry to `config/retired-migration-command-exceptions.json`. The initial hash-pinned historical set includes the named `AGENTS.md`/`DECISIONS.md`/deferred-plan incident evidence, immutable comments in migrations 13, 48, 61, and 99, `evals/shape-aware-stage2.md:114`, `evals/macro-first-battery.md:445`, and every matching line in the two legacy `HANDOFF.d/2026-08-06T1510Z-*` records; each entry stores path, exact line-text SHA-256, classification, and rationale—never a directory wildcard. The scanner test first proves its generated inventory exactly equals active updates plus this manifest, then rejects an added/changed/unclassified occurrence; negative-test fixtures use a separate test-only allowlist. It governs repository callers, not external administrators or old binaries.
- Update `.github/workflows/pr-check.yml` so its ordinary Ubuntu build/test job remains capped at 15 minutes, while required Ubuntu `migration-proof-candidate` has a 40-minute budget for portable pure/database tests only: setup plus guarded provisioning/migration/idempotency/old-app compatibility is capped at 15 minutes, catalog proof at 15, cleanup at 5, leaving 5 minutes headroom. A required `m0-windows-guards` job on `windows-latest` runs PowerShell ACL, loopback-listener, firewall capability/cleanup, process-kill, and BitLocker-detection fail-closed tests against synthetic local data; it does not require Docker or production access. **Verification and delivery gate**

Version language in the umbrella verification bullets is strict: M0C “control schema” means bootstrap 001's ledger/release tables only; D1 runs its executor only through the direct test entrypoint and creates no proof/authorization table; “once M0D is merged” for proof-bearing initialization means specifically after D2 has installed bootstrap 002 and its proof engine; authorization paths remain unavailable until D3 installs bootstrap 003; the production writer fence remains unavailable until D4 installs bootstrap 004 in synthetic/fresh tests and M0F applies that checksum-pinned file to production. No milestone may import a type, query a table, install a trigger, change an alias, or claim a test owned by a later version. D2's proof-manifest head enumerates only bootstrap 001+002's four existing control tables; D3 replaces it with the six-table head after bootstrap 003; D4 extends the infrastructure manifest with bootstrap 004's function/trigger inventory without adding a seventh table. Any later umbrella phrase “all six control tables” describes D3/final table state only and is not a D2 dependency.

- A pure unit fixture—without creating migration files—passes synthetic names `13_extraction_constraints.sql` and `105_jev_claim_support_holds.sql` to `orderRawMigrationNames` and proves `13` precedes `105`; it also proves every synthetic `100+` normal file follows `98` and optional `99` remains separately last. Against the real directory, preflight aborts before URL lookup/connection/apply on malformed or unapproved-duplicate input. A hostile import test replaces all connection/env hooks with throwers, imports the pure modules/verifier, and proves zero connection, seed, or migration side effects. Tests cover wrong/retired project, production URL passed as snapshot, spoofed username/host, non-TLS, wrong database/role, and target drift between guard and transaction; all make zero writes.
- M0D adds named `packages/db/src/verify-raw-migration-baseline.ts`, `packages/db/src/verify-raw-migration-idempotency.ts`, `packages/db/src/migration-proof-mac.ts`, and `packages/db/src/migration-proof-store.ts`. M0C deliberately stops short: it may bootstrap its control schema and exercise ledger/release/crash transitions only with synthetic no-op fixture actions, while every request to apply `01`, Drizzle, or any real raw project migration fails before migration SQL with `M0_PROOF_ENGINE_NOT_INSTALLED`. Once M0D is merged, fresh initialization proves an empty guarded target and invokes `bootstrap`; only then does `migrate` apply `01_extensions.sql` in one runner-owned transaction, verify the exact extension postcondition, and record `01`. Only after `pgcrypto` exists may database-side canonical SHA-256 roots run for Drizzle and remaining raw files; fresh/snapshot tests authenticate their aggregate roots through the injected fake MAC adapter, while production is hard-coded to the Cloud KMS adapter established by M0I. Fresh/snapshot targets are exclusive offline databases, so no application writer is permitted while legacy self-managed files execute. Production bootstrap/migrate/idempotency execute no legacy SQL: each verifies the target, opens one dedicated non-pooled `SERIALIZABLE` transaction with database-enforced absolute four-minute `transaction_timeout` plus shorter `statement_timeout`, `lock_timeout`, and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`, attempts only `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` on the migration key for at most 30 seconds, and rolls back/exits non-zero without writes if contention persists. Once the transaction-scoped lock is held, it locks and re-verifies the current release/proof head; a waiter whose snapshot predates a competing commit must receive/retry a serialization failure before any candidate effect. It then captures before state, performs/observes its ledger action, captures after state in that same snapshot (including its own writes), validates, MACs the bounded aggregate proof through KMS, inserts its proof row, commits, which database-atomically releases the advisory lock; rollback, statement timeout, idle-in-transaction timeout, backend termination, and connection loss do the same without client cleanup authority. A two-runner barrier test makes runner B wait through runner A's commit and proves B chains from A rather than forking; a stuck-holder fixture proves timeout at 30 seconds, zero writes, actionable holder diagnostics without query/secret text, and clean retry after release. Disconnect/lock-release, KMS failure, and serialization anomalies abort safely. The production maintenance boundary below drains and locks repository-controlled writers before these observations; external privileged changes remain outside that atomic guarantee and are detected/fenced by the mandatory post-commit check. The read-only production baseline uses the same bounded lock-before-snapshot comparison but inserts no proof.
- **Versioned manifest rollover:** replace one mutable manifest with immutable `config/migration-proof-manifests/v<N>.json` files and `config/migration-proof-manifest-head.json`. Every authorization packet pins distinct `beforeManifestVersion/digest` and `afterManifestVersion/digest`. The current verified release's after-manifest/root must equal the next release's before-manifest/root. Inside `apply_verify`, compute the before root under the old manifest; execute SQL; require the candidate catalog exactly matches the reviewed after manifest; compute the after root under that new shape; and store a transition digest over prior proof/root, both manifest versions/digests, migration checksums, expected delta, and after root. Roots with different schemas are never compared as if identical. A new table/column/control-schema version must appear only in the candidate after manifest with explicit encoder, owner, ACL, policy, and inclusion rules; any changed object absent from the before/after diff or any unlisted post-object rolls back. The same transaction advances manifest head plus release/proof/action state, so a crash cannot expose a new schema with an old head. Baseline bootstrap is the explicit `legacy-v0 -> control-v1` transition; Step 1A is `control-v1 -> jev-foundation-v2`, and every 104–107 release adds its own successor. Tests cover additive tables, nullable/non-null columns, enums/indexes/policies/grants, control-table upgrades, invalid version skips/forks/downgrades, old-app compatibility, missing encoders, extra/missing objects, tampered prior roots, crash at head advance/commit, and successful next-release continuity.
- Comparison stays in database temporary tables and computes versioned SHA-256 aggregate roots; raw rows and per-row hashes never leave the database session. Only the bounded top-level root packet leaves through a protected pipe to Cloud KMS `macSign`, whose HMAC-SHA-256 key is non-exportable. The active immutable manifest selected by `config/migration-proof-manifest-head.json` explicitly enumerates every included application schema/table/column and the schema/ACL/owner shape of all six control tables; an unlisted user relation or column hard-stops. Tables with primary keys use per-key/value digests folded into one aggregate; others use a sorted multiset aggregate. Canonical row encoding is versioned and length-prefixes the table, column, declared type OID/name, NULL marker, and PostgreSQL binary-send representation under fixed UTC/C collation settings; the verifier rejects any included type without an approved stable encoder. Fixtures cover every type actually present, plus NULL/empty, NaN/-0, timestamps, arrays, JSONB, bytea, pgvector, enum, domain, and collation cases. Application roots include no control rows and are compared only before/after **within one locked migration snapshot**, where the maintenance boundary blocks ordinary runtime writes and only the migration's allowlisted data effects may differ. They are recorded for audit but never compared across runs because legitimate application traffic changes rows between proofs; M0 does not claim to detect arbitrary between-run administrator DML. Mutable ledger/release/key/authorization/action rows are deliberately excluded from any historical table root. Instead, every state transition inserts an immutable proof version containing canonical complete before/after snapshots and digests of every affected control row, transition kind/ordinal, and the prior immutable proof hash/MAC in the same transaction that mutates the current row. Before any action, the verifier walks all proof versions oldest-to-newest, KMS-verifies each full immutable row, replays the allowed transition state machine, and requires the folded head snapshot to equal the complete current mutable control rows before computing/MACing the prospective version. Historical roots therefore replay against stored append-only versions, never today's mutated rows. Missing/forked/duplicate/out-of-order versions, a current-row mismatch, or tampering with head/middle/oldest proof blocks. Tests exercise every legal state transition and alter each snapshot, ordinal, current row, and chain field while proving no raw KMS key, application row, or per-row digest leaves the database/KMS boundary.
- The proof is deny-by-default across database and cluster state. Each immutable file under `config/migration-proof-manifests/` selected by the reviewed before/after head transition enumerates every supported user-owned PostgreSQL object class and rejects any unclassified class/command tag before apply.
