Canonical database-side aggregate roots cover database properties and per-database/per-role settings; roles, attributes, memberships and default privileges (never plaintext verifiers); schemas; all relation kinds; columns; constraints; indexes; sequences; routines; triggers and event triggers; views/materialized views and security options; RLS policies; types/domains/enums; operators/casts; languages; collations/conversions; text-search objects; extensions; publications/subscriptions and replication origins; foreign-data wrappers/servers/user mappings; tablespaces; statistics; and large-object metadata **and content**. Secret-bearing subscription/server/user-mapping options and large-object bytes are digested only inside the database; only the final aggregate root is sent to KMS for MAC and emitted. A pre/post inventory of user-owned `pg_catalog` object classes plus DDL event-trigger command tags must equal the applicable reviewed manifest version; unsupported access methods, cluster objects, dynamic command tags, or new classes hard-stop rather than disappear from the root. Tests create/alter/drop one object in every class—including role membership/settings, event trigger, publication/subscription, FDW/user mapping, large object, database setting, and dynamic SQL—and require either an exact allowlisted root delta or pre-apply rejection.
- Mutable sequence state is gated exactly on exclusive fresh/snapshot targets; rehearsals attribute any direct/indirect change to the exact file. Production rejects a candidate whose rehearsal changed sequence state; such work needs a separate reviewed maintenance plan. Production takes no sequence locks and treats observations as non-gating audit. Catalog/cluster continuity requires prior after-roots to equal next before-roots except an exact managed-service allowlist, while current DDL matches its change allowlist. Production in-transaction proof work is cursor-paged with 30-second per-table and a **three-minute 30-second proof ceiling**, leaving 30 seconds for commit/rollback inside the absolute four-minute transaction cap and one further minute for fenced canary/local release inside the five-minute writer-outage cap; exceeding a bound aborts rather than samples. Longer offline fresh/snapshot evaluation may use a separately declared 15-minute ceiling but grants no production extension. Production bootstrap permits only exact control DDL/proof rows; idempotency changes only its chained proof row.
- Proof storage is atomic with the candidate database effect, while its non-exportable MAC service is an explicit dependency inside that transaction. The same database transaction inserts an immutable admin-only `oracle_migration_proofs` row containing run ID, proof kind (`action|renewal`), exact Git SHA, target fingerprint, manifest/file checksums, transaction snapshot/txid, before/after aggregate row/catalog roots, bounded changed-key aggregate digests, canonical before/after control-state snapshots/digests, transition ordinal, result, prior-proof ID/hash/MAC, full-chain accumulator, Cloud KMS key-resource/version, MAC, and 90-day **action-freshness** deadline. KMS timeout/refusal occurs before insert/commit and rolls the transaction back. Expiry blocks a migration action but never invalidates an old MAC. `oracle_migration_proof_keys` stores only KMS resource/version/fingerprint/status—never key bytes—and every registry transition is itself represented in the next immutable proof snapshot. `packages/db/src/renew-migration-proof.ts` can create a no-schema-change proof after a full guarded read-only comparison; if the predecessor is expired it requires the production action gate plus a new full baseline, rather than making the chain permanently unusable. Proof versions and every referenced KMS version are retained indefinitely: they are small, content-free control evidence, and no compaction, checkpoint, deletion, key disable, or key destruction path exists in M0. A later retention design would be a separate reviewed infrastructure/schema outcome that first proves equivalent historical replay; it is not assumed here. Tests reject proof DELETE/UPDATE, missing historic KMS versions, head/middle/oldest tampering, KMS refusal/timeout/wrong-version, transition replay mismatch, crash before/after proof commit, and retry. Commit failure leaves neither migration/ledger effect nor proof; success leaves both. A post-commit reporter may put only the content-free proof ID/digest on issue #14; report failure is retryable and cannot change proof validity.
- Add side-effect-free `packages/db/src/read-production-version.ts`, `packages/db/src/provision-m0-snapshot.ts`, `scripts/provision-m0-postgres.ps1`, `scripts/provision-m0-snapshot.ps1`, and `scripts/run-guarded-db-proof.ps1`. Before any container selection, version preflight also executes `SHOW transaction_timeout`, proves the server enforces it in a disposable four-second transaction despite repeated sub-second statements and a lost client, and blocks M0 entirely when the setting is absent or ineffective—there is no client-timer fallback. Wrapper action `version-preflight` exchanges the exact-workflow GitHub OIDC token for a short-lived GCP identity, reads only the two M0I Secret Manager versions into protected pipes, uses the existing PostgreSQL client only for target-guarded `SHOW server_version_num`/identity reads, performs zero writes, emits only the version and target fingerprint, and applies the same cleanup/leak sentinels as later actions. The provisioner accepts only that nonsecret signed preflight artifact and selects a tested entry in `config/m0-postgres-runtime.json`, which maps a bounded production patch range to one digest-pinned container image and exact `pg_dump`, `pg_restore`, `psql`, and temporary-server versions; an artifact mismatch/expiry or no version match fails closed. The Windows synthetic-guard branch (never production credentials/data) publishes a random loopback-only `127.0.0.1` port with a generated one-run credential delivered by pipe, verifies with `Get-NetTCPConnection` that no wildcard/non-loopback listener exists, adds/removes a scoped firewall deny rule for non-loopback traffic, and passes the protected loopback URL to the host-side pnpm/PowerShell commands. It proves the configured version relationship and a schema-only restore probe and fails closed if the pinned runtime/image, BitLocker, firewall, or compatibility test is unavailable—there is no assumed machine PostgreSQL service or ambient identity. That Windows synthetic branch creates exclusive `oracle_m0_snapshot_<run>` storage on its system drive only after proving the container volume and encrypted capture/staging directory are local NTFS paths and ACLs grant only the current user, the container runtime service, and SYSTEM. The dump is streamed through AES-256-GCM encryption to an authenticated file, decrypted only through a pipe into restore, never written plaintext, and deleted after restore. A registry records creation, port, firewall rule, image digest, versions, 24-hour destruction deadline, and cleanup state before capture; startup and `finally` scavengers resume interrupted cleanup, cryptographic erasure deletes the one-run key first, and cleanup verification proves database/container/volume removal, listener/firewall removal, process closure, file absence, ACL removal, and registry completion. Failure to prove encryption, ACLs, retention, compatibility, or cleanup blocks that test; production uses the isolated ephemeral Linux contract below and refuses this Windows branch. For production, the proof wrapper receives the current session-pooler URL and Supabase management token only from Secret Manager and invokes Cloud KMS MAC operations by resource name; no KMS key, 1Password token, cloud-management credential, or long-lived GCP key enters the runner. It transports values only through distinct current-user-only protected handles/anonymous pipes, parses URLs in memory, invokes the package command with bound parameters, sets session error-parameter logging length to zero, zeroes/deletes all inputs in `finally`, and emits no values. Sentinel tests use independent markers for every secret and scan captured stdout/stderr, process arguments/environment, protected-file ACLs, client diagnostics, `pg_stat_activity`, `pg_stat_statements`, PostgreSQL error/activity logs, and the bounded Supabase log API window; any marker fragment blocks delivery. Tests also cover preflight write attempts, stale/tampered artifacts, loopback reachability from host pnpm/PowerShell, non-loopback refusal, every configured version boundary, wrong client/server version, incompatible dump/restore, missing runtime/image, each missing/partial secret pipe, child failure, cleanup failure, process kill, Ctrl-C, hostile output capture, and OIDC/KMS/Secret Manager denial.
- Add top-level `scripts/run-m0-rehearsal.ps1`. It owns version preflight, container start, fresh proof, policy-bounded encrypted production schema/masked-data capture and restore, snapshot proof, artifact deletion, snapshot/database drop, firewall/listener removal, and container/volume stop in one `try/finally`; individual provision commands are internal and refuse calls without its one-run capability. The production orchestrator obtains short-lived GCP identity through the exact-workflow OIDC contract, reads the current production session-pooler URL and management token from the two named Secret Manager versions through distinct protected pipes, and invokes Cloud KMS by resource name; local/synthetic rehearsal uses only generated credentials and the fake MAC adapter. Guarded Node code opens and keeps alive one verified read-only `REPEATABLE READ` production transaction, re-proves exact project/host/user/TLS/database/role, and calls `pg_export_snapshot()`. The orchestrator creates a one-run libpq service file inside the isolated production container's mode-0700 memory-backed tmpfs, owned only by its one-run UID, points `PGSERVICEFILE` only to its nonsecret path, and runs single-connection `pg_dump --schema-only --dbname=service=m0 --snapshot=<exported-id>`; separately allowlisted masked data uses snapshot-bound `COPY (SELECT ...)` streams under the export policy; the exported snapshot can be imported only on the same database cluster/database, binding every schema/data capture connection to the verified live transaction. No secret appears in arguments or environment; the service file and tmpfs key are zeroed/unlinked in `finally`, and the exporting transaction stays open until dump success then rolls back. Before restore, a content-free signed role manifest enumerates every role referenced by schema/object ownership, ACLs, default privileges, policies, and routine definitions. The local container creates those names as NOLOGIN/no-membership/no-superuser shadow roles (plus the known Supabase fixture roles), restores with ownership/ACL statements enabled—not `--no-owner`—and then requires exact owner/grantee parity while separately proving every shadow role remains NOLOGIN and unprivileged. Missing/unexpected role references, unsafe attributes, or owner/ACL drift abort. Wrong cluster/database, expired snapshot, parallel dump, service-file substitution, ACL failure, or guard/dump disconnect also aborts. Tests cover custom owners/grantees/policy roles and terminate before/after every child command, proving the next startup scavenger plus `finally` leaves no database, dump, service file, key material, artifact, listener, firewall rule, container, volume, shadow role, or registry entry; wrong/retired project, dump-connection drift, and every secret leak sentinel fail closed.
- The Windows-only synthetic guard's temporary state and lifecycle registry live only below fixed `C:\ProgramData\POP-Creations\OracleM0`, never a caller-selected path. Preflight opens the root by handle, resolves its final canonical path, proves a fixed local system drive with BitLocker on, rejects any reparse point/junction/symlink in every ancestor/child, and proves it is outside every Git worktree, repository, user profile, OneDrive/Dropbox/sync root, temp directory, UNC/network/subst path, and container bind outside that root. It creates the root/run directory atomically with inheritance disabled and explicit current-user/SYSTEM ACL before writing; every later open revalidates handle identity and stays beneath the root. Synthetic sentinel status plus filesystem canaries prove no artifact can enter a worktree or sync/network path. Tests cover junction swap races, symlink/reparse ancestors, case/8.3 aliases, `..`, subst/UNC paths, repository nesting, sync roots, and cleanup after process kill.
- Supabase log leak proof is watermark-based, not a single immediate query. Record the provider log watermark before the operation; after the database transaction and writer fence have ended, poll the bounded log API with backoff until its monotonic watermark is later than operation completion plus the documented ingestion grace, then scan the entire interval for every sentinel. This external poll never extends the writer fence or database transaction. Ten minutes without an advancing watermark, unavailable/partial pages, cursor gaps, or rate-limit exhaustion marks the release `contaminated`, blocks idempotency/future release/deployment success, and preserves no success artifact, but cannot falsely claim that writes remained blocked. Fake delayed-ingestion, stale-watermark, pagination-gap, duplicate-page, unavailable-log, and fence-duration tests prove the sentinel cannot pass early or extend the outage.
- Exact rerunnable sequence: `pnpm db:verify-raw-order`; then `pwsh -File scripts/run-m0-rehearsal.ps1 -RunId <run>` owns synthetic rehearsal and cleanup. After M0V0-C, M0V1-C, and M0V2-C, M0X0/X0-C merges and records only enable metadata, M0X1/X1-C deploys/proves Vercel/web, and M0X2/X2-C deploys/proves Trigger/worker. Only then may no-diff M0F obtain its database-write approval and run `run-guarded-db-proof.ps1 ...`. M0F alone owns baseline, bootstrap/migrate, fenced canary, fence release, external `postcommit_verify`, and idempotency. Its dedicated non-pooled `SERIALIZABLE` transaction has database-enforced four-minute `transaction_timeout`, shorter statement/lock/idle limits, one transaction-scoped advisory lock, and backward-compatible `applied_pending_external_check`; commit/rollback releases the lock atomically. The immutable database-clock fence expires by five minutes even after client loss. A fresh connection proves lock absence and active fence before fixed canaries; failure contaminates/blocks without extending the outage. Bootstrap/migrate/idempotency re-prove target/chain, never seed, and write only allowlisted state. KMS changes remain separate M0I2-shaped outcomes. No Jev schema may merge until M0F-C records strict proof.

- **M0W disabled workflow identities and image source of truth:** after M0D3, one deployment-only session adds `config/github-hosted-runner-images.json` and four default-branch paths: `.github/workflows/migration-production.yml` accepts only `identity-probe`, `.github/workflows/production-approval.yml` only `reviewer-identity-probe`, `.github/workflows/deployment-production.yml` only `deployment-identity-probe`, and `.github/workflows/vercel-build-suppression.yml` only `settings-identity-probe`. All four use pinned actions, `workflow_dispatch`, minimal `contents: read`/`id-token: write`, and no secret/resource client, reviewer, attestation, database, deploy, project-setting, read, rehearsal, or write behavior; every other action hard-fails `M0_PRODUCTION_ACTION_NOT_WIRED`. The image policy pins one official `actions/runner-images` release tag/commit, the SHA-256 of its `Ubuntu2404-Readme.md`, exact `ImageOS`, and exact `ImageVersion`. Each probe runs runner/isolation checks, requests an OIDC token for its future fixed audience, exposes no token/value, accesses no resource, and at M0W—before M0G creates any trust—must receive expected STS/provider denial. `pnpm verify:m0w:workflow-identities` rejects secret references, environments, production URLs, database/model/deployment/settings clients, non-probe actions, broader permission, unpinned actions, alternate triggers, reusable callers, or artifacts beyond content-free runner/OIDC claim digests. The same PR adds the always-run, path-unfiltered `Migration production contract` check, which scans all workflows and rejects duplicate check-run names or milestone-state drift. M0W records all four source checksums plus observed check-run name/App ID, provisions nothing, and performs no production action.
- **M0R repository-governance prerequisite:** the legacy `branches/main/protection` endpoint returned 404 on 2026-09-23, but M0R treats that as one signal only. In its own repository-governance session after M0W, add `config/repository-governance.json`, `scripts/reconcile-repository-governance.mjs`, and fake-transport tests. Read-only preflight must enumerate the legacy protection response, every repository ruleset, every organization ruleset visible to the authenticated owner, and GitHub's effective rules for `refs/heads/main`; unavailable organization/effective-rule visibility blocks rather than assuming absence. It normalizes rule IDs/sources/targets/enforcement/bypass actors/required checks/merge restrictions, detects overlapping or contradictory rules, and refuses a second ruleset when an existing effective rule already supplies the requirement or would make the desired result ambiguous. Before writing desired state, query the M0W merged commit's check runs and pin the exact observed context strings and GitHub App integration IDs; the current verified baseline is `Build @oracle/web` and `verify`, both from GitHub Actions App ID `15368`, while `Migration production contract` must be observed on M0W's merge before it is admitted. Guessed workflow/job composites such as `PR check / build` or `task gates / verify` are forbidden. The desired state records the complete preflight inventory and the minimum reconciliation needed so the effective result on `main` has no bypass actors; blocks deletion and non-fast-forward/force updates; requires a pull request, resolved conversations, squash merge, and each of those three observed, App-bound, up-to-date successful checks. That may create or update active repository ruleset `oracle-main-release`, but it never weakens, duplicates, or silently supersedes an inherited rule. The contract check remains always-run on every PR and main push, scans all workflows, and fails on duplicate/spoofable required-check names, any production secret reference, or production workflow permissions/triggers/paths/actions outside the milestone-state contract. The session first merges the reviewed config/script through the repository's then-effective rules, then runs the repository/infrastructure gate and obtains an independent exact-resource/action APPROVE for the one ruleset apply; `--plan` is read-only and `--apply <digest>` refuses inventory drift. Post-apply enumeration of legacy, repository, organization, and effective rules must match the reviewed union and prove zero effective bypass actors; a protected throwaway-ref/API fixture proves direct/force/delete updates are refused and PR checks are required without mutating main. Fixtures cover inherited-only protection, overlapping stronger/weaker rules, conflicting merge/check requirements, hidden organization rules, duplicate contexts, stale inventory, and rollback. Until that evidence is recorded, M0I cannot create WIF trust. Same-path code merged later remains an acknowledged repository-owner trust boundary, but it can no longer arrive by direct push or unchecked PR. Rollback is a separate reviewed ruleset reconciliation, never an ad hoc admin bypass.
- **M0R sensitive-path independent ownership and executable closure:** M0R adds `.github/CODEOWNERS` entries covering `.github/CODEOWNERS` itself, every `.github/workflows/**` file, all `packages/db/**`, all `scripts/**`, all `config/**`, every migration/bootstrap/generated journal path, the two app-side writer-boundary files plus every web/worker mutation caller and transitive helper discovered by the mutation scanner, and root/package execution inputs (`package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `turbo.json`, `.npmrc`, and any later file discovered by either closure scanner). This intentionally broad floor prevents an unchanged privileged wrapper or app writer from executing altered migrator, guard, authorization, rehearsal, deployment, activation, revocation, erasure, writer-fence, secret-delivery, manifest, bootstrap, enable-config, package-script, or transitive import content. `scripts/verify-production-executable-closure.mjs` has twenty registered roots: migration, approval, `.github/workflows/m0-infrastructure-approval.yml`, deployment, `.github/workflows/vercel-build-suppression.yml`, `.github/workflows/production-approval-revoke.yml`, future `.github/workflows/m0-secret-transfer.yml`, future `.github/workflows/jev-use-case-activation.yml`, future `.github/workflows/typesafe-control-plane-provision.yml`, future `.github/workflows/typesafe-vercel-secret-delivery.yml`, future `.github/workflows/typesafe-trigger-secret-delivery.yml`, future `.github/workflows/typesafe-key-emergency.yml`, future `.github/workflows/jev-live-recall-post.yml`, future `.github/workflows/source-erasure-prepare.yml`, `.github/workflows/source-erasure-verify.yml`, `.github/workflows/source-erasure-production.yml`, `.github/workflows/source-restore-reconcile.yml`, `.github/workflows/source-erasure-tombstone-lifecycle.yml`, `apps/web/lib/database-write-boundary.ts`, and `apps/workers/src/lib/database-write-boundary.ts`. Before their owning milestones, absent roots are recorded as `not-yet-installed`; M0G introduces the identity-only transfer root, M0D4 introduces the two app roots and reverse-resolves every production mutation entrypoint through its boundary, M0E introduces revocation/deployment, Step 1B introduces activation, Step 2A introduces all four TypeSafe control/delivery/emergency roots, Step 8B-2 introduces the live-post root, and Step 1E must introduce all five erasure/restore roots and replace every marker. Every installed workflow is independently forward-resolved through pinned actions, local actions, commands, package aliases, scripts, imports, config/schema/manifest/migration/registry/replica reads and gets its own content digest; the M0G approval binds the identity-only transfer root and five identity/resource graphs, while the M0I1–M0I5 approvals bind its later exact capability/source/destination transition; the 2A approval binds each TypeSafe workflow's independent closure plus its registry/action/source-field/destination graph, the 8B-3 approval binds the live-post closure/manifest, and the 1E approval binds the ordered five-root digest set plus cross-workflow handoff schemas, so one workflow cannot inherit another's approval. The scanner emits a sorted content-addressed closure manifest and fails on a missing required root after its owning milestone, any mutation path that bypasses a boundary, dynamic/unresolved paths, shell-generated commands, out-of-repository code, or any node not matched by the exact independent CODEOWNER rule. The always-run production-contract check recomputes this stage-correct closure and digest on every PR/main push and compares it with the reviewed manifest; M0G/M0I1/M0I2/M0I3/M0I4/M0I5/M0S/M0E/M0V0/M0V1/M0V2/M0X0/M0X1/M0X2/M0F/Step 1B/2A/2K/activation/8B-3/1E approvals bind the applicable digest. M0G proves the five identity/resource graphs; M0I1 proves the transfer graph and M0I2–M0I5 prove one identity each; M0E proves deployment/revocation and dependencies; M0V0 proves the setting transition, M0V1 the web bundle, and M0V2 the worker bundle; 2A proves the four TypeSafe workflow closures and denial graphs; 2K proves credential provisioning/rotation/revocation scopes; 8B-3 proves live-post isolation/rollback; 1E proves all five erasure/restore workflows, their attestation logic, and cross-workflow handoffs. All are CODEOWNED, independently approved after the last push, and included in the sealed review digest. The named owner is an independently controlled GitHub account granted only the minimum repository Write role GitHub requires for CODEOWNERS recognition, with no administration, secret, GCP, production, or bypass authority; the initial candidate is `@devopswithkube`, but M0R must verify that account accepts and remains independent. If none accepts, M0R blocks. Effective rules require one approval from that exact CODEOWNER, dismiss stale approvals, require approval of the most recent reviewable push by someone other than the pusher, require the exact closure/check results, and define zero bypass actors. The plan deliberately does **not** use GitHub's restrict-updates rule: GitHub implements allowed updaters as bypass actors, which conflicts with zero bypass. Any repository writer may perform only the mechanical squash merge after all no-bypass rules pass; the merge grants no authority and cannot change the approved head. A review is valid only after the exact-head attested `ai-codex-review final-check` artifact is attached; the code owner confirms that artifact, the complete closure manifest/digest, and the exact sensitive-path diff. Fake GraphQL/REST plus protected throwaway-PR fixtures cover self/stale/pre-last-push/pusher approval, unavailable code owner, direct/force/delete update, same-PR wrapper/check/dependency/CODEOWNERS changes, hidden dynamic dependency, omitted/new workflow or app root/helper, raw mutation bypass, unowned closure node, manifest forgery, merge before requirements, and administrator/ruleset bypass; all refuse. The positive fixture proves an ordinary writer can squash-merge only the unchanged exact last push after external and CODEOWNER approval. Later closure changes remain governed identically; repository write access alone can never produce a main-bound privileged identity.
- Independently of import reachability, M0R creates generated-and-reviewed `config/platform-build-inputs.json`, the exhaustive protected executable/deployment floor for Vercel, Next, Trigger, pnpm, TypeScript, and the package/build launchers. It explicitly includes `vercel.json`, `apps/web/next.config.ts`, `apps/web/tsconfig.json`, `apps/web/postcss.config.js`, `apps/web/proxy.ts`, root and app `package.json` files, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `turbo.json`, every root/extended `tsconfig*.json`, `.npmrc`, `apps/workers/trigger.config.ts`, `.vercelignore` when present, all Next convention candidates (`instrumentation.ts`, `instrumentation-client.ts`, `middleware.ts`, `proxy.ts`, and supported extension variants), environment declaration surfaces, and every referenced build script. A framework/CLI-resolution scanner regenerates the manifest from the pinned installed versions and rejects an omitted, extra, renamed, alternate, dashboard-overridden, generated, or dynamically selected convention/config/build input; import reachability is never the completeness rule. CODEOWNERS, the closure manifest, exact-head review, deployment approval, and every later erasure approval bind every digest. Mutation fixtures change each manifest member alone and add each recognized convention/config candidate alone, proving the always-run contract check and sealed approval both invalidate before deploy or production access.
- M0X1 and M0X2 deployment approvals and M0F's later database approval both bind that complete executable closure digest. Each may use it only for its one platform to deploy and prove the app boundaries; M0F may use the unchanged digest only after X2-C no-drift proof and cannot deploy.
- **M0G final GCP identity/resource provisioning:** after M0R-C, a code-only Oracle PR adds CODEOWNED `config/m0-gcp-identities.json`, `scripts/verify-m0-gcp-identities.mjs`, provider-native declarative commands, and mutually exclusive `gcp-identity-proof` actions to the four protected workflows. It also installs `.github/workflows/m0-secret-transfer.yml` as the registered fifth root in identity-only form; it accepts only `transfer-identity-proof`, contains no 1Password/broker/secret-value client, and cannot add a secret version. The manifest may name only project `vertex-ai-497120`, pool/provider `oracle-github-actions`, five final exact-workflow service accounts/bindings, six empty authoritative destination Secret Manager resources, six empty source-capability bootstrap resources, the empty content-free journal resource, two non-exportable KMS keys initially denied for MAC/sign use, and exact IAM/audit queries. These are direct `workflow_dispatch` jobs, so each provider condition pins repository ID `1244191375`, owner ID `55610577`, `refs/heads/main`, event `workflow_dispatch`, its exact `workflow_ref`, and the exact merged M0G `workflow_sha`; `job_workflow_ref` is forbidden because no reusable workflow is involved. A workflow can never self-authorize a different SHA. Fake-provider tests reject every extra resource/action/member, wildcard, inherited/broad role, alternate project/repository/ref/workflow/event/SHA, PR/fork/sibling workflow, `job_workflow_ref` substitution, mutable script, secret version/value, KMS operation, database/network/deploy/settings call, and unlisted delta. The exact-head-reviewed PR must merge before any cloud mutation.

  After that PR merges but before any GCP apply, all five direct identity-proof actions run with no cloud trust and request their GitHub OIDC tokens only for local claim inspection. `--mode claim-shape` verifies GitHub issuer/signature/JWKS, audience, repository/owner numeric IDs, `refs/heads/main`, `workflow_dispatch`, exact per-file `workflow_ref`, and exact M0G `workflow_sha`; it proves `job_workflow_ref` is absent/unused, emits only canonical claim digests and run/attempt IDs, and rejects a missing, undocumented, or unexpected claim. A named Google Cloud security administrator then uses a fresh provider-hosted Cloud Shell session authenticated by the existing corporate Google identity; no reusable credential, key file, browser-exported token, GitHub secret, workstation, or Oracle production secret is introduced. A fresh sealed independent technical APPROVE binds the administrator identity, exact project, immutable manifest/plan/closure digests, the five verified claim artifacts and exact M0G workflow refs/SHA, allowed resources/IAM, expiry, rollback command, and single-use operation ID. The administrator runs only the reviewed plan/apply command from a content-addressed release fetched and hash-verified in Cloud Shell. It maps and conditions on both `assertion.workflow_ref` and `assertion.workflow_sha`; later workflow revisions remain denied until their own milestone's fresh approval and external administrator IAM transition replaces only that workflow's old SHA with the exact new merged SHA. It creates the final resource shells and bindings directly—there is no bootstrap service account, delegated installer, standing admin credential, or self-provisioning workflow. A wrong-project, claim-shape mismatch, existing-resource drift, extra delta, or inability to express the five least-privilege bindings stops before apply. The five merged identity-proof actions then live-prove only their own identity and metadata-level resource visibility plus cross-identity, PR, fork, sibling, branch, tag, owner/repository, workflow-ref/SHA, direct token exchange, secret-version/value, KMS-use, IAM-change, database/network/deploy/settings, and unlisted-resource denials. Cloud Audit Logs must match each approved operation. The content-free M0G artifact binds claim/plan/apply digests, administrator principal ID, pool/provider/five-account/resource/binding IDs, CEL digests, allow/deny counts, audit watermark/query digest, and rollback command; M0G-C independently records it. A crash or partial apply invokes only the sealed exact rollback/resume from a fresh Cloud Shell session and blocks G-C/M0I1; no workflow can broaden or repair IAM.
- **M0I1–M0I5 workflow-bound credential outcomes:** after M0G-C, five ordered implementation/closeout pairs replace the former M0I umbrella. M0I1 changes only the transfer workflow to `transfer-six`, initializes and completes or rolls back the journaled six-row transfer, and M0I1-C records it. M0I2 changes only migration to `credential-probe`, enables/proves the two KMS keys and two database-resource reads without opening a database, and M0I2-C records it. M0I3 changes/proves only reviewer token access; M0I4 only the two deployment-token resources; M0I5 only the settings-token resource; each has its own `-C` documentation closeout before the next starts. Every implementation PR is exact-head reviewed and merged first. Then the M0R-installed credential-free infrastructure-approval workflow issues a fresh GitHub-attested approval for one exact IAM delta; a named Google Cloud security administrator uses a fresh Cloud Shell session to replace only that workflow condition's prior `workflow_sha` with the exact new merged SHA, proves the old SHA denied and new SHA allowed, and exits. The workload workflow cannot change IAM or validate itself into authority. Every database/read/rehearsal/review/attestation/deploy/project-setting/write action not owned by that milestone still hard-fails. No pair creates a service account, pool, secret resource, KMS key, or unrelated IAM grant.

  The transfer identity additionally has `destroy-version` only on those six bootstrap resources; “read only” above distinguishes bootstrap resources from writable destinations and does not omit this mandatory cleanup authority. It has no destroy authority on any destination.

  No secret is ever read, copied, piped, hashed, or temporarily held on `916-alien` or another persistent workstation, and no 1Password service-account/vault token is stored in GitHub. Before any capability is issued or any secret-bearing bootstrap version exists, the named administrator creates and reads back the M0I1 content-free write-ahead operation record in the M0G-precreated journal resource, binding operation/approval/mapping/closure digests, all six intended source and destination slots, and state `planned`. Only then may that separately reviewed fresh hosted environment obtain one provider-enforced, expiring, one-field read capability at a time and write it into its distinct pre-created GCP bootstrap resource; the journal is advanced to `issuing` before each issuance/upload and to `issued` only after version-ID readback. Each capability can reveal only its named item/field, cannot list/read another vault item or write anything, is bound to the one M0I1 operation, and expires in at most 30 minutes. If 1Password cannot enforce that exact field/item boundary, M0I1 blocks for a provider-supported isolated-vault/broker design; a vault-wide service token is forbidden even temporarily. After the separate sealed approval and exact-SHA IAM transition, `.github/workflows/m0-secret-transfer.yml` runs once on a fresh `ubuntu-24.04` runner under `oracle-secret-transfer`, reads only those six recorded capabilities, redeems each directly into a masked process-local stream, and writes it immediately to the mapped destination. The job has no checkout after verifying the reviewed six-row mapping, no cache/artifact/debug/step-summary access, no job-level secret environment, and no database/KMS/deploy/reviewer/settings authority. It fingerprints destination readback, disables explicitly named predecessor destination versions only after all six match, destroys all six bootstrap versions/capabilities, zeroes buffers, destroys the runner, scans args/environment/logs/temp for sentinels, and emits only resource/version/fingerprint/denial metadata. Interruption during issuance is recoverable from the journal: inventory finds and destroys any `issuing|issued` version before resume or rollback. Success requires live denial of every bootstrap capability and transfer identity after cleanup; an ambiguous write/redemption/cleanup fails and recovery uses the same operation journal with fresh exact-field capabilities, never a broad token or blind second write.

  M0G pre-creates nonsecret Secret Manager resource `oracle-m0-secret-transfer-journal`; the administrator may add only the initial content-free write-ahead version under the sealed M0I1 issuance approval, and only the transfer identity may append later state/access/disable versions. The journal contains operation/approval/mapping/closure digests, six intended slots, source-capability version IDs as they are created, destination/predecessor version IDs, per-row `planned|issuing|issued|redeeming|written_unverified|verified|rolled_back`, timestamps, and cleanup states—never a value or reversible hash. It advances and is read back before and after every external side effect. Recovery under a new sealed approval inventories journal, all six bootstrap resources, and destination versions created since operation start; it destroys unknown/partial bootstrap versions before reissuance, retains exactly one matching destination version per row, disables duplicate/incomplete new versions, restores predecessor aliases if the six-row set cannot complete, destroys every bootstrap, and records terminal `committed|rolled_back`; blind issuance or add-version retry is forbidden. M0I1-C rejects any missing pre-issuance record, nonterminal journal, duplicate/new unrecorded version, live bootstrap, or destination set other than all-six committed or the exact predecessor set. Fault tests stop before/after initial journal readback, every capability issuance/upload/readback/redemption, destination add/readback/disable, journal transition, bootstrap destroy, and runner termination.

  The five positive boundaries are therefore proved one at a time in M0I1 through M0I5, never in one dispatch or milestone. Each fresh runner verifies image/namespace/source checksum and exchanges only after external IAM already pins that exact merged SHA. M0I1 proves transfer/journal/bootstrap boundaries; M0I2 proves migration's two reads plus public-nonce MAC/sign and every sibling denial; M0I3 proves only reviewer-token fingerprint access; M0I4 proves only its two exact-project deployment-token fingerprints; M0I5 proves only settings-token fingerprint and read-only exact-project/single-field scope. No probe connects to a database, deploys, changes a project setting, invokes a canary, or invokes Codex. Each pair's table-driven fixtures reject sibling workflow, old/new/unapproved SHA, PR, fork, wrong ref/event/repository/path, cross-resource access, and IAM mutation; its closeout records live audit events before the next pair may rotate another binding. Any later workflow/IAM/key/resource/version change is a new independently reviewed transition outcome; none of M0I1–M0I5 runs production SQL, deployment, or setting mutation.
- **Ephemeral production runner isolation and route-specific egress:** M0D adds `scripts/assert-production-runner.mjs`, `scripts/lockdown-production-runner.sh`, an isolated rootless-container launcher, and fake-host tests; M0W binds them to the exact GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` image policy and rejects `self-hosted`, workstation, reused, or unknown provenance. Each sensitive job gets a fresh one-job VM and unique unprivileged PID/mount/network namespace with read-only source, `no-new-privileges`, dropped capabilities, seccomp/AppArmor, non-dumpable processes, no host sockets, mode-0700 tmpfs, and no persistent protected cache/volume. Before OIDC it verifies pinned runner/image and clean isolation. Deny-by-default egress is route-specific: migration/rehearsal/canary allows exact Supabase, exact `https://oracle.designflow.app` canary origin, GCP STS/Secret Manager/KMS, GitHub API/artifact, DNS, runner manifest, and time—never Trigger directly because the web endpoint performs fixed server-side dispatch; deployment allows GCP STS/Secret Manager, exact Vercel/Trigger API/registry hosts, GitHub read/artifact, DNS, manifest, and time, with no Oracle/database/Supabase/KMS route; reviewer allows GCP STS/Secret Manager, `api.openai.com:443`, GitHub read/artifact, DNS, manifest, and time; validate/attest allows only GitHub/OIDC, DNS, manifest, and time. Telemetry/update/fallback endpoints are disabled. TLS/SNI/address pinning defeat rebinding; wildcard provider/model egress is forbidden. Additional endpoint need blocks for reviewed policy change. Tests require Oracle canary only from migration, provider deploy only from deployment, reviewer model only from review, and negative cross-route/exfiltration. Only after lockdown may identity be requested. Separate fresh VMs exchange only content-free checksummed artifacts; cleanup kills isolation, destroys credentials/tmpfs, and scans output/state. Tests cover image drift, prior processes, ptrace/dump, inherited FDs, listeners, DNS/IPv6/Unix bypass, cache persistence, secret artifacts, cleanup failure, and route crossover. `916-alien` and every other persistent workstation are unconditionally excluded from production runner and secret-transfer use; no exception or fallback exists.
- **Bounded production maintenance, application-writer fence, migration-owned write capability, and external-admin boundary:** M0C reserves a single-row writer-fence generation/state in `oracle_schema_releases`; M0D4 adds `packages/db/src/maintenance-fence.ts`, `production-maintenance-boundary.ts`, updates `packages/db/src/client.ts`, adds `apps/web/lib/database-write-boundary.ts`, `apps/workers/src/lib/database-write-boundary.ts`, protected canaries at `apps/web/app/api/admin/maintenance-fence-canary/route.ts` and `apps/workers/src/trigger/maintenance-fence-canary.ts`, and `scripts/verify-runtime-write-fence.mjs`. Each writable application table gets the four-trigger topology defined in bootstrap 004. For ordinary/runtime sessions its `BEFORE STATEMENT` trigger calls `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock_shared` on the fixed key **before row effects**; `false` raises fixed SQLSTATE `55P03`/`ORACLE_MAINTENANCE_ACTIVE` immediately, while `true` snapshots the generation/run/transaction context. It never makes a blocking shared-lock call. The migration-owner branch first proves from `pg_locks` that this exact backend already holds the granted exclusive transaction-scoped advisory lock, then validates its locked authorization/action context without requesting a conflicting shared lock. `AFTER INSERT` reads its `NEW TABLE`, `AFTER DELETE` its `OLD TABLE`, and `AFTER UPDATE` both transition tables; each canonicalizes the table's manifest-declared primary/composite key plus before/after row hashes, sorts the affected set, and checks its operation/delta digest. A table without a stable manifest key, an unsupported partition/view/foreign-table topology, or PostgreSQL transition-table support blocks the release before DDL rather than receiving a generic trigger. The only active-fence exception is the exact guarded migration transaction: both `session_user` and `current_user` are the allowlisted migration role, that same backend's exclusive lock is granted, the snapped generation/run ID and transaction ID match the locked authorization/action row in `apply_verify`, the lease/revocation check is current, and each event-specific actual delta digest is in that action's reviewed data-delta allowlist. No caller-set GUC, role name prefix, owner membership alone, `SET ROLE`, or forged `pg_locks` input satisfies it. This permits only approved migration backfills and settings seeds while the fence remains active; an unlisted row/key/value effect raises from the `AFTER` trigger and rolls back the statement and release transaction.

  Every web/worker mutation is classified by the mutation scanner into one of two executable boundary modes. Database-local decisions use `withDatabaseWriteBoundary`: it opens the transaction, uses the same non-blocking shared-lock attempt and fixed maintenance error **before the first authoritative read**, performs every read/derive/write inside that transaction, and commits only if the generation is unchanged. Long/external-compute flows use `readDecisionSnapshot`: one short repeatable-read transaction first performs that non-blocking lock attempt and returns a content-free token containing generation plus every input row version/hash; after external work, `commitDecisionSnapshot` opens a new transaction, repeats the non-blocking attempt, locks/re-reads every declared input, and rejects/requeues unless generation and all versions/hashes still match before writing. No caller may perform an authoritative read before these entry points or keep an unversioned derived decision. The scanner reverse-traces reads as well as mutations and rejects an uncovered read→derive→write path, hidden SQL, a blocking shared-lock call, or a token missing any read dependency. Before bootstrap, generation is the fixed `prebootstrap` token and only the advisory lock exists, so M0V can deploy safely. Targeted two-session barrier tests hold the exclusive key in session A while session B exercises the boundary and every `BEFORE` trigger operation; B must return exact `55P03` without waiting beyond two seconds or touching a row, then succeed after A releases. The same suite covers every manifest caller plus the known claims action and business-model merge patterns: migration between read/write, row-only drift, external-call delay, retry, worker replay, and queued transactions must either recompute from the new generation or commit the still-current decision—never stale state.

  For every later schema release, the guarded after-manifest enumerates writable relations; `apply_verify` installs/verifies all four triggers inside the candidate transaction, and missing/changed/unmanifested topology rolls back. Migration opens one dedicated non-pooled `SERIALIZABLE` transaction with database-enforced absolute four-minute `transaction_timeout` plus shorter statement, lock, and idle-in-transaction timeouts, acquires the same transaction-scoped exclusive advisory lock, drains shared work, locks/revalidates the proof head, and atomically records immutable `fence_deadline_at = database_clock + interval '5 minutes'`; the transaction has a four-minute monotonic/statement timeout. Commit or rollback database-atomically releases every relation and advisory lock; a fresh-connection absence proof is required before `applied_pending_external_check` may enter canary work. Runtime boundaries use only transaction-scoped `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock_shared` inside their own transactions, so their shared locks release on commit/rollback and never return to a pool. Until the earlier of explicit inactive transition or database-clock deadline, those boundaries/triggers read the active generation/deadline and return the fixed maintenance error; at/after deadline they permit writes but leave the release `paused_expired`/unverified and all future release/deploy actions blocked. Signed canaries/local checks are capped at ten seconds and explicit release must occur before deadline. Provider-log/global-root polling follows with no fence. Statement/transaction timeout rolls back work still active at four minutes; the database timeout rolls back and releases the exclusive lock even if the client or network is gone. DDL may block reads only during transaction; writes are unavailable at most five minutes. Database-clock/fake-clock barriers plus success, rollback, cancellation, connection loss, and pool-reuse tests prove no advisory lock survives its owning scope, restart/renewal/API/log delay cannot extend the fence, and no failure marks success. External superuser activity remains detectable, not preventable.

  Synthetic tests run concurrent web and worker decision/read/write flows before/during/after migration and migrations 103–107 table fixtures. They prove trigger topology/deltas, exact migration-owner allowlists, runtime denial, snapshot revalidation, crash deadline release, and privileged-race contamination. Each deployed canary has two separately signed fixed actions. `runtime-boundary-select` is the pre-bootstrap M0V/M0X action: through the ordinary helper it issues only `SELECT` and proves no-lock success/exclusive-lock immediate refusal/recovery. `database-trigger-rollback-write` is M0F/later-release only: it bypasses only the helper through one scanner-allowlisted diagnostic, uses the real platform pooler identity, attempts the manifest-pinned reversible INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE sequence on one surface-owned table, and always rolls back; active generation must reach the trigger and refuse within two seconds, while after release all three execute then roll back. Neither accepts caller SQL/key/table/payload/timeout. Tokens bind current deployments, exact approval/run, action, mode, and generation; M0V/M0X cannot request the write action, and M0F cannot request a different relation. Expiry/closeout disables both. No event trigger or generic SQL surface is created.
- **M0S provider-native automatic-build suppression:** after M0I5-C and before D4, M0S changes only `.github/workflows/vercel-build-suppression.yml` and its closure/tests to add `apply-constant-skip|readback-constant-skip|restore-reviewed-prior`. The PR exact-head review and merge happen while cloud trust still pins M0I5; the old SHA is denied the new action. The M0R infrastructure issuer then approves one exact old-SHA→M0S-SHA IAM condition replacement, and the clean Cloud Shell administrator applies/read-backs it before dispatch. A sealed exact-project/field/value/precondition approval permits only compare-and-set of Vercel project `prj_rP6Jlima7iK1paffEPhLqxlswGsC` Ignored Build Step to the documented constant successful skip. Fake/live tests prove rollback, API/audit readback, old-SHA/PR/sibling denial, and refusal of deploy/environment-secret/team/billing/domain/build-source/other-project changes. An inert documentation commit must create only the provider-documented canceled/skipped record, execute zero project build, and leave the production alias and Trigger unchanged. M0S-C records exact workflow/IAM/setting/audit/deployment evidence; absence, drift, broad scope, or any real build blocks D4.
- **M0V0/M0V1/M0V2 setting and deployment sequence:** after M0E-C, M0V0 alone merges CODEOWNED `scripts/vercel-ignore-build.mjs` plus the complete platform-input manifest while M0S constant suppression remains active. Its exact-head review/merge happens before cloud access; the M0R issuer then authorizes one settings-workflow old-SHA→M0V0-SHA IAM transition, and the clean Cloud Shell administrator applies it. The M0V0 workflow compare-and-sets only the Ignored Build Step to the already-merged manifest-aware rule, proves audit/readback and an ignored inert commit, and deploys nothing; M0V0-C records only that setting outcome. After V0-C, M0V1 owns one exact-SHA Vercel deployment and the web no-lock/exclusive-lock/recovery canary; V1-C records it. After V1-C, M0V2 owns one exact-SHA Trigger deployment and the worker canary; V2-C records it. Each implementation gets its own approval, scoped credential, artifact, cleanup, recoverable failure state, and documentation closeout. No milestone may deploy or probe its sibling platform, and no artifact can stand in for another.
- **Optional vector-index cutover:** M0A classifies `.env.example` and `turbo.json` as deployment surfaces. M0E removes `ORACLE_RUN_VECTOR_INDEXES` from both, updates all three active references in `docs/macro-understanding-implementation-plan.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, and every other active operator reference to that environment switch, documents only guarded `--include-optional 99_vector_indexes.sql`, and scans the whole repository for either spelling. Because applied migration files are immutable, the exact current comment lines in immutable migrations `48_taxonomy_vector_indexes.sql` and `99_vector_indexes.sql` are the only vector-switch source-hash-and-line-pinned historical exceptions; the adjacent active README states that comment is obsolete and points to the guarded command. Tests prove the old environment variable cannot activate or appear in runtime/config/workflow inputs, neither deprecated comment can expand or move, an ineligible optional migration stays `deferred`, and eligible explicit activation either records `applied` proof or exits non-zero—never reports false success.
- **Bounded M0E export policy:** M0D adds reviewed `config/m0-snapshot-export-policy.json`, bound by checksum into the approval and artifact. It defaults to schema-only for approved application schemas, rejects every unlisted schema/table/column, and always excludes `auth`, `storage`, `vault`, `realtime`, provider/platform internals, identity/session/token/secret tables, message/document/claim bodies, uploaded bytes, and credentials. `pg_dump --schema-only` writes one encrypted schema stream; data is never taken by unrestricted `pg_dump`. Any strictly required data fixture is an individually approved table/query entry with declared data class, projected columns, deterministic typed redaction expressions, stable key order, per-table row/byte/time ceilings, and aggregate limits of 100,000 rows, 256 MiB encrypted, and 15 minutes; it streams through `COPY (SELECT ...)` into the encrypted bundle. The runner validates the schema dump TOC plus every data-bundle entry before restore and after restore proves no excluded relation/column/content or excess count/bytes exists. A migration needing excluded real content blocks for a separate plan and approval; synthetic fixtures cover it by default. Tests inject excluded schemas/tables/columns, unsafe TOC commands, unmasked values, unknown classes, row/byte/runtime overflow, truncation, and approval/policy checksum drift, all before a success artifact.
- **Real-production timing gates:** filtered snapshot size is not accepted as evidence. In M0E's approved read-only rehearsal, the isolated migration runner opens three fresh `REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` snapshots and executes the complete manifest root over every included production row/object/large-object byte entirely inside PostgreSQL, with no export, writer fence, DDL, DML, or control row. Each records only timing/count/byte/root/plan/load summaries. Every table must finish within 20 seconds and worst-case root total at most three minutes, leaving one minute inside the four-minute transaction cap. The same rehearsal runs three end-to-end no-write shadow sequences that measure approval recheck, local proof, concurrent signed web/worker canary round trip, fence-release bookkeeping simulation, and real Supabase log-watermark advancement; each must project writer-fence work at or below four minutes 30 seconds and total orchestration at or below 20 minutes, leaving 30 seconds fence and five minutes orchestration headroom. The real M0F fence is still enforced by database deadlines, not this estimate. Any timeout, auth/egress failure, stale watermark, catalog/load drift, or slower run blocks M0E-C/M0V/M0X/M0F and requires reviewed redesign rather than sampling or raising limits. The M0E artifact binds both database-root and end-to-end timing envelopes; `verify:m0e:live-proof` rechecks production size and provider-ingestion bounds before activation.
- **Filtered-restore dependency bootstrap:** M0D adds reviewed `config/m0-restore-dependencies.json`, checksum-bound into the export policy/artifact. A read-only `pg_depend`/catalog preflight closes every dependency of included application objects and requires each to resolve to an included object or an explicit local-only dependency recipe. Before restore, the pinned container installs exact approved extension/version pairs—including `vector` and `pgcrypto`—and creates data-free compatibility objects such as `auth.uid()` with the exact signature, volatility, security, search path, and JWT-setting behavior required by Oracle policies; it creates no platform tables, users, sessions, tokens, or copied platform code/data. Unknown extensions, routines, schemas, operators, casts, languages, or transitive dependencies block capture. After restore, a completeness check resolves every `pg_depend` edge, compiles/views every routine and policy, exercises vector columns/operators/index definitions, and runs RLS allow/deny smoke tests under local shadow roles with synthetic JWT claims. Fixtures prove the filtered real-shape TOC restores successfully and that missing/wrong-version extensions, absent or incorrect `auth.uid()`, hidden transitive dependencies, unsafe shim attributes, or any attempt to import excluded platform objects fail before rehearsal success.
- **Independent production-approval trust contract and ownership:** M0D3 adds the approval schema/trust/revocation files, verifier/preparation/renewal helpers, and component suite; its sole direct gate is `m0-authorization.ts`. M0W creates the disabled `production-approval.yml` identity probe, M0I3 transitions it to credential probe, receives the external exact-SHA IAM transition, and proves only its reviewer identity boundary, and M0E transitions that governed file to the complete review/validation/attestation state machine. Top-level permissions are empty: `review` gets only contents/actions read plus OIDC, `validate-envelope` only contents/actions read, and `attest` contents/actions read plus OIDC/attestations write; all actions are commit-pinned. The review job checks out the exact merged target read-only and creates canonical schema-v1 `PRODUCTION_APPROVAL_REQUEST.json` with every authorization field and no verdict. It also creates canonical `PRODUCTION_APPROVAL_BRIEF.json` whose instruction explicitly says: decide whether to authorize this exact request; return APPROVE only if safe; bind and restate every named request field and both request/brief digests; any omission, addition, ambiguity, or generic repository-readiness verdict is REJECT. It invokes the pinned M0Q interface exactly as `ai-codex-review final-check --base <merged-parent> --assert-head <merged-sha> --sealed-brief PRODUCTION_APPROVAL_BRIEF.json --untrusted-snapshot-root`; omission of either production-required option fails. The wrapper injects the brief into the top-level reviewer prompt, runs from the neutral root, and seals both files; snapshot inclusion alone is insufficient. The workflow accepts only an atomic report with explicit APPROVE and exact field-for-field equality for run ID, authorization kind, repository, target/fingerprint, SHA/tree, ordered actions, resource/manifest/file/allowlist/expected-delta digests, data classes/destination when applicable, identity, issued/start-before/lease times, request digest, and brief digest. A separate fresh attestation job revalidates those equalities and signs the canonical envelope subject. Neither executor nor migration identity can attest; reviewer/attestation cannot impersonate migration. The verifier requires valid GitHub/Sigstore bundle, subject/issuer/repository, governed workflow identity/ref/checksum, run/attempt, reviewer mode/model/report/source/brief digests and explicit authorization, schema, scope, permissions, time, and current-main revocation before production OIDC. Fake provider/API, tokenless workflow, STS, and Secret Manager tests reject generic APPROVE, reference-without-authorization, omitted/extra/mismatched fields, isolation omission, permission drift, unpinned actions, executor verdicts, substituted MACs, edited bundles, cancellation/revocation, trust drift, and forged rotation. GitHub Environment names, Markdown reviews, local signatures, and database proofs are never approval.
- **Reproducible reviewer job and real Codex authentication path:** the production-approval workflow's `review` job runs only from the merged default-branch workflow on a separate fresh GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` VM. It verifies the Codex CLI version pinned in `production-approval-trust.json`, downloads the public `u2giants/ai-devops` recovery toolkit at its exact commit/archive SHA-256, installs it job-locally read-only, runs `ai-codex-review doctor`, and asserts the supported mode/model/reasoning/sandbox header. After runner/source lockdown it creates a new mode-0700 temporary `CODEX_HOME` outside the checkout/cache/artifact paths, uses OIDC to read only `oracle-production-reviewer-api-token` into a protected pipe, and feeds that pipe solely to a separate `CODEX_HOME=<temp> codex login --with-api-key` process; review-prompt stdin is not shared with login. It requires `CODEX_HOME=<temp> codex login status` success, then runs one bounded read-only qualification using the exact pinned `gpt-5.6-sol`/medium/sandbox command and a fixed nonce prompt whose only accepted output is a fixed nonsecret token. Only after qualification does `ai-codex-review final-check` run with the same isolated `CODEX_HOME`; no guessed credential argument or job-level token environment exists. In `finally`, the job terminates reviewer children, runs isolated `codex logout` where supported, deletes the temporary home/pipe, and scans process args/environment, checkout, cache, artifacts, stdout/stderr, and runner temp for token sentinels or auth files; any qualification, cleanup, or scan failure prevents an approval artifact. The job receives no database, migration proof, KMS, migration service account, migration Secret Manager resource, or attestation authority. Pull-request and sibling workflow identities fail the external CEL/IAM boundary before secret access. M0E owns this auth integration because M0W's approval path remains probe-only; tests use a fake Codex executable to assert the exact `login --with-api-key` stdin boundary, isolated home, status, qualification, review, logout, cleanup, and no stdin collision, then a separately gated live M0E qualification proves the exact pinned CLI/token/model before the first real approval. If the pinned CLI lacks this observed login contract, M0E blocks and must update ai-devops/tooling in a separate reviewed prerequisite rather than exposing the token another way. The downstream attestation job runs on a different fresh VM with no model or production secret, downloads only checksummed request/report artifacts, reruns schema/digest/verdict checks, and attests.
- **M0E deployment and canary authority transition:** M0E changes the already protected deployment workflow from credential-probe-only to exact `deploy-vercel|deploy-trigger|read-deployments` actions and changes migration workflow from credential-probe/rehearsal-only to also accept `writer-boundary-canary`; all remain unreachable until sealed action-specific approval. Deployment checks out only the attested merged SHA, verifies a clean tree/closure, reads scoped provider tokens through pipes, runs repository-pinned Vercel/Trigger deploy commands for exact projects, reads immutable deployment/image/source SHAs, and destroys credentials; it cannot connect to the database or invoke migration. Provider probes deny env/secret/team/project mutation, another project, arbitrary source, rollback, or unapproved deploy. `config/production-canary-authority.json` pins the public half of M0I's asymmetric key and canonical token fields: action (`runtime-boundary-select|database-trigger-rollback-write`), exact deployment SHA/IDs, approval/run ID, fence generation, random nonce, issued/expiry, and fixed mode `no-lock|exclusive-lock`; no SQL/table/key/payload is caller-selectable, and milestone/action rules make the write action impossible before M0F. Only migration workflow can call asymmetricSign. The web canary verifies signature/key/expiry/action/deployment offline, runs the matching fixed local action, and dispatches the fixed worker canary through the existing server-side Trigger client with the identical token; worker verifies independently before the matching action. Responses contain only status/code/timing/run IDs. Tokens expire within five minutes. Fake/wrong/expired tokens, forbidden milestone/action pair, replay flood, direct Trigger invocation, or general admin session fail. Thus M0V/M0X deploy only through deployment workflow, while M0V/M0X/M0F canary only through migration/KMS; workstation/reviewer receive neither.
- **Production-read gate for M0E:** the rehearsal's capture/export is a production action even though it writes nothing. Before fetching credentials or connecting, run `ai-task-gates check --before production` and obtain the GitHub-attested independent-reviewer `APPROVE` defined above, bound to the exact merged M0E SHA, project `eqccjfbyrywsqkxxpjvg`, read/export action, maximum data classes, fixed encrypted destination, and 24-hour cleanup deadline. `run-m0-rehearsal.ps1` invokes `production-approval-verifier.ts` on that protected artifact and its one-hour start window before secret retrieval; missing, stale, wrong-SHA, wrong-target, non-APPROVE, or broader-scope artifacts fail before connection. The trust-contract forgery suite plus scope-specific fake-artifact tests cover every refusal. A disabled write flag is not treated as production authorization.
- **M0E rehearsal artifact contract:** the ephemeral GitHub-hosted production workflow uploads artifact `m0e-rehearsal-<merged-m0e-sha>` with 30-day retention and sole member `evidence/m0e.json`. Canonical JSON schema v1 contains run/workflow IDs, exact merged SHA, target fingerprint, approved data-class set, runner policy/release/`ImageOS`/`ImageVersion`, isolation/tool digests, before/after manifest hashes, three full-root timing summaries and tested row/byte envelope, start/end UTC, provider-log watermark range, suite result/digests, cleanup attestation, read-approval digest, Cloud KMS MAC/key-version metadata, and no secrets or row content. The protected execution job calls `macSign` but never receives key bytes; a separate credential-free attestation job signs the JSON subject digest through GitHub OIDC. Pre-merge `pnpm verify:m0x` is strictly secretless: by run ID it downloads the M0E and historical M0V artifacts/attestations, runs GitHub/Sigstore public verification, and requires exact artifact/name/member/schema, GitHub run conclusion/commit, subject digest, current M0E SHA, M0V source/closure identity, approval binding, pinned runner-image identity, timing/deployment/closure all-pass results, cleanup, age, and trust/workflow identity; it cannot and does not call production KMS. After M0X merges, its isolated deployment step produces the exact-M0X `m0x-deployment` artifact defined below. The later M0F `migration-production.yml` job obtains its short-lived workflow-bound GCP identity, calls KMS `macVerify`, and verifies M0E, historical M0V, and current M0X-deployment public attestations **before** reading either Secret Manager value or opening a database connection. Tests prove no production OIDC/resource access reaches PR CI or sibling jobs, public verification rejects forged/stale/rerun/duplicate/mutable artifacts, and protected verification rejects a publicly attested artifact with wrong/missing MAC/key version. Neither M0X nor M0F can accept prose or a local file as proof.

- **Named production-enable control:** M0D adds `config/database-targets.schema.json` and `config/database-targets.json` with required object `profiles.production.migration = { "enabled": false, "m0eArtifactRunId": null, "m0eArtifactSha256": null }`; the schema requires a literal boolean and null-or-bounded strings, rejects unknown keys, and production remains false through M0E. `packages/db/src/database-target-guard.ts` exports `assertProductionMigrationEnabled(config, exactMergedSha)` and is the sole constructor of a production write capability. It resolves the repository-root file by module-relative canonical path, rejects symlink/reparse/substitution, validates schema, requires current exact merged SHA and verified M0E run/digest, and runs before secret retrieval, connection, lock, or write; environment variables, CLI flags, working-directory files, and imported objects cannot override it. M0X0's reviewed diff may change only `enabled: false -> true`, the two null evidence fields to the exact current M0E run ID/digest, and its STATUS/evidence prose; a handoff may appear only if independently legal under the capacity rule. `verify:m0x0` compares the diff structurally, fetches/verifies that artifact, and tests missing file/key, wrong type, unknown key, stale/mismatched artifact/SHA, alternate path, env/CLI override, symlink race, and any extra diff all fail before credentials. Rollback is a new reviewed PR setting `enabled:false`; no runtime toggle exists.
- **M0X0/M0X1/M0X2 enable and re-proof sequence:** M0X0 owns only the tiny activation PR and secretless candidate/merged-tree verification; it performs no deployment, canary, or database connection. M0X0-C proves that merge was ignored and both M0V deployments stayed current. M0X1 then obtains one Vercel-only approval, deploys the exact M0X0 SHA through the protected workflow, and reruns only the enabled web boundary canary; M0X1-C records that artifact. M0X2 then obtains one Trigger-only approval, deploys the same exact source through the protected workflow, and reruns only the enabled worker boundary canary; M0X2-C records the combined current deployment set and no drift. Each live pair has its own workflow run, scoped credential, approval, cleanup, and recoverable failure state; it cannot deploy or probe the sibling platform. M0F waits for X2-C and verifies both independently closed artifacts before database access.

- **M0F production-write authorization:** M0F starts only from merged M0X2-C and owns only the production database-write outcome; it makes no repository, Vercel, Trigger, or deployment change. Before authority or credentials it rechecks current main, the independently closed M0X1 Vercel and M0X2 Trigger deployments, both bundle/closure digests, X0-C ignored-build proof, and the M0E timing envelope. Drift routes to the one affected M0X1- or M0X2-shaped deployment milestone; M0F cannot repair it. Its distinct APPROVE binds current X2-C tree, both deployment artifacts, target, ordered actions (`baseline`, `bootstrap`, `migrate`, `fenced_canary_release`, `postcommit_verify`, `idempotency`), bootstrap/manifest/file digests, allowlists, migration identity, one-hour start expiry, **25-minute orchestration lease, immutable five-minute writer-fence deadline, four-minute transaction cap, and ten-minute external-log cap**, and one run ID. Before every phase the verifier rechecks main/deployments/digests/registry/cancellation/time. Bootstrap/migrate install only the six control tables, exact proof/authorization state, owner functions, and four-trigger topology; no unlisted business/role/grant/seed effect is allowed. The transaction commits only as backward-compatible `applied_pending_external_check`. `fenced_canary_release` immediately signs and invokes the fixed web/worker rollback canaries concurrently, requires exact trigger refusal/no-row within ten seconds, performs local catalog/control proof, marks inactive, and releases before the database-clock five-minute deadline. `postcommit_verify` then runs global-root/provider-log checks without a fence; failure marks contaminated and blocks idempotency/future release but does not extend outage. Idempotency invokes the identical signed canaries after release and requires real web/worker INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE to execute then roll back before final `consumed`. Fresh/upgrade/rollback/trigger/privilege/crash/drift/auth/timeout tests prove complete install or rollback, exact signed canary callers, automatic deadline release, and no false success. Any missing/stale artifact or unexpected in-transaction delta rolls back that transaction; interruption yields a resumable/contaminated state, never a claim that the writer fence exceeded five minutes. M0F approval is distinct from both M0X deployments and M0E read/export approval and authorizes only this database run.
- **Durable authorization state and ordering:** bootstrap DDL creates admin-only authorization/action tables binding unique artifact/run IDs, exact SHA/target/identity/allowlist/manifest/deployment/timing digests, start/orchestration/fence bounds, state, ordinal, timestamps, and failure/proof/revocation metadata. Bootstrap ordinals are `baseline=0`, `bootstrap=1`, `migrate=2`, `fenced_canary_release=3`, `postcommit_verify=4`, `idempotency=5`; later releases use `preflight=0`, `apply_verify=1`, `fenced_canary_release=2`, `postcommit_verify=3`, `idempotency=4`. The exclusive advisory lock is transaction-scoped and acquired inside the dedicated non-pooled `SERIALIZABLE` write transaction after database-enforced absolute `transaction_timeout` and shorter statement/lock/idle timeouts are set; commit, rollback, statement timeout, idle-in-transaction timeout, connection loss, or backend termination releases it atomically, and fresh-connection absence proof is required before canary/external phases. The durable generation/deadline row supplies the short post-commit fence after that release. Every action revalidates its predecessor/appends proof, and only clean external postcheck permits final consumption. Before each action/resume the wrapper verifies no-cache registry/cancellation/trust. Approval authorizes only the four-minute transaction and immutable five-minute fence; no renewal extends either. Revocation before cutoff rolls back, while later revocation blocks subsequent action/deployment. API outage, cancellation, trust drift, expiry, ordinal/deadline mismatch prevents writes. Kill/barrier/fake-clock and connection-reuse tests require consumed, safely paused/contaminated with database-enforced lock release and writes restored by deadline, or no-write stop—never a pooled lock or false availability.

- **Authorization for every later schema release:** M0F's enable flag is only a kill switch, never blanket permission. M0D ships/tests `prepare-production-release.ts`; after M0F it builds one request per exact merged schema-release SHA with reviewed files/checksums/target/identity/deltas/trigger manifest/allowlist. Steps 1A and each `103`–`107` release obtain unique APPROVE/run IDs and advance `preflight -> apply_verify -> fenced_canary_release -> postcommit_verify -> idempotency`. No approval covers multiple SHAs/future migrations. `apply_verify` executes candidate SQL, installs/verifies trigger topology, captures locked post-state/deltas, checks the in-boundary allowlist, appends proof, and commits one backward-compatible transaction as `applied_pending_external_check`; unexpected/missing effects roll back. `fenced_canary_release` runs the exact signed canaries plus local catalog/control proof and must release before the immutable five-minute fence deadline. Only then does unfenced `postcommit_verify` run mandatory global-root/provider-log contamination checks and advance to `verified|contaminated`; it never extends downtime or substitutes for the in-transaction allowlist. Idempotency uses the same bounded proof rule. Synthetic 103–107 tests prove triggers, canary authentication, deadline release, and rollback; wrong approvals/topology/relations/deltas fail, competing release stops before write, and external privileged races contaminate post-commit without prolonging the fence. Each schema step records its approval/workflow/authorization IDs.
- **Expiry, renewal, and recovery:** an unclaimed approval expires at `start_before` with zero writes. A claimed run gets a 25-minute orchestration lease, but its immutable database-clock fence deadline remains five minutes and transaction cap four minutes; renewal can extend only unfenced external verification/cleanup, never the transaction, advisory lock, or writer fence. The database's absolute `transaction_timeout` kills/rolls back the active transaction and records the last committed ordinal. A crash before commit leaves no effect; after compatible commit it becomes `paused_expired`, automatically restores writes by the original fence deadline, blocks competing/future release, and resumes only after a sealed renewal for the same run/SHA/target/scope/digests/proof head/next ordinal. `renew-production-authorization.ts` advances only orchestration lease. Sealed abort is terminal only when proofs show no unverified partial application; otherwise reviewed reconciliation is required. Fake-clock/barrier tests cross start, four-minute transaction, five-minute fence, 25-minute orchestration, renewal/abort/revocation races, and prove consumed, safely paused/contaminated with writes restored, or no-write refusal.

**M0W/M0G/M0I1–M0I5 five-identity transition contract:** M0W merges migration, approval, deployment, and Vercel-settings default-branch paths before cloud trust; M0G adds the identity-only transfer root and externally binds all five identities to the exact M0G SHA. No later workflow can rely on an in-workflow SHA check. M0I1 changes, externally rebinds, and proves only transfer; M0I2 only migration/KMS; M0I3 only reviewer; M0I4 only deployment; M0I5 only settings. Each implementation is followed by its own documentation-only `-C` proof before the next begins. The M0R infrastructure-approval issuer binds each one-workflow old-SHA→new-SHA IAM delta, and the clean Google-hosted administrator applies it before the new workflow requests cloud credentials. Tests prove the old SHA and every sibling/PR/fork/ref fail at STS, the exact new SHA receives only its resource scope, and no workload can change IAM. Only I5-C unlocks M0U; M0S, D4, and M0E remain blocked until successful M0U-C and M0S-C as applicable.

**Natural cuts:** stop after every implementation row and again after its `-C` closeout. This includes M0I1/I1-C, I2/I2-C, I3/I3-C, I4/I4-C, I5/I5-C; M0V0/V0-C, M0V1/V1-C, and M0V2/V2-C; and M0X0/X0-C, X1/X1-C, and X2/X2-C. M0I1 owns only the journaled six-row transfer; I2 only migration/KMS; I3 only reviewer; I4 only deployment credentials; I5 only settings credentials. M0V0 owns only the manifest-aware setting transition, M0V1 only Vercel/web, and M0V2 only Trigger/worker. M0X0 owns only the enable manifest, M0X1 only enabled Vercel/web, and M0X2 only enabled Trigger/worker. Every pair starts from current main in a new worktree, gets a fresh exact-head approval and—where IAM changes—a fresh one-workflow infrastructure approval, reaches one recoverable live outcome, then returns control. All other previously named M0 cuts remain unchanged. Do not bundle a milestone or closeout with another, Step 1A Jev schema, or a vendor call.

**Mandatory post-merge closeouts:** every live implementation has its own immediately following documentation-only closeout. The list includes M0R/R-C, M0G/G-C, M0I1/I1-C through M0I5/I5-C, M0U/U-C, M0S/S-C, M0E/E-C, M0V0/V0-C, M0V1/V1-C, M0V2/V2-C, M0X0/X0-C, M0X1/X1-C, M0X2/X2-C, and M0F/F-C. An implementation enters `awaiting_live_proof` when its PR merges or sealed no-diff action starts; it is never marked complete in advance. Its owning action session waits through completion, emits only the defined content-free artifact and signed issue #14 result, and stops. A fresh current-main closeout session independently verifies that one artifact and live read-only state, updates only its STATUS/evidence row, exact-head reviews, and merges. It cannot change code/config/workflow/cloud/database state or combine with the next milestone. Only that `-C` merge unlocks the next row; M0F-C is the durable M0 release record.
