**Exact-candidate enabled-route rehearsal:** M0X adds no runtime code, but its secretless `pnpm verify:m0x:candidate -- --assert-head <sha>` must archive the exact committed candidate tree into a new temporary directory, install from the lockfile, inject fake GitHub attestation/OIDC/Secret Manager/KMS/audit adapters plus a disposable local PostgreSQL target, and dispatch the real `activate` workflow/orchestrator path with the candidate's `enabled:true` and exact M0E evidence fields. It must complete the full baseline/bootstrap/migrate/postcommit_verify/idempotency state machine against only synthetic data and prove the real guard, artifact verification, action ordering, manifests, allowlists, proof chain, cleanup, and retry behavior—not a test-only alternate route. It denies all network endpoints and production project refs, so any production credential/OIDC exchange/connection attempt fails the rehearsal. After squash merge, the same command runs again from a fresh archive of the exact merged M0X SHA and emits a content-free tree/test digest; a candidate-only pass is insufficient because the merge SHA/tree may differ. Production gate, independent approval, deployment, OIDC, secret reads, connection, and production SQL remain forbidden until that merged-tree rehearsal passes. Fixtures alter the candidate/merged tree, config fields, artifact, injected authority, target, action order, and cleanup and require refusal.

**Reviewer-token pre-execution confinement:** M0G's desired-state GCP file defines provider `theoracle-production-approval`, service account `oracle-production-reviewer@vertex-ai-497120.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, and Secret Manager resource `oracle-production-reviewer-api-token`, initially bound to the exact M0G SHA. It reads only that secret and has no KMS/database/deployment/migration authority. M0I1 delivers the token as one journaled row but cannot read it; M0I3 alone changes the approval workflow, receives the external exact-SHA IAM transition, runs the fingerprint-only probe without Codex, and M0I3-C records it. M0E later gets another one-workflow approval and external IAM transition before enabling review. PRs, forks, reusable callers, old/unapproved SHAs, other refs/events/paths/repositories fail at cloud trust, so a same-path workflow cannot omit a local guard and still obtain the token.

#### Step 0 — Establish vendor, privacy, and test-data boundaries

**Change / action**

- Record the owner decisions from §8 on issue #14.
- Complete the TypeSafe contract/DPA/security review before any Oracle company data is sent.
- Create the field schema for a 1Password item in vault `vibe_coding` named `TypeSafe AI - The Oracle`: concealed `vercel_production_api_key` and `trigger_prod_api_key`, their provider-issued immutable key IDs, and issuance-evidence references. Step 0 puts no guessed or manually copied key/writer/lifecycle value there; only Step 2K's fresh hosted provisioning workflow may populate them and the separate writer/lifecycle items. The evidence must bind each key fingerprint/ID to the same exact organization/account and vendor posture; notes also identify the approved data-class set and retention mode. A note or operator assertion alone is not ownership evidence. TypeSafe must provide either an authenticated no-content key-introspection endpoint returning immutable key ID plus organization/account, or a signed issuance record whose signature/key and fingerprint binding Oracle can verify offline; if neither exists, production delivery and every live pilot block pending a provider-supported account proxy/attestation. Never reuse one target's key in the other target or put either value in chat, shell arguments, logs, screenshots, or Git.
- Until approval, use only repository-owned synthetic/public fixtures. `public_synthetic` is granted only by an exact code-owned fixture builder and expected fixture hash; it can never come from a caller flag, origin label, request text, or semantic inspection. Any altered fixture, appended context, or caller-supplied lookalike is classified through its real provenance and gains `unknown_restricted`. Do not assume an existing `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is approved for company data.
- Record an explicit allow/deny matrix for `public_synthetic`, `internal_company`, `employee_communication`, `licensed_content`, `personnel_hr`, `customer_personal`, and `unknown_restricted`. Approval of one class never implies another; mixed input is allowed only when every derived class is allowed. `unknown_restricted` is denied by default.
- Record the intended matrix/evidence on issue #14 in Step 0. After Step 1 creates the schema and owner-only approval control and at least one target environment has its protected target-specific API key, Albert must authenticate through his exact pre-enrolled Microsoft identity and approve the canonical data-class/vendor-posture plus ordered target/fingerprint-map digest in the Decisions UI; that immutable database approval, not the issue text, is consumed by the guarded post-secret transaction that materializes `v1-public-synthetic`. The migration seeds no active policy, owner approval, or credential fingerprint. Adding the second target, rotating either key, or allowing company data requires a new exact owner approval bound to the complete vendor evidence, class set, and ordered target map. Until a policy contains the current runtime target, every call on that target is blocked; issue/1Password notes are evidence references, not the enforcement source.
- Record the trusted provenance mappings used by `data-classification.ts`; do not rely on semantic guessing or on a caller-provided downgrade. Where a document/source lacks durable classification metadata, leave it `unknown_restricted` until Step 1's reviewed admin classification flow writes an explicit value.
- Record the recommended initial spend cap ($5) on issue #14, but treat it only as a proposal. Step 1B materializes an epoch only after Albert authenticates through the exact pre-enrolled owner identity and approves the canonical integer-microusd cap in the Decisions UI; no active epoch or owner approval is seeded by migration and no TypeSafe call can occur without one.

**Dependencies / parallelism**

- Synthetic client/harness work in Step 1 may proceed before vendor approval.
- Every Oracle-data pilot waits for the contract/DPA and credential note.

**Verification gate**

- Issue #14 contains the dated approval or explicit synthetic-only limitation.
- The 1Password item exists by title and field presence; no value appears in terminal output.
- Tests prove every unapproved member of the derived class set, mixed-class input, unknown provenance, and a falsified caller downgrade are refused before any network call.

**Natural cut:** end the session here if the owner/vendor decision is not complete. Do not pretend synthetic success authorizes company data.

#### Step 1A–1E — Add schema/client, cut over runtime DB roles, then prove physical source erasure

**Files/functions**

- For every application table, column, enum, ordinary index, and relationship introduced in Steps 1A or 8A–8B, `packages/db/src/schema.ts` is the source of truth and a generated Drizzle migration carries the representable DDL first. Reserved raw migrations `103`–`107` may contain only data reconciliation/backfill, triggers, owner/security-definer functions, RLS/grants, and partial/expression/constraint details that Drizzle cannot represent. Clean-install, generated-schema, guarded snapshot, and post-migration drift checks must agree; a raw-only application object or a generated/raw duplicate fails before merge.
- `packages/ai/package.json`: add pinned compatible `@typesafe-ai/sdk`; update `pnpm-lock.yaml` through pnpm, never by hand.

- New `packages/ai/src/decisions/typesafe-client.ts`: wrap `TypeSafeClient.systemOne`, default to the pinned model, inject the SDK client/fetch for tests, normalize errors, and calculate input cost from the documented price. Instantiate the official SDK with explicit `logLevel:'off'`; never inherit `TYPESAFE_LOG_LEVEL` or an SDK default because debug logging includes request/response bodies.
- New `packages/ai/src/decisions/question-pack.ts`: require stable pack ID/version, an approved data-class allowlist, expected language, complete question meanings, and a caller-approved total deadline. Build the final serialized SDK request before network access and enforce conservative UTF-8 byte ceilings of 64,000 total and 32,000 for state plus the longest serialized question, plus `MAX_QUESTIONS_PER_CALL=100`, the 255-option Choice limit, and Score levels restricted to the documented inclusive range 2–10. These byte caps intentionally underuse the documented token allowance rather than depend on an unavailable tokenizer. IDs are audit identifiers, not hidden instructions.
- Treat the existing model-capability catalog fetchers under `packages/ai/src/model-capabilities/sources/{anthropic,openai,deepseek,qwen,google,openrouter}.ts` as a separate **source-independent metadata-egress** class, never as an exception to the egress scanner. Move their network boundary behind `packages/ai/src/model-capabilities/catalog-egress.ts`, whose typed request accepts only a closed provider enum plus repository-owned catalog refresh parameters, builds only allowlisted provider catalog URLs/headers, and rejects source anchors, prompts, messages, documents, query text, arbitrary URLs/bodies, or any caller-supplied content. The wrapper records only provider/model capability metadata and cannot invoke inference, embeddings, files, batches, caches, or Jev. The scanner permits network imports only through this wrapper and proves all six existing source modules use it; AST/type fixtures reject a seventh direct fetch/SDK client, arbitrary endpoint, payload-bearing parameter, imported application content, or reuse by an inference path. Captured fake transports assert zero Oracle/source bytes and unchanged catalog results. These calls need no outbound source lease because their request is provably source-independent; any future catalog source that cannot meet that contract must use the normal source-member lease before transport.

Erasing any contributing source never merely removes one evidence row while keeping possibly contaminated derived text: it invalidates and physically deletes the complete affected claim/artifact/message/meeting-derived version, its translations/embedding, and every downstream Brain/relationship/recommendation/map version; only after commit may ordinary pipelines create a new ID/version solely from re-read surviving sources. Approved or operational descendants must first take the versioned pre-105 or post-105 business-resolution transition below. The manifest scanner fails on an unclassified source reader/writer/table/column, nullable-orphan path, or derived artifact without complete lineage.

Before 1E inventory, the Recall webhook must authenticate, size-limit, normalize, and persist through that function, dispatch only `{legacyIngressId}`, and make `teams-live-recall-utterance` load/lease that row; terminal handling clears the payload immediately, 24-hour timeout clears it and fails terminal, the keyed token/status remains for at most 30 days unless source erasure deletes it first, then the row deletes. `apps/web/lib/trigger.ts` logs only task name, returned run ID, and a fresh random non-source correlation ID—not payload or hash—and a whole-repository task-dispatch/logger scan rejects source-bearing payload, locator, hash, or log argument. Thus all new Trigger history and Vercel application logs are ID-only before 1E can pass; legacy full-payload platform copies remain governed by the inventory/expiry proof above.

No free-text reviewer note or raw state is stored. Durable identity includes `use_case`, mode, activation generation, exact `input_hash`, decision/pack/threshold/data-policy versions, requested model, and eventually resolved model. A database partial unique index over **all pre-resolution identity columns** including `input_hash` and activation generation uses PostgreSQL `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` where status is `pending OR succeeded`, so two direct inserts with the same identity cannot bypass uniqueness merely because an allowed identity component is null; failed/discarded rows release it. A second partial `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` index covers the finalized effective-config/resolved-model identity for succeeded rows. Reuse additionally verifies the stored resolved model exactly; an upgrade changes requested model/pack and every activation/reactivation increments generation.

Chat employee/channel use `ON DELETE CASCADE`, not `SET NULL`; checks require chat origin employee/channel/locale/scope fields together and forbid them for incompatible kinds, so no half-nulled chat origin can survive. Store no query text/hash. Every decision and outbound lease links the exact origin anchor/version plus all contributing content-source anchors. MCP expires within 24 hours; synthetic probes are public only. Expiry first starts the ordinary source-erasure freeze/drain/reconciliation saga for that origin anchor and may delete the origin only after its TypeSafe/provider request/result is absent or inaccessible under the approved posture. Deleting/expiring any origin or contributing source invalidates the run; company-data queries without the complete versioned origin/source anchor set block before transport.

Meeting, external-system, and manual-admin inputs must include their source anchor even when no message/document exists. Output rows are permitted only for claim support, have no input ordinal/hash, and encode exactly one truthful effect/target: new-claim insertion or hold targets that new `claim_id` with `inserted_claim|review_hold_claim`; an applied duplicate targets the existing `claim_id` with `duplicate_claim`; a duplicate review that leaves the claim unchanged targets the held `candidate_id` with `review_hold_candidate`. Exactly one output may be inserted solely by the named finalization function in the same transaction that finalizes/applies the effect; fallback/failed/discarded/shadow-only runs cannot add one. All rows are immutable after insert. The exactly-one-target check is paired with one target-specific partial unique index for **every** UUID/text target column over `(decision_run_id,phase,effect,target_column,input_ordinal) NULLS NOT DISTINCT WHERE target_column IS NOT NULL`; bounded eval/entity-query targets get their own equivalent indexes. Thus nullable ordinal/hash and unrelated null target columns cannot admit duplicates. Erasure deletes every source-linked subject/hash before the source anchor and deletes the run when required, leaving no identifying hash. Zero input rows remain allowed only for the exact public-synthetic probe.
  - Do not depend on parent deletion or broad cascades for physical source erasure. Every physical deletion of a message, document, meeting transcript, external-system record copy, or manual-admin evidence enters the durable `oracle_source_erasure_runs` saga; application/runtime/browser/service roles cannot directly delete source parents, storage objects, replicas, anchors, or call its owner functions. The authenticated admin action first inserts the immutable `prepared` run and target link. Stage 1 invokes the exact two-phase lease protocol above: F1 re-resolves/locks the anchor version, commits `freezing` plus reserved-call cancellation and the in-flight snapshot, then releases all locks; the workflow aborts/waits outside a transaction while short finalizers record outcomes; F2 re-locks, proves unchanged version and zero reserved/in-flight/unknown lease, commits `frozen`, then and only then writes the resulting complete database/derived/external manifest plus random comparison key into the temporary sealed request, records its opaque digest, and invalidates/discards linked Jev work so no ingestion/cache/batch/use can restart. Any undrained or `unknown` outbound lease blocks before manifest/approval. Stage 2 reconciles/delete-or-expires every completed TypeSafe, synchronous provider, or embedding request, cancels every active linked OpenAI, Anthropic, and Vertex batch job, waits for terminal state, deletes/verifies every OpenAI file and Vertex GCS object, deletes exact Storage/cachedContent resources, deletes or proves inaccessible expiry of Anthropic job/results and Trigger/Vercel copies, and deletes/verifies every source-derived Recall and Microsoft Teams message. Provider reconciliation must show every exact request/object/file/job/result/message/run/log copy absent or inaccessible; wrong generation/request/file/job/message/run, unknown replica, active call/batch, API outage, partial deletion, unverifiable history, or implicit cache blocks with the source frozen. Only after all live replicas are `external_verified` does `finalize_oracle_source_erasure(run_id)` read the request in owner memory, re-lock/recheck anchor version and both dependency digests, apply the current business-resolution contract, delete/redact linked `job_runs`, delete workflow/generic lineage, physically delete every affected claim version plus translation/embedding, every Oracle-side employee/Teams assistant message derived from it, source-bearing meeting participant/transcript-URL/AI-summary rows, and every downstream Brain/relationship/recommendation/map/model/cache version, then delete other joins/tags/attachments, evidence/candidates, chunks/outlines/groups, the exact source copy/anchor, and remaining Jev subjects in one transaction. That same transaction scrubs temporary requester/anchor/version/reason fields plus inventory, deletes lineage/source mappings, and ends in `database_deleted_request_retained`; it cannot call Secret Manager. After commit, a fresh read-only connection verifies the exact release/proof checkpoint and zero closure, waits until the recorded WAL position is flushed, reads the database server clock, and atomically records that conservative post-commit time plus content-free commit/release digests as `database_commit_attested`. Only then may the workflow destroy the exact Secret Manager version, independently read back `DESTROYED`, record `request_destroyed_at`, and advance to `live_deleted_backup_pending`; this is still not final success. A crash before database commit leaves `external_verified` and retries the whole transaction; after commit/before attestation it resumes only the zero-closure/commit checkpoint; after attestation/before destruction it resumes only exact-version destruction; after destruction/before state update it proves that version destroyed and advances without recreating it. An ambiguous destruction response polls the exact version and otherwise remains blocked—never rewrites the request or database. The workflow then proves through the Supabase management API that the earliest restorable PITR point is later than `database_commit_attested_at` and the complete retained scheduled-backup inventory contains no backup with snapshot/capture start at or before that cutoff and no ambiguous capture metadata. Only after that proof and the restoration-safe tombstone contract below may it mark `verified`. No old value may be copied into a replacement; surviving evidence is re-read only in a later normal pipeline run. The named closure includes `extraction_validation_results`, message/document/meeting/external/manual domain/entity joins, nullable contradiction/gap/intervention pointers, **every `job_runs` JSON/error writer**, **every assistant/meeting/source-derived text/vector/JSON writer**, and **every workflow-map field/downstream FK**. Change only source-independent historical FKs explicitly listed by the manifest to `SET NULL`; core source/evidence FKs stay `NO ACTION`. Retry resumes only the recorded durable checkpoint; already-missing external resources succeed only with prior inventory identity proof. Fault injection covers before/after database commit, post-commit observation, WAL flush proof, commit-attestation write, secret destroy request/read-back, and pending-state write, with process/network loss at each edge. Deleting a chat employee or channel creates a durable fan-out coordinator as specified in the test contract below; it never cascades or directly deletes source rows. A `messages` trigger handles logical deletion by freezing/incrementing its anchor and performing decision/derived/map/job/batch/platform-copy invalidation; clearing it never revives discarded/deleted state.

Every corresponding subject FK uses `ON DELETE CASCADE` only as a final cleanup backstop after this trigger; core application/evidence FKs keep their separately stated behavior. They can never activate, widen, classify, raise spend, inspect source locators, or invoke erasure. Every new owner, human-control, runtime-transition, maintenance, erasure, and trigger function is owned by `oracle_jev_owner`, declared `SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = pg_catalog`, and schema-qualifies every Oracle relation/function/type/sequence/extension (`public.*`, `extensions.*`); dynamic identifier SQL is forbidden. Immediately after creation, revoke every exact signature from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`, `oracle_web_runtime`, and `oracle_worker_runtime`, then grant only the exact manifest role. Trigger functions receive no caller grant; human RPCs grant only `authenticated`; runtime functions grant only their named group; overloads are forbidden unless every exact signature is independently revoked/granted/tested. Route-level auth remains required for shared web-login operations; the plan does not claim per-user DB identity for those direct calls or resistance to malicious schema-owner use.

It uses pinned libpq's `PQencryptPasswordConn` against a workflow-owned loopback helper connection only to derive the SCRAM verifier, then writes that verifier through a protected anonymous pipe to the fixed workflow executor and zeroes plaintext buffers before every exit. The migration executor independently requires TLS, a direct/session connection, `server_version_num>=170000`, `password_encryption='scram-sha-256'`, the expected project/database/migration identity, an unconsumed exact authorization, and the exact registered target OID/attributes. It rechecks that the target is one of the two literal versioned web/worker LOGIN roles and has no elevated attribute or unexpected membership, validates the verifier grammar, and executes exactly one compiled statement branch, `ALTER ROLE <literal allowlisted role> PASSWORD '<validated SCRAM verifier>'`; no caller-supplied identifier or other SQL is accepted. Immediately afterward it proves the target's catalog attributes/memberships are otherwise byte-identical, new login succeeds, old login fails, the concealed platform secret alias moves atomically, both target deployments identify as the staged login, and rollback can restore the prior secret/login before the drained old role is disabled. Tests use a unique sentinel and scan client output plus accessible PostgreSQL statement, audit, activity, and forced-error logs to prove plaintext never reaches the server or logs. Authority/closure tests prove no app/workflow identity other than the guarded migration runner can issue role DDL; the rotation action rejects owner/migration/group/unrelated/spoofed-prefix/unregistered/elevated/stale/disabled targets, every `GRANT|REVOKE|SET ROLE|SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION|CREATE ROLE|DROP ROLE`, role rename/drop/create, membership/attribute change, a second statement, authorization reuse, and a changed migration workflow. The executor's emitted SQL AST/digest must match one of the two reviewed password-only branches. `oracle_jev_owner` and both runtime roles remain NOCREATEROLE, no role receives `ADMIN OPTION` for rotation, and the migration credential never enters either platform or helper.
- Step 1B may land the explicit clients and tests unused, migrate operational scripts, and retain only the scanner's hash-pinned legacy web/worker baseline. Step 1C first provisions `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` with the worker login while deployed workers still use `DIRECT_URL`, then refactors every worker caller and flips the scanner to `worker-strict` in one CI-green commit, deploys/proves its identity and full manifest regression, and only then removes Trigger `DIRECT_URL`. Step 1D separately refactors every web caller, removes both legacy exports, flips the scanner to `strict`, proves required `DATABASE_URL` with no fallback, switches it to the web login, redeploys/proves identity and full manifest regression, and only then removes Vercel `DIRECT_URL`. A missing target URL fails startup/probe; it never selects migration credentials. No phase may require code that a later phase is scheduled to create.
- `DECISIONS.md`: record the dated decision that Jev is a decision primitive, not a generative provider.
- `docs/architecture.md`: add the separate decision path beside `OracleAIClient` and state what may never call it.

**Required behavior**

- Empty questions, duplicate IDs, unsupported question types, invalid criteria, over-cardinality Choice, oversized state, disallowed data class, and absent key fail before network access.
- More than 100 questions, total serialized UTF-8 payload over 64,000 bytes, or state plus longest serialized question over 32,000 bytes fails before network access. Count JSON structure, IDs, criteria, options, and multi-byte text—not just state strings.
- A pack declares an allowlist of data classes. Runtime requires `actual classes ⊆ pack allowlist ⊆ active policy allowed classes` for the exact stored policy version before network access. Caller-supplied labels can only make the actual set more restrictive; they can never remove a class derived from source records.
- A company-data call additionally requires its code-owned runtime target to exist in the active map, an exact in-memory API-key match to that target's fingerprint, and current `enterprise_zdr` vendor posture. The raw key never reaches database rows or logs. Any target/credential/posture mismatch makes zero network requests; revocation, map replacement, or posture drift during an in-flight call causes the activation fence to discard the result.
- Hard mappings are deliberately conservative and content-independent: arbitrary MCP query text and every employee message/meeting utterance carry the full possible pasted/free-text union `internal_company`, `employee_communication`, `licensed_content`, `personnel_hr`, `customer_personal`, and `unknown_restricted`. This intentionally blocks free text unless the active policy approves every class; neither user identity nor channel provenance proves pasted content ownership. Claims inherit the union of every evidence-source class; retrieved context inherits every included claim/source class; document content is `unknown_restricted` until its stored source classification is explicitly approved. Mixed sources retain every class instead of collapsing to one enum value. Later narrowing requires a separately planned and reviewed deterministic provenance rule with fixtures that prove it cannot understate pasted content—never semantic guessing, a caller label, or an LLM classifier.
- The API key is never included in thrown errors, raw usage, telemetry, or debug logging.
- Contract tests set hostile `TYPESAFE_LOG_LEVEL=debug`, inject state/key-like sentinels plus success/error responses, capture console/stdout/stderr and any SDK logger hook, and prove explicit `logLevel:'off'` produces no request, response, key, or state output.
- The wrapper returns normalized typed answers plus provider `typesafe`, requested model, resolved version, latency, tokens, and computed cost.
- The production default is the pinned version, not `jev-latest`.
- `typesafe-client.ts` configures every SDK invocation with `retry: { maxRetries: 0 }` and enforces the question pack's total wall-clock deadline with `AbortController`. Only the application-owned, separately leased attempts share that total budget and cancellation signal; the SDK may never retry internally. There is no unbounded background request or late result that can affect behavior.

**Verification gate**

- `pnpm --filter @oracle/ai typecheck` passes.
- New `pnpm --filter @oracle/ai verify:typesafe-decision-contract` passes with mocked transport and no real key.
- Root `package.json` exposes `verify:jev-contract`, and `.github/workflows/pr-check.yml` runs it as a network-free required step with a placeholder key that cannot reach the network.
- A repository search proves `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` appears only in env/config documentation and server-side decision code.
- Split delivery into ordered outcomes. **1A:** commit the `inventory` manifest/scanner with a hash-pinned legacy baseline; merge migration/schema, group/versioned-login roles, exact grants/RLS/function topology; migrate and verify journal, identities, ownership, and policy coverage with no Jev reader. **1B:** migrate every operational script, merge clients/controls/workers, set scanner `dual`, and run every real-DB manifest regression under both new login roles. **1C/1D:** atomically migrate all callers and tighten the scanner to `worker-strict`, then `strict`, while cutting over one platform per session: create/store/test its versioned credential through the protected flow, deliver via authenticated platform API, redeploy, verify actual identity/RLS/privileges and full business regression, then remove migration credential; disable the old login only after drain/proof. Any unclassified or non-baseline legacy call, scanner-phase regression, extra/missing grant/policy, connection-string failure, or changed behavior blocks removal. No new-table reader merges before 1A proof.
- `pnpm db:generate`, migration verification, `pnpm db:check-drift` against the intended non-production target, and schema typecheck pass before Step 1A. A clean-install migration test and a current-production-snapshot rehearsal prove the schema order before the guarded production migration.
- Direct-access tests exercise browser JWT roles, service role, both runtime group/login roles, and migration owner. Human RPC succeeds only for signed active admin; runtime functions only for enumerated transitions. Fresh-install/snapshot tests create and compare exact grants **and runtime RLS policies**, including direct operations that previously relied on `authenticated`; every manifest regression runs against real SQL across auth/linking, chat/messages, admin settings/catalog/documents/claims, MCP, Teams/Recall webhooks, extraction, document ingestion, contradiction, synthesis, transcript, batch-drain, and maintenance families. Negative tests remove one required policy and add one extra policy to prove both fail. Isolated-process environment tests prove each DB accessor reads exactly one variable and missing targets throw even when `DIRECT_URL` exists; static transitive scans enforce surface/client pairing; deployed bundles contain no `DIRECT_URL` read. Post-cutover probes prove `current_user`, expected group membership, `rolbypassrls=false`, pooler mode/SSL, exact access, rotation/rollback behavior, and migration-credential absence.
- Database-authority concurrency tests bypass application helpers and race 100 raw inserts for the same run identity with each nullable identity field set to null in turn; exactly one pending/succeeded identity survives. They then race each subject target type with null and non-null ordinals/hashes; every target-specific index admits exactly one logical subject while different targets/ordinals remain distinct. Schema introspection asserts `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, the partial predicates, and complete one-index-per-target coverage on clean and production-snapshot databases.
- Source-erasure tests independently delete a message, document, meeting transcript, external-system record copy, and manual-admin evidence source through their typed authorization routes. A two-claim contradiction fixture deletes each side separately and cannot affect an unrelated run. Real-shape fixtures seed Recall `job_runs` text/error output, Trigger/Vercel history, GitHub sentinel fields, workflow-map quotations, shared OpenAI files/jobs, Anthropic jobs/results, Vertex jobs/GCS input/output, a source-derived Recall/Teams question, an Oracle assistant reply quoting the source, meeting participants/transcript URL/AI summary, import-created channel title/membership, Recall bot channel/mapping, transcript-created employee/channel/memberships, and a multi-evidence approved claim whose summary/embedding/translation feed Brain and relationships. Erasure must remove every live database/provider/platform copy, prove GitHub contains no source metadata, delete the entire contaminated derived chain, requeue only unaffected batch sources, rebuild solely from surviving evidence under new IDs after commit, remove locators, plain hashes, transient/provider comparison HMACs, lineage, and requester/target/reason fields; destroy the temporary request; and leave in ordinary database/provider/platform evidence only random run IDs plus counts/outcome codes/timestamps. The sole explicit exception is the separately access-locked permanent admission tombstone: it retains only source kind plus a non-exportable-key HMAC in GCS so erased identity cannot be re-admitted. It is classified as irreversible pseudonymous control data, excluded from application retrieval and proof output, and must be disclosed in and accepted by the owner-approved vendor/data-retention terms before any non-synthetic activation. A restored pre-erasure snapshot remains isolated until tombstone reconciliation deletes the resurrected source and all derivatives and a zero-match signed artifact permits traffic; final verification additionally proves no remaining Supabase backup/PITR point can contain it. Wrong external record/manual employee, unclassified JSON/table/column, unlinked derived field, missing provider job/file/message ID, provider-list gap, readable post-success request, retained request metadata, missing tombstone, restore bypass, or any sentinel in logs/artifacts/attestations/reports blocks success.
- Before the first live synthetic probe on a target, the post-secret transaction has created exactly one active immutable `v1-public-synthetic` policy whose entry for that code-owned target matches its loaded key fingerprint. Before the first company-data pilot, exactly one active immutable replacement policy matches Step 0's approved matrix/evidence and every enabled target's loaded credential; otherwise the mismatched or absent target is blocked.
- Policy tests swap the API key with settings unchanged and require zero network requests; independently change account, retention, residency, subprocessors, evidence, and expiry and prove each fingerprint change forces shadow, invalidates reuse, and starts a fresh canary cohort. Expired/non-ZDR policy permits only exact public-synthetic probes; raw key bytes never appear in database/log output.
- Document-classification tests prove legacy/default rows remain blocked, non-admin and invalid/empty classes fail, an approved multi-class row records actor/version/event, policy revocation blocks it, and deleting the classifier nulls only the FK.

#### Step 1B/2 — Integrate the client together with observability and failure behavior

This section is not a later milestone: every file, migration, verifier, and acceptance condition below is owned by the single STATUS row `1B/2` and must merge/prove with the decision client. There is no separately runnable Step 2 and no client-only completion state.

**Step 1E milestone ownership used throughout this section:** 1E1 adds a required `SourceProvenanceEnvelope` to `packages/ai/src/client/types.ts`, `packages/ai/src/providers/types.ts`, `packages/ai/src/embeddings.ts`, every batch/cache/file contract, and every production caller, and makes Recall task plus Trigger/Vercel dispatch ID-only; it uses captured fake transports and shadow-compatible telemetry to prove complete source-set propagation but does not enforce leases, block an existing request, or change provider/retry behavior. 1E1-C proves the exact deployment and complete current-callsite inventory, not one synthetic call. 1E2 owns derived-lineage dual-write, inventory, reconciliation, and one atomic enforcement-generation cutover: ambiguity blocks the cutover while prior behavior stays active; after the cutover, every synchronous provider, embedding, batch/cache/file, TypeSafe attempt, and platform dispatch must reserve its complete source set before bytes leave, with no compatibility bypass. 1E2-C exercises every request class with captured fakes plus applicable fixed public-synthetic live probes. 1E3 owns the prepare/verify/production workflow code, temporary request, and three independently closure-bound but inert identities—the production identity receives no provider, tombstone, KMS, backup, or database-finalizer authority, while the verifier's installed tombstone mode must fail because it lacks KMS/object access. 1E4 owns only KMS, retained tombstones, restore/lifecycle workflow infrastructure, backup-metadata read credentials, and the verifier's read-only tombstone plus KMS MAC-sign grants; it deploys no application route or caller and changes no ingestion behavior. After 1E4-C, 1E4U separately deploys the private source-admission service, every ingestion caller, upload-intent clients/routes/effect outbox, and bounded orphan cleanup, then 1E4U-C proves both healthy and denied service paths before 1E5; restore still cannot dispatch the not-yet-installed finalizer. 1E5 alone adds AI/embedding/Storage/cache/batch lifecycle adapters and exact permissions; its TypeSafe adapter tests use only the captured fake. 1E6 adds Recall/Teams/Trigger/Vercel lifecycle adapters; 1E7 owns the database finalizer, `pre105-v1`, fan-out coordinator, and finalizer-dispatch grants; 1E8 owns isolated restore reconciliation and fresh-authorized backup-finalization resumption. 1E9 alone enables/proves the composed public-synthetic route for every non-TypeSafe live surface and uses the captured fake for TypeSafe retry/crash/ZDR/erasure behavior; it must make zero TypeSafe network requests because no TypeSafe credential exists. Every 1E1–1E8 implementation, including 1E4U, remains incomplete until its separate `-C` proof merges; 1E9-C records that bounded infrastructure/live result and cannot claim vendor proof. Step 2L alone owns the real TypeSafe public-synthetic retry/crash/ZDR/erasure proof after 2K-C has provisioned the restricted credentials, and 2L-C must merge before any Jev pilot. Any later “Step 1E” statement is an umbrella requirement allocated by this rule, not permission to combine milestones, permissions, or approvals.

**Files/functions**

- 1E4U adds CODEOWNED `config/source-admission-writers.json` and `scripts/verify-source-admission-writers.mjs`. The manifest enumerates every production source or source-derived writer with file/export, deployed surface, relation/object/RPC, `source|derived|metadata_only` classification, stable-key resolver, admission point, transaction/effect boundary, deployment artifact, and regression ID. The scanner covers tracked TypeScript/JavaScript, SQL tagged templates, owner functions, Storage upload/copy, Drizzle insert/upsert/update, raw `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT`, and task-dispatch imports; it fails on an unmanifested writer, new source-bearing or source-derived column/table/object/field, indirect helper, dynamic SQL, caller, or route. The pinned current baseline explicitly includes both document routes; ordinary `/api/messages`; admin import; Teams notification plus scheduled/ad-hoc discovery upserts; Teams transcript `raw_transcripts`, employee bootstrap, channel, membership, and message writes; admin-import channel/title/membership writes; both Recall channel/bot-ID/mapping and user/assistant inserts; chat assistant persistence; contradiction and lull assistant inserts; and every new external-system, manual-admin, query-origin, anchor, and upload-intent writer. Generated Vercel and Trigger entrypoint closures must equal this manifest and the deployed bundle/source-map inventories for the exact merged SHA; a source writer present in only one surface or only at runtime fails 1E4U-C.
- The manifest's source contracts are executable. Ordinary channel-message clients keep draft plaintext only in component memory and persist **only** a random client nonce, returned `messageIntentId`, signed handle, issue/expiry time, and non-content state in per-user session storage; no draft/message/file bytes, filename, caption, hash, or reversible ciphertext enters browser storage. Authenticated metadata-only `POST /api/messages/intents` accepts only channel plus nonce, revalidates active membership, allocates or returns the same 24-hour `ordinary_message` intent under the unique creator/channel/nonce constraint, calls `oracle-source-admission` for `internal_message\0<intent-id>`, stores only its opaque token/permit digest, and returns the scoped signed absence handle; a lost issuance response is retryable. `/api/messages` verifies the handle's signature, issuer/key version, user, channel, intent state, and expiry before `request.json()`, then revalidates membership, atomically binds a keyed exact-content token, uses the intent UUID as the message/source UUID, and returns the existing row only when token and scope match. On success or a lost submit response, an authenticated metadata-only intent-status request returns only `uncommitted|committed|expired|erased` plus the existing message ID when authorized; `committed` clears the in-memory draft and all opaque session state, `uncommitted` after reload requires the user to re-enter text, and clients never reconstruct content from storage. Explicit cancel, logout, account switch, handle expiry, source-erasure notification, and startup scavenging delete the opaque session entry; startup purges malformed entries and every entry older than 24 hours. Another device/browser may retain only an opaque expired handle: server intent state and the permanent tombstone are authoritative, so post-erasure status/submit returns `erased`, triggers local cleanup, and makes zero source/storage/dispatch writes. Changed content under one intent, cross-user/channel replay, guessed UUID, expired/consumed/erased handle, signature/key drift, concurrent submit, and lost response fail or converge without a second message/lull effect. The admin-import client persists only a random upload intent/handle and timestamps through retry—never file/import plaintext—and follows identical acknowledgement/cancel/logout/expiry/erasure cleanup; `/api/admin/import/intents` issues it, and the import route verifies that signed intent before reading the body, keys each deterministic chunk as `admin_import\0upload_intent_id\0ordinal`, and atomically binds the ordered output digest/count and all messages/anchors. Tests cover submit-before/after-commit response loss, reload/crash, browser close/reopen, logout/account switch, 24-hour expiry, malformed/stale storage, cross-device erasure before/after commit, delayed erasure notification, offline reconnect, and browser storage inspection proving zero plaintext while exactly-once server effects remain recoverable. Teams notification/discovery/ingestion share `microsoft_graph\0tenant\0transcript_id`; transcript ingestion admits that meeting-transcript anchor before persisting full VTT or any cue and treats cue messages as its derived children. Every Recall delivery first resolves the parent source identity `recall\0bot_id\0transcript_id`, excluding text, participant, offset, and the current truncated digest; missing bot/transcript identity blocks before ingress storage or dispatch. All utterance revisions, messages, bot-named channels, mappings, and posts are derived children of that parent anchor. Deleting any Recall utterance therefore erases the complete parent-transcript closure and its tombstone blocks identical or corrected-payload replay. Step 8B may add a strong per-event identity for delivery idempotency, but it must retain and check the parent anchor/tombstone for both strong and weak payloads. Chat, contradiction, lull, and Recall-question assistant messages are derived-only: their owner functions require the complete contributing source-anchor/version set and may not create an independent source anchor. External/manual/query-origin writers use their normative keys above. Per-caller tests create a tombstone then replay/retry/resync the exact ordinary message, import, document, meeting/transcript, Recall event, external record, manual evidence, and query origin; every path makes zero source/storage/dispatch writes, while service/KMS denial fails before persistence. Derived-writer tests prove complete lineage and reject source-anchor creation; live proof invokes every Vercel and Trigger writer under healthy and denied admission and binds both exact deployment IDs/SHAs.
- `source_derived_artifact_sources` is also the mandatory field-level provenance ledger for source-created channels, channel names, bot IDs/mappings, memberships, and employee bootstrap fields—not only AI outputs. Admin import links its source-title-derived channel name and created membership to the import anchor. Teams transcript ingestion links every created/updated employee name/email/directory field, meeting channel/name, participant membership, raw transcript, and cue message to the meeting-transcript anchor. Recall links the bot-named channel, bot mapping/ID, membership, utterance, and assistant post to the parent Recall transcript anchor. The erasure manifest specifies per field/row: if the artifact has no independent provenance and no surviving content, delete it; if a channel survives, replace only still-matching source-derived title/bot fields with a fixed neutral value and delete source-only memberships/mappings; preserve a membership or employee field only when a separately recorded independent source proves the same value. A bootstrapped employee with later references but no independent identity is a retryable blocker for admin identity reconciliation, never silently retained or cascade-deleted. Hash/version drift, shared channel, independent membership, independently linked employee, and concurrent new content are rechecked under ordered locks. Fixtures cover import-only and later-shared channels, Recall bot channels, transcript-created employees with/without independent identity, memberships with one/two proven sources, field edits, and erasure of either source; no title, bot ID, employee field, membership, or mapping derived solely from the erased source survives. This 1E4U/1E7 closure supersedes any Step 8B wording that deferred Recall channel cleanup.
- Add one narrow private `oracle-source-admission` service in 1E4U so tombstones prevent ordinary re-ingestion, not merely duplicate active rows. Its stable identity is provider namespace plus immutable provider message ID for external messages; the client-persisted permanent `messageIntentId` used as the row UUID for an internal message; `admin_import\0upload_intent_id\0chunk_ordinal` for imported message chunks; provider namespace plus immutable meeting/transcript ID for meetings; `document\0upload_intent_id` for an Oracle document upload; external namespace plus immutable record ID; permanent creation UUID for manual evidence; and query-origin UUID. Step 1E4U adds authenticated metadata-only `POST /api/admin/documents/intents` and `POST /api/documents/intents` preflight handlers plus matching admin/channel client calls. Each preflight accepts one bounded ordered metadata array, rejects duplicate per-file nonces or a count above the existing ceiling, and returns an object keyed by those nonces—not array position. For each item it receives the exact normalized filename, caption or filename-derived message text, admin context/domain hints, channel, purpose, content type, and declared size, rejects disagreement with the authenticated route, stores only their keyed canonical commitment, and embeds that commitment and scope in the signed handle. Before either client sends a multipart body, it persists one random batch nonce and one random per-file nonce plus the returned signed handles in per-user browser session storage, obtains one server-created signed intent handle per file, and retains those values through success or explicit cancel; handles are user/scope-bound, expire within 24 hours, and cannot authorize any other operation. Replaying the same creator/scoped nonces returns the same intent after a lost preflight response. The admin multi-select UI converts its batch into one bounded multipart request per file; the routes accept exactly one `files` part and one signed-intent header per request, so no header-to-part ordering exists. They reject zero/multiple file parts, a missing/extra handle, or a handle/header over 8 KiB. Each route verifies the one header before calling `request.formData()` or reading/storing bytes; the existing multipart endpoints reject missing, expired, consumed-by-another-document, wrong-user, wrong-channel/context/purpose, tombstoned intents, or any multipart metadata whose recomputed commitment differs. The intent is bound server-side to uploader, channel/context, declared purpose, and source kind; every retry, re-sync, storage path, and replacement Oracle row for that one upload instance must reuse it. A SHA-256 of the bytes is computed only in memory and retained only as a keyed comparison token for rejecting changed bytes under the same intent; it is never a global document identity. A separately sourced external document instead uses provider namespace plus immutable upstream document ID. Distinct upload intents remain distinct legitimate sources even when bytes, filename, uploader, channel, or context coincide. This registry is normative and supersedes any later umbrella wording that generically assigns an Oracle row UUID or content hash to document, externally sourced message, or meeting identity. The service converts only that typed stable key into the non-exportable KMS HMAC used as the tombstone object name, checks the exact object by name, and returns a short-lived signed absence permit plus opaque admission token. It has only KMS MAC-sign and exact-object `get`—no list/write/delete—and request/body logging is disabled and live-verified. Vercel/Trigger runtime receives only exact service invocation through enforceable short-lived platform workload identity; if either platform cannot supply that identity, every non-synthetic ingestion path blocks for a separately reviewed provider proxy and never receives a static/broad key.
- Every create/upsert/re-sync/re-upload path calls source admission before inserting a source or anchor. The upload streams only to the deterministic intent-derived Storage path with create-if-absent generation precondition and server-owned keyed byte-token metadata; an existing object is accepted only when generation, byte token, size, and content type match, and is never overwritten. Its database transaction then takes the advisory transaction lock derived from the opaque token, revalidates the unexpired signed permit and metadata commitment, enforces one active anchor per token through a partial unique index, and atomically binds the intent, object generation/token, document, source, and anchor. For channel upload it also creates or reuses the exact caption/filename-derived message plus `message_attachments` row in that same transaction; admin context/domain hints and every user-visible field come only from the committed preflight metadata. The three child tables' unique intent FKs make an identical-byte retry return the same IDs with no second object, row, message, or attachment. The transaction records `dispatch_pending` and the three applicable effect slots. Only after commit does one outbox dispatcher send document ingestion, channel lull check, and the Oracle-reply request under separate stable keys `document-upload:<intent-id>:ingest|lull|reply`; admin uploads mark the two channel-only slots inapplicable. The channel client no longer launches an unkeyed reply after success: it observes or retries the intent-bound reply slot. Each adapter persists its run/message ID, reconciles `unknown` or a lost provider response before retry, and advances `complete` only when every applicable slot is terminal exactly once. Erasure takes the identical lock before durable tombstone creation. Tombstone present, service/KMS outage, stale permit, unknown identity, or reuse of an upload intent with different bytes fails closed. Reintroducing erased data is a new reviewed admin reauthorization flow and is out of scope. Barrier tests cover external resync under the same ID; one upload intent retried under a new filename, storage path, or database ID; two distinct upload intents with identical bytes across different uploader/channel/context combinations; erasing one such instance while the other and its derivatives remain unchanged; changed bytes under one intent; lost preflight, object-create, database-commit, and dispatch responses; failure before/after object creation, each document/message/attachment insert, transaction commit, each ingest/lull/reply dispatch send, provider response, and run/message-ID persistence; partial multi-file success followed by retry using the original per-file handles; client reload/cancel behavior; the same meeting under a new Oracle UUID; retried external/internal messages; erasure-versus-ingestion races; service/KMS outage; and log-sentinel absence. The admission tombstone is a permanent non-reversible deny marker: its held object, object generation, and named KMS key version are never released, deleted, disabled, or destroyed, even after backup/restore evidence expires. Only separate content-free run/commit attestations may follow finite retention. The only way to reintroduce the same stable identity is the future reviewed reauthorization design, which must create a distinct signed allow record checked by admission; absence of that not-yet-built record always denies.
- New `packages/ai/src/decisions/run-decision.ts`: derive provenance classes, serialize/hash the exact request, and snapshot all pre-call control fields including activation generation; attempt the partial-unique pending insert plus subject links before the call. Configure the pinned SDK explicitly with `retry: { maxRetries: 0 }`, inject the captured one-request fetch transport, and implement the reviewed maximum of three billable attempts in application code so each network attempt receives its own committed outbound lease. The loop uses the pinned SDK's reviewed retryable status/error classification and bounded backoff but refuses a fourth attempt; an SDK/version change that makes one method call emit more than one HTTP request blocks. Before enabling any network call, pin and contract-test the vendor's exact integer **maximum billable input tokens per attempt**, including the complete SDK-generated request and all provider-billed protocol/model overhead; if the vendor does not guarantee one ceiling, activation is blocked. The initial pinned Jev contract uses the documented 64,000-token total request ceiling only after a captured-SDK/live-synthetic test proves reported billable input cannot exceed it. Store the documented price as the integer rational `42,000 microusd / 1,000,000 tokens`; lock the active spend epoch and atomically reserve three times `ceil(max_billable_tokens × 42,000 / 1,000,000)` microusd—never floating-point dollars or a byte/token estimate—before network access. Thus 64,000 tokens reserve exactly 2,688 microusd per attempt and 8,064 microusd for three attempts. If no epoch exists or `spent + reserved + full three-attempt ceiling > cap`, record `discarded/budget_blocked` and execute current behavior. Finalization atomically moves the entire ceiling reservation from reserved to permanent spent; reported actual cost is observational and never releases unknown retry cost. A provider report above the pinned ceiling is a contract breach: settle the reported amount, atomically force every Jev mode off, alert, and block all further calls; this exceptional vendor violation is reported as an external overrun rather than falsely described as client-enforced. Under the pinned contract, admitted calls cannot exceed the epoch cap. Crash reconciliation settles the full reservation exactly once and reconciles each possible attempt lease before any retry. Duplicate identity never calls TypeSafe. Fresh/reused effects still pass `withDecisionActivationFence`, which revalidates mode, generation, policy, pack, threshold, requested/resolved model, exact input hash, and subjects under the same control lock; mismatch discards/current-path. Do not copy or write generation `model_runs` records.
- New `packages/ai/src/decisions/reconcile.ts` and `retention.ts`; call both from the existing `apps/workers/src/trigger/claim-extraction-batch-drain.ts` schedule rather than adding a Trigger.dev schedule. The task records a monotonic start and enforces a **510-second end-to-end work deadline**, leaving the final 90 seconds of Trigger's 600-second limit exclusively for lease release, durable `job_runs` failure/success finalization, and bounded error emission. An atomic database task lease keyed to `claim-extraction-batch-drain` admits one run and has a fixed, non-extendable `lease_until = database_start + 9 minutes 45 seconds`; heartbeats may prove liveness but may never extend that deadline. A second invocation before expiry records `skipped/lease_held`; the next ten-minute cron tick is therefore always later than the killed predecessor's lease and can reclaim it. Replace the unbounded loop with cursor/indexed pages. **Before any provider batch**, run decision reconciliation/origin expiry for at most 60 seconds, Live-Recall recovery for at most 60 seconds once Step 8B lands, and daily retention for at most 30 seconds; each has its own durable cursor and stops before its sub-deadline. Provider draining then gets at most 300 seconds and no more than 25 due jobs, while 60 seconds remain for setup/overhead inside the 510-second work window. Unused maintenance time is not donated to batches, and no new unit starts after its phase or task deadline.
- Step 1A adds nullable `drain_lease_owner`, `drain_lease_until`, `drain_attempt`, and `next_poll_at` fields plus the claim index `(status,next_poll_at,submitted_at,id)` to existing `provider_batch_jobs`. Each provider submission sets the first bounded `next_poll_at`; each nonterminal poll advances it by the provider-specific bounded backoff. The drain selects only due jobs in stable oldest-first `(next_poll_at ASC, submitted_at ASC, id ASC)` order, claims them in one short transaction using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a random invocation ID, and database time, and never advances a cursor past an older unclaimed due row. Polling/finalization compare-and-set that same owner/attempt; a killed/stale owner can be reclaimed only after its fixed task/batch lease expires, and a late predecessor then cannot poll, promote candidates, change messages, or finalize. The maintenance pass marks every decision `pending` past `deadline_at + 2 minutes` as `discarded/process_interrupted`, expires query origins, and uses its independent database lease to run bounded retention at most once per UTC day. `runDecision` also reconciles the same versioned decision key before insert. Remove the drain's early successful return when no provider batches exist. Per-batch failures remain retryable with error metadata, but any drain or maintenance failure sets the scheduled `job_runs` row failed and throws one bounded aggregate error before the 510-second deadline; if finalization cannot be durably confirmed by that deadline, the Trigger run fails and the task lease remains only until its fixed expiry rather than reporting success.
- Extend `RetrieveBatchArgs` with required `signal: AbortSignal` and absolute `deadlineAt`; the drain refuses to start a poll with fewer than 30 seconds remaining, derives an AbortController deadline no later than the 480-second mark, and passes it through Anthropic, OpenAI, and Vertex adapters. Each adapter disables SDK retries and passes both the abort signal and remaining request timeout into its captured fetch/SDK request; the transport destroys the HTTP request/socket on abort and must settle within five seconds. `Promise.race` without transport cancellation is forbidden. An adapter/SDK that cannot demonstrate cancellation is disabled for batch draining until replaced by the same provider through a cancellable request path. Contract tests use never-responding TCP/fake SDK endpoints for all three adapters, abort immediately before/during response and JSON parsing, and prove the promise settles, the lease/failure row finalizes before 510 seconds, and the 90-second reserve remains intact.
- Capacity telemetry records `due_count`, `claimed_count`, and `oldest_due_age`. Before activation, a 30-day production-shaped trace or a conservative synthetic envelope must prove no more than 20 newly due jobs per ten-minute interval and oldest-due age at most 20 minutes with the 25-job cap; exceeding 20 minutes makes the scheduled run fail visibly and alert while it still drains the oldest jobs first, and blocks Jev batch activation until a separately reviewed capacity design lands. Fake-clock/real-PostgreSQL tests have two explicit profiles: (a) a 10,000-job initial backlog plus newer arrivals proves every initial job is claimed in strict order within the first 400 admitted ticks while the capacity gate remains failed until the backlog clears, and (b) a steady 20 newly due jobs per tick proves every job is claimed within the same or following tick and `oldest_due_age<=20 minutes`. Both profiles also prove the exact 25 cap, next-tick recovery after a kill at 599 seconds, stale-owner rejection, exactly-once candidate/message effects, maintenance-first fairness, at least 90 seconds of shutdown reserve, and no timeout/partial/no-work/capacity breach returning `{ok:true}` or a successful Trigger run.
- New `packages/ai/src/decisions/health.ts`: `reserveCanary` transaction first inserts a reservation by `(use_case,config_fingerprint,ISO week,decision_key,input_hash)`; only a successful insert locks/increments the matching fingerprinted counter and records the first-N/every-tenth result, while a conflict returns the existing reservation unchanged. `reserveAgreementReview` atomically updates that reservation/counter for the weekly agreement-review minimum. Pack, threshold, policy, requested model, approved resolved model, or activation-generation change creates a fresh fingerprint and fresh first-N cohort even midweek. The module also persists comparator results, append-only human adjudication, rolling error calculations, and fenced `active→shadow` plus control event. Never disable unrelated use cases.
- `retention.ts` performs the daily bounded cleanup from that shared schedule. Delete pending/failed/discarded runs after 30 days, successful shadow runs after 90 days, and successful active runs after 365 days; child subjects/adjudications cascade with the run. The ten-minute maintenance path expires every MCP query origin no later than 24 hours plus one schedule interval and, in the same transaction, invokes source-erasure invalidation and deletes the now-orphaned decision run, removing its decision/input hash immediately. Normally an open review pauses age-based deletion. **Source erasure takes precedence but business resolution comes first:** the ordered erasure procedure must complete every transition and audit event required by the currently installed `pre105-v1` or `post105-v2` handler before it may dismiss/delete the linked decision; a failed resolution rolls back source and decision deletion. Only then may the orphan run delete within 24 hours. Retain acknowledged control events for 730 days and unacknowledged events until acknowledged; their run/adjudication FKs become null while bounded snapshots remain. Retain immutable data-policy versions, consumed owner approvals, and spend-epoch ledgers permanently because they contain no source text and explain historical authorization/spend; delete unused expired owner approvals after 30 days. Decision deletion never decrements spend. Delete canary counters and matching reservations after 90 days. Deleted employee/reviewer/approver FKs become null only through the enumerated allowlists. Store no raw source text, free-text review note, or employee ID outside typed FKs.
- Add `scripts/prepare-oracle-source-erasure.ts`, `packages/db/src/__verify__/source-erasure-authorization.ts`, protected default-branch `.github/workflows/source-erasure-prepare.yml`, `.github/workflows/source-erasure-verify.yml`, `.github/workflows/source-erasure-production.yml`, and a signed two-mode verifier (`manifest|tombstone`). The authenticated admin action—not the preparer—resolves the typed business object through its fixed server-side resolver and atomically inserts a random `prepared` erasure run with temporary nullable anchor FK/version, authenticated requester FK, and bounded reason; direct SQL/runtime/service roles cannot create or retarget it. The prepare workflow accepts only that random run ID, locks and reads the registered anchor/replicas, and cannot select another object. Step 1E pre-creates exact Secret Manager resource `projects/vertex-ai-497120/secrets/oracle-source-erasure-request`; a database advisory lock permits exactly one active erasure, so each run uses one enabled secret version labeled only by random run ID and all prior versions must already be destroyed. The prepare command builds the exact source/resource/action/dependency manifest and a random per-run HMAC key in memory and streams them directly into that version; Secret Manager's managed encryption is the sole at-rest encryption layer. Arguments/stdout/files receive no source locator, external resource ID, path, hash, secret payload, or comparison key. It emits only the random run ID, contract/closure versions, action-class counts, expiry, and keyed manifest digest. Each execution then requires its own sealed production APPROVE bound to that verifier attestation and opaque digest, target-project aliases, action classes/counts, procedure/workflow/closure checksums, per-stage/five-minute bounds, and run ID. Canary sentinels in every sensitive field plus GitHub API retrieval of logs/artifacts/attestations/reports prove zero sentinel or identifying metadata escaped; any output-capable step, upload, annotation, shell tracing, debug logging, unverifiable retention, or timeout/partial-write ambiguity blocks 1E at its last durable checkpoint.

  Step 1E4 creates non-exportable Cloud KMS HMAC-SHA256 key `projects/vertex-ai-497120/locations/us-east1/keyRings/oracle-erasure/cryptoKeys/restore-tombstone-hmac`, GCS bucket `oracle-erasure-tombstones-vertex-ai-497120` with uniform access, versioning, public-access prevention, and locked retention equal to the proved maximum Supabase backup/PITR window plus 30 days, and exact-workflow service account `oracle-source-restore@vertex-ai-497120.iam.gserviceaccount.com`. Each source kind uses the normative admission identity defined above: externally sourced messages use provider namespace plus immutable provider message ID; internal messages use their permanent client-persisted intent/row UUID; imported message chunks use `admin_import\0upload_intent_id\0ordinal`; Oracle document uploads use `document\0upload_intent_id`, with byte hashes limited to same-intent mutation checks; externally sourced documents use provider namespace plus immutable upstream document ID; meetings use provider namespace plus immutable meeting/transcript ID; external-system records use namespace plus immutable record ID; manual evidence and query origins use permanent UUIDs. Legacy/pre-anchor adapters use deterministic existing provenance; a document row created before `upload_intent_id` uses `legacy-document\0` plus its permanent historical document UUID as that one upload instance, so an old backup can derive the same key without a later backfill row. An ambiguous namespace or document instance blocks reconciliation. Fixtures cover case-sensitive ASCII, Unicode, delimiter/NUL rejection, the full 255-character external ID, and identical record IDs in two namespaces producing different stable keys/tombstones with no cross-deletion. Changing either external component creates a distinct source and requires separate erasure. At 1E4 the erasure identity gains only `cloudkms.cryptoKeyVersions.useToSign` on the key and create-if-absent access to two immutable random-run objects: the pre-deletion tombstone and the later content-free database-commit attestation; it still has no provider or database-finalizer access. The tombstone is created with an event-based hold that the erasure, restore, and ordinary lifecycle identities cannot release. Before any provider or database deletion, the independent verifier reads the sealed request and tombstone, recomputes `HMAC(key, source-kind\0stable_source_key)` from its fresh database resolution, checks generation/hold/key-version/object digest, then emits a signed content-free `tombstone-durable` attestation. The production workflow cannot advance from `prepared` without that exact attestation; timeout, partial upload, stale generation, missing hold, ambiguous response, missing read-back, or retry with different bytes leaves source/request intact and blocks. After database commit, the workflow writes the second held object only from the independently observed `database_commit_attested` row; it binds tombstone digest, random run ID, commit/release proof digests, conservative post-commit database-server timestamp, and `retain_until = database_commit_attested_at + proved maximum backup/PITR window + 30 days`, and an independent read-back must pass before secret destruction. The tombstone's required retain-until is derived from that commit attestation, not its creation time; until the cutoff exists both objects remain held indefinitely. A backup appearing after tombstone creation but before database commit is therefore governed by the later commit cutoff. At 1E4 `.github/workflows/source-restore-reconcile.yml` gets the restore identity with only key MAC-sign, held-object read/list, and read-only Supabase backup metadata; it has no hold-release, finalizer dispatch, provider-delete, object-write/delete, application runtime, deployment, or IAM authority. Step 1E7 later adds only the ability to dispatch the existing migration finalizer with a sealed run ID/version/digest; it never grants database credentials. The pre-deletion tombstone contains only the stable-key HMAC, key-version resource, source kind, random run ID, contract version, and `prepared_at`; the separate commit attestation contains no stable-key HMAC or locator. Neither object contains a raw locator, source/version ID, content hash, or reversible ciphertext. The pre-deletion admission tombstone's event-based hold is permanent and no lifecycle identity can release, overwrite, or delete it. Only the separate content-free commit-attestation object may have its hold released after a separately approved action proves the run `verified`, both objects' digests, current time at or after `retain_until`, no unreconciled restore, and the bucket's locked-retention requirement; only a later run may delete that unheld attestation object. Automatic and manual rotation are disabled for this admission-tombstone key: exactly one pinned HMAC key version signs every stable identity for the key's lifetime, never changes primary status, and has no disable or destroy path. Any added version, changed primary, rotation schedule, or version-state drift blocks admission, erasure, and restore. Moving identities to another version is a separate future migration that must prove every old and new object mapping before activation and is out of scope. Desired-state and live tests prove the same source maps to the same object name across time and reject every version/primary drift; finite-retention attestations use a separate key if rotation is ever needed. A deletion delayed past the bucket's ordinary retention period therefore leaves the permanent tombstone held and its key version enabled; only the content-free commit attestation starts its finite retention clock from the post-commit cutoff. Delay-past-retention, any tombstone hold-release/delete/key-disable/key-destroy attempt, early attestation release/delete, lifecycle crash, and admission after ordinary backup retention all fail closed; only the eligible commit-attestation cleanup succeeds. Desired-state config, plan/apply scripts, OIDC CEL, API enablement, bucket location, stable-key registry, fixed key-version/retention registry, exact IAM bindings, drift reconciliation, teardown refusal, and live allow/deny probes are named deliverables of 1E4; absence or drift blocks that milestone.

  Backup restoration is gated, not assumed away. Live deletion reaches `live_deleted_backup_pending` only after the finalizer commit, independent post-commit/zero-closure attestation, immutable commit-attestation read-back, and exact temporary-request destruction are each durable. The erasure workflow queries the Supabase management API for PITR range and the **complete currently retained scheduled-backup inventory**, records only content-free timestamps/counts, and cannot mark `verified` until the earliest restorable PITR point is strictly later than `database_commit_attested_at` **and every retained scheduled backup capable of containing any state from before that cutoff is gone**. Concretely, every backup with snapshot/capture start at or before the cutoff blocks, including one completed long before deletion and one that begins before but completes after it; missing capture-start metadata is ambiguous and blocks until that backup expires. A later backup is clear only when authenticated metadata proves its snapshot and entire capture window start strictly after the cutoff. `prepared_at`, provider-deletion time, completion time alone, or “window reaches cutoff” is never sufficient. Every restore procedure keeps web and workers disabled, restores into an isolated target, then invokes the separately approved restore workflow before DNS/traffic. That workflow scans both current anchors and legacy/pre-anchor source tables, derives the stable key for every historical row regardless of `erasure_version`, asks KMS to recompute tombstone HMACs, deletes every match and its full derived closure through the current reviewed restore-reconcile executable and its schema adapter without assuming a restored run/finalizer row, rescans to zero, records `restore_reconciled` in the external restore record, and emits a signed content-free artifact. A deployment guard refuses traffic without that exact target/backup/restore artifact. Missing tombstone/commit-attestation access, KMS denial, unknown locator kind, incomplete scan, backup-API ambiguity, or any match after reconciliation blocks traffic. Tests restore the same source at an older `erasure_version`, a snapshot from before the erasure run exists, a snapshot from before anchor creation, and a current pre-erasure snapshot; each remains dark until the source and every derivative are removed. A delayed-deletion fixture creates the tombstone, creates a containing backup, waits, commits database deletion, and proves that backup remains in scope until expiry even though it postdates `prepared_at`; separate fixtures cover a backup completed before the cutoff, a backup beginning before and completing after it, an ambiguous/no-start-time backup, and a fully post-cutoff backup. Only the last may be ignored. Ordinary restore, expired-request, missing-tombstone/commit-attestation, wrong-key/version, and bypass attempts fail closed. The admission tombstone and its KMS version survive permanently. The content-free commit attestation survives every possible restore plus 30 days; only after the complete containing-backup set expires and proof that no restore occurred or every restore was reconciled may the run become `verified`, after which lifecycle cleanup may remove only that attestation.
