It re-queries database, TypeSafe, both environment-name inventories, and both deployed runtime health endpoints; success requires policy revoked/all modes off, the compromised provider key irreversibly revoked, both environment entries absent, both deployments healthy with `missing_key`, and zero Jev calls. Every action is idempotent and queried before retry. A crash or ambiguous response leaves the emergency open/failed, alerts, blocks all activation, and reruns only missing steps; it never reports completion from partial evidence. The exact recovery command is `node scripts/run-typesafe-key-emergency.mjs --registered-key-id <id> --approval <sealed-artifact>`; a separate 2K-shaped rotation plus delivery, fresh owner-approved policy, deployments, and canaries is the only re-enable path. Tests fault before/after every database/provider/platform/deploy step, exercise one/both compromised targets and drifted duplicate copies, prove both deployed copies are removed, and reject create/inference/account-admin/other-key/project/variable authority.

**Write-ahead rule for every later capability transfer:** the M0I1 journal contract is mandatory for the mirrored Step 2A Vercel and Trigger delivery transfers, the Step 2K four-bootstrap provisioning transfer, Step 2L's two-bootstrap proof transfer, and every 1E transfer. Before a security administrator issues a capability or creates a secret-bearing bootstrap version, the destination workflow's pre-created content-free journal must contain and read back `planned`, exact intended slots, approval/closure digests, and deterministic operation ID. The journal advances to `issuing` before each provider issue/upload call and stores only the returned capability/bootstrap version ID before the next side effect. Recovery inventories the provider and every bootstrap resource from that write-ahead record and must destroy or reconcile an ambiguous version before reissue. A workflow whose journal is created only after capabilities exist fails its contract test and cannot run. Fault injection surrounds journal creation/read-back, every issue/upload/read-back, every redemption, and every destroy; no later paragraph may weaken this ordering.

**Required behavior**

- Shadow calls record both Jev's judgment and the unchanged current outcome.
- Shadow/current and canary comparator outcomes are written to the decision run with comparator version/status, and any generation comparator is linked by typed model-run subject. Human adjudication is append-only and is the only source for rolling safety-miss rates.
- Provider failures are visible, bounded, and non-recursive. Do not call a generative model merely to interpret a Jev error.
- Retry policy explicitly disables SDK-owned retries with `retry: { maxRetries: 0 }` and permits only the application-owned loop of one initial plus at most two retryable attempts inside the pack's total deadline. Its captured transport proves one SDK call equals one HTTP request, and each attempt must commit/finalize its own outbound lease before the next begins. Do not inherit environment retry changes or add another loop. Any retry-policy, SDK, or transport change is a reviewed config/code change that must update maximum-attempt spend reservation, lease, crash, and request-count contract tests together. Initial deadlines are 600 ms for synchronous MCP discovery and 5 seconds for asynchronous worker pilots, then may tighten from measured p95 data.
- Cost is calculated from actual input tokens, not estimated request length.
- Raw state and full response bodies are not written to general logs.
- If pending-record creation fails, do not call TypeSafe. If the provider succeeds but final audit persistence fails, discard the Jev answer, record only a redacted operational error where possible, and execute the unchanged current path. Never apply an unaudited answer.
- For stateless effects such as ranking a response, `affected_behavior=true` means the audited result is authorized and returned to the caller only after the final row update commits. For database effects such as marking messages skipped, update the decision row and business rows in one transaction; either both commit or neither does.
- Process death before finalization is treated as an unknown outcome, never success. The stale-pending reaper discards it; a retried caller follows normal idempotency/current-path rules and may issue a fresh provider request.

**Verification gate**

- Contract tests assert one success row and each failure class without leaking state/key text.
- A fake 429 and 529 produce the expected final error after bounded SDK behavior.
- A fake timeout preserves the caller's current path and records a failed attempt.
- Fault-injection tests fail pending-run insert, subject-link insert, provider completion, final decision update, and combined business-effect transaction in turn; they prove no unaudited Jev effect reaches the caller and no partial record is presented as success.
- Crash tests terminate after the pending insert and after mocked provider completion but before final update; advancing the clock runs reconciliation, atomically converts any unsettled three-attempt reservation from epoch reserved to spent exactly once, proves both rows become discarded with no effect, and proves a retry succeeds once without reusing the stale result or double-settling spend.
- Duplicate-call tests race at least 100 `runDecision` calls for one exact identity and assert one pending/success row, one transport invocation, one set of effects, and fallback/reuse for every loser; after a forced failed/discarded winner, exactly one later retry may call the provider.
- Activation-fence race tests pause a provider response, then commit emergency `off`, policy revocation, or automatic `active→shadow`; the late result must become `discarded/control_changed`, apply no skip/rerank/intervention effect, and follow current behavior. Reactivation increments generation, starts a fresh first-N canary cohort, and cannot reuse the pre-fallback decision.
- Fingerprint tests keep a human-readable decision key/version constant while changing one serialized state byte or question criterion; `input_hash` changes, the old result is not reused, and the distinct call is separately audited.
- Migration/schema verification proves tables, constraints, indexes, role/RLS topology, settings trigger, same-run adjudication FKs, mutation allowlists, and function/grant boundaries. Direct runtime/admin/`service_role` mutation of immutable fields fails. Input subjects cannot be added/changed after transport; generic output inserts fail; only successful claim-support finalization may add one allowed output atomically with its effect. Fixtures require claim-target outputs for inserted/new-held/applied-duplicate outcomes and a candidate-target `review_hold_candidate` for duplicate holds; wrong target/effect combinations, fallback/failed/discarded/shadow/wrong-use-case/second-output cases fail. Direct `jev_*` mutation fails while named transitions record events. Cross-run adjudication/head and invalid version/head combinations fail.
- Function-security tests inspect `pg_proc`, ownership, `prosecdef`, exact `proconfig`, function definitions, and ACLs for every new definer/trigger function. They prove `search_path=pg_catalog`, schema-qualified application/extension references, no dynamic identifier SQL, no `PUBLIC`/anon/service/wrong-runtime execution, exact intended caller success, hostile shadow-object resistance, and CI failure for any new unmanifested or improperly granted function/signature.
- Privacy-redaction tests delete nullable employee/reviewer/approver/run/adjudication parents that are explicitly source-independent and prove only the declared pointer nulls while bounded non-source payload, timestamps, totals, and evidence stay byte-identical. Documents and all other source anchors are excluded from this nulling path and must use the erasure saga. Direct nulling by authenticated/service/web/worker callers fails. A chat employee/channel deletion never cascades sources: one durable fan-out coordinator enumerates and locks all owned source anchors in UUID order, creates one child erasure run per source, marks the parent `pending_erasure` and unusable, then drives each ordinary saga idempotently. The parent physically deletes only after every child reaches `verified`; each child independently discards linked decision runs, closes review, and removes subjects/identifying hashes. Partial provider failure leaves the coordinator retryable with completed children immutable/idempotent and the parent disabled but present; retry resumes only incomplete children, cancellation is forbidden after any child deletion, and unrelated employees/channels/runs remain byte-identical. Crash/barrier tests cover fan-out enumeration, child creation, every child stage, final parent delete, concurrent new source creation refusal, and repeated retry. Parent-run retention may null a younger child's parent pointer without a check failure. Migration tests inspect every FK action and allowlist.
- Ordered-erasure tests seed one document with multiple chunks and one message across claim/candidate evidence, validations, joins, attachments, outlines/groups, contradiction/gap/intervention pointers, and Jev subjects. Direct parent DELETE fails. The procedure leaves zero source/chunk/evidence/join rows, preserves validations with null evidence pointer, discards linked decisions, and leaves no dangling FK. Fault injection after every phase rolls back everything; concurrent erasure, review, promotion, and retention produce one terminal outcome without deadlock/orphan.
- Parent-invalidation tests create a separate linked decision for every supported parent table and delete each through its authorized path (query origin via its anchored expiry saga; every source anchor/message/document/meeting/external/manual/query-origin object via ordered erasure; all other derived parents through that saga or their source-independent owner/retention path). Before the parent disappears, the named trigger must discard the run, dismiss/resolve review, remove remaining subjects/hashes, and prevent reuse while preserving unrelated runs. Explicit fixtures cover all six source kinds, the licensed-evaluation external anchor, assistant/meeting outputs, `model_runs`, relationship/Brain parents, every evidence/candidate layer, direct unauthorized DELETE denial, trigger ACL/owner/search-path, and rollback if invalidation fails.
- Retention/erasure tests physically delete each source type and separately transition a linked message `deleted_at` from null to set. Both message paths discard the whole run, dismiss review, remove surviving subjects, prevent reuse, and preserve unrelated evidence; clearing `deleted_at` does not revive it. Races between soft delete and pending/finalizing transport fence the late result. Replacing exact evidence has the same invalidation. An aged run referenced by an unacknowledged event deletes while the bounded event survives with null FKs.
- Admin-page tests cover open/resolved/dismissed lifecycle, append-only adjudication/supersession, rejected cross-run or stale-head supersession, allowed reason codes, canary-review enforcement, idempotent actions, redaction, typed-link disappearance, and non-admin denial. Race two writers against one expected head and prove exactly one new head while the loser receives a conflict and does not affect health. A submitted safety miss must atomically change the exact active key to `shadow` and create its control event. Banner tests seed that Jev event—not a model attempt—then prove display, link, admin-only acknowledgement, and continued visibility of unrelated unacknowledged events.
- Real-browser visual verification is mandatory for the new Decisions list/detail/policy/activation panels and health banner at desktop and mobile widths. Capture evidence that keyboard-only navigation can reach/operate actions, focus/error/success states are visible, long bounded metadata does not overflow, the banner links and acknowledges correctly, and admin/non-admin renderings match authorization. Component/server tests alone do not complete these UI steps.
- Configuration tests enumerate every named mode/model/pack/threshold/data-policy/evidence key, prove migration defaults every mode `off` with **no active policy or owner approval**, and prove no call can run before protected secret delivery plus guarded synthetic-policy creation. Missing key/posture/owner approval or a transaction fault creates neither policy nor event. Reject missing evidence/version/policy metadata and widening, deny direct generic/settings SQL transitions, and prove generic GET remains read-only. Real PostgREST JWT fixtures prove human-control RPCs derive the actor from `auth.uid()`; only the exact enrolled Albert Microsoft issuer+tenant+subject digest can create owner approvals, while another active admin or same subject under another tenant/issuer cannot. Non-admin/disabled/expired/missing/spoofed-claim/runtime/`service_role` attempts fail, and changing only email/display name/admin state cannot satisfy owner identity. Separate runtime functions cannot activate, widen, classify, or create/raise an epoch. Cover owner approval for synthetic/company policy and exact $5 cap, purpose/payload/precondition mismatch, expiry, reuse, concurrent consumption, issuer/tenant/subject mapping drift, synthetic create, company-policy replace/revoke, cookie-bound off→shadow, workflow-only shadow→active with exact manifest/authorization/login, emergency off, stale compare-and-set, retry/idempotency, and simultaneous-admin races. Every success creates exactly one event; every failure leaves approvals/policy/settings/generation/evidence unchanged; replacement/revocation atomically demotes dependents and fences in-flight results. UI integration proves incomplete posture/fingerprint cannot activate. Document-classification tests prove direct insert/update reject `public_synthetic` alone or mixed while the authenticated-admin RPC exposes only six non-synthetic classes. Fingerprint drift forces shadow, clears evidence, increments generation, discards late results, and requires new exact evidence; resolved-model drift does the same.
- Policy-replacement fixtures run against the real immediate partial unique index and complete setting set. They prove the locked revoke-old→insert-new→rebind-all-dependents order commits once, pins every dependent `<key>_data_policy_version` to the new active ID, demotes/clears/increments each exactly once, rejects old-version late results, never exposes two active rows or a setting pinned to a revoked policy, rolls back to the old active row and old bindings on any fault, rejects a newly appeared/unlisted/stale dependent, requires the fresh exact owner approval, and serializes concurrent replacement/call-finalization/setting-creation races.
- Target-credential fixtures install distinct fake Vercel and Trigger keys, prove each built runtime accepts only its code-owned target entry, and reject a missing entry, swapped keys, one shared key, caller/environment/database-selected target, cross-runtime artifact, stale deployment, or one-target rotation under the prior policy. Built Next and Trigger artifacts must contain exactly their different expected target literal and no target fallback/opposite literal. Adding/rotating Trigger atomically replaces the ordered map and dependent setting bindings, forces both targets to shadow, rejects every in-flight old-map result, and requires successful target-specific canaries before either target can reactivate. Delivery-workflow tests prove each fresh hosted job can read only its one TypeSafe field/key ID/issuance evidence and one exact write-only platform credential, mutate only `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` in the named project/environment, cannot deploy/read back/cross-target/administer, and leaves no secret in GitHub, logs, artifacts, runner files, or persistent hosts. Fake and live provider-contract probes reject expected-account text without cryptographic/introspection proof, wrong organization/account, valid other-account key substitution, swapped key IDs, forged/stale issuance evidence, wrong verification-key/schema version, and inference-success-only claims; positive paths bind both distinct keys to the same exact approved account/posture before emitting target-labelled fingerprints.
- Spend-control tests capture the final SDK request and live-synthetic usage to prove the pinned ceiling includes all request/protocol/model overhead. Unit vectors assert `0→0`, `1→1`, `23→1`, `24→2`, `64,000→2,688` microusd per attempt and exactly `8,064` for three 64,000-token attempts, using checked integer arithmetic with overflow rejection. Race 100 distinct calls with remaining balance one microusd below, exactly at, and above the 8,064-microusd three-attempt ceiling: below makes zero network requests; admitted calls serialize reservations and cannot exceed the cap under the contract. Mock attempt-1/attempt-3 success, all-retry failure, crashes after attempts 1/2/3, provider usage exactly at the ceiling, and an impossible ceiling+1 report. Normal paths settle the full reservation once and leave none stranded; ceiling+1 debits reported actual, forces every mode off, blocks subsequent calls, and raises the explicit external-overrun alert rather than claiming the application cap held. Zero/missing/spent epoch and day/week/process restart never refill it. Decision-run retention leaves permanent spent totals unchanged. Only a fresh exact owner-identity approval may close/create any replacement epoch, with one control event.

**Natural cuts:** Step 1A is the manifest/schema/role migration proof, including dormant provenance and legacy-ingress shape. The single Step 1B/2 outcome lands the client and complete observability/cost/failure contract plus real-database role regression with no Jev behavior; neither half may merge or close alone. Step 1C cuts Trigger to the worker role and proves its full regression group; Step 1D separately cuts Vercel to the web role and proves its group. The former Step 1E is an umbrella only. Each 1E1–1E8 implementation, including 1E4U, starts from current main in its own worktree, has one exact-head-reviewed implementation PR, and stops after merge/deployment with its STATUS row still awaiting proof. A fresh session then runs only that row's command against the merged main/deployment, publishes the immutable artifact, and opens a documentation-only `-C` PR that records merge SHA, deployment ID, command/closure digest, target aliases, random synthetic run IDs, pass/fail/negative-probe counts, artifact URL/digest, timestamps, and reviewer verdict without source/provider identifiers. Only the merged `-C` row unlocks the next implementation. 1E4 and 1E4U are separate infrastructure and live-application outcomes with separate proofs. 1E9 is itself the one composed public-synthetic erasure outcome after 1E8-C, using the captured fake for TypeSafe while exercising every other named live surface; 1E9-C records that bounded result and cannot claim live TypeSafe lifecycle proof. A failed proof leaves the implementation row open, disables or rolls back only that milestone's newly introduced route/grants, opens one owned correction issue, and cannot be bundled into the next step. No implementation PR may claim post-merge evidence; no closeout may change code, configuration, permissions, or workflows. Recovery resumes only the current step's durable state using its named idempotent command; drift requires a same-shaped correction PR, fresh exact-head review, rerun, and new closeout. No pilot starts until both database cutovers removed the migration credential and 2L-C is merged. After 1E9-C, the separate 2A/2A-C dormant activation-control outcomes run, followed by 2K/2K-C credential provisioning and 2L/2L-C live TypeSafe lifecycle proof, before 3A; none before 2L performs a Jev call or mode transition.

| Proof closeout | Exact post-merge command | Required immutable artifact |
|---|---|---|
| 1E1-C | `pnpm verify:source-provenance-envelope-live -- --assert-deployment <sha>` | Exact deployed SHA; exhaustive typed/static/runtime callsite manifest for every provider, embedding, file/cache/batch, and dispatch contract; ID-only Trigger/Vercel probes; pre/post Jev-off behavior parity; no lease enforcement |
| 1E2-C | `pnpm verify:source-lineage-and-lease-cutover-live -- --fixture fixed-public-synthetic` | Zero ambiguous lineage; one enforcement generation; captured before-I/O lease proof and missing-provenance refusal for every provider/request class; legacy-path parity; rollback restores the wholly prior generation |
| 1E3-C | `node scripts/verify-source-erasure-control-plane.mjs --commit <merge-sha>` | Workflow/closure digests; request-secret round trip; every deletion/provider/finalizer permission denied |
| 1E4-C | `node scripts/verify-source-erasure-infrastructure.mjs --commit <merge-sha>` | KMS/bucket retention, tombstone read-back, backup-metadata reads, restore dry-scan, cross-identity denials, and no finalizer dispatch |
| 1E4U-C | `pnpm run verify:source-admission-live -- --commit <merge-sha>` | Machine-checked full writer inventory; exact Vercel/Trigger deployed closures; every message/import/document/meeting/Teams/Recall/external/manual/query-origin caller healthy+tombstoned+outage proof; derived-writer lineage denial; object/row/effect retry idempotency; lull/reply exactly-once; and 24-hour orphan cleanup |
| 1E5-C | `node scripts/verify-source-erasure-ai-storage.mjs --run-id <random-public-synthetic-run-id>` | Per-provider delete-or-expire results and negative inference/create/cross-project probes; no database finalizer |
| 1E6-C | `node scripts/verify-source-erasure-platform-history.mjs --run-id <random-public-synthetic-run-id>` | Both Recall bot-creation routes, bot/recording/transcript/chat inventory and delete-or-expire (including ambiguous create/late resources/pagination/active-meeting races), Teams receipt deletion, and Trigger/Vercel delete-or-expire plus create/post/deploy/config/cross-account denial probes |
| 1E7-C | `pnpm --filter @oracle/db run verify:source-erasure-finalizer-live -- --run-id <random-public-synthetic-run-id>` | Guarded release ID; six-kind/derived/business-state/fan-out/idempotency proof; provider execution still disabled |
| 1E8-C | `npx tsx scripts/prepare-oracle-source-erasure-resume.ts --run-id <random-public-synthetic-run-id>` | Fresh-approved isolated restore into a non-production `VerifiedRestoreTarget`; pre-run/pre-anchor/old-schema reconciliation from permanent tombstones; exact-host credential scope/revocation; before/at/after-retention resumption; and dark-traffic proof |
| 1E9-C | `node scripts/verify-oracle-source-erasure-e2e.mjs --run-id <random-public-synthetic-run-id>` | One complete composed source path, all prior artifact digests, final negative retrieval, backup-safe verification, and exact activation generation |

Every command is created and fake-transport tested in its implementation PR, accepts only content-free IDs/commit arguments, exits nonzero on a missing/failed/ambiguous probe, and writes the artifact through the existing GitHub-attested evidence channel. The closeout session retrieves it through GitHub, independently verifies its signature/digest and exact main/deployment SHA, runs `ai-task-gates check --before ship`, and records it in this STATUS table plus the step's evidence section before the docs-only merge.

### Phase B — Lowest-risk product pilot

- Spend-epoch lifecycle tests use real PostgREST JWTs and the actual partial unique index/RPC grants: an active admin with matching owner-evidence digest creates the first $5 (`5000000` microusd) epoch and one event; exact retry is byte-identical; concurrent/second create loses without effects; reservations then admit one bounded synthetic call. Close-only prevents new reservations while preserving totals. Close-and-replace atomically closes old, creates the approved new cap, and writes one event; injected fault rolls back both. Stale evidence/current epoch, cap increase without fresh owner evidence, and non-admin/disabled/expired/missing/spoofed/runtime/`service_role`/direct SQL attempts create nothing. Green completion ends with one active epoch, immutable closed history, unchanged prior spend, and a successful authorized reservation—never permanently `budget_blocked`.
- Source-erasure authorization tests use the real protected-workflow contract with fake TypeSafe, Supabase, Vertex, OpenAI, Anthropic, Google Gemini, DeepSeek, Qwen, Recall, Microsoft Graph, Trigger, Vercel, and GitHub transports plus a disposable database: only exact approved main-workflow identities may perform their isolated prepare/verify/provider-delete/finalize/restore-reconcile actions; direct web/worker/service/admin SQL, deployment/admin credentials, every normal Jev/inference/embedding key, and an unapproved wrapper fail. The manifest verifier rejects a preparer that substitutes the anchor, source kind, stable key, version, locator, dependency, replica, resource, or action while retaining the same visible counts. Successful cases for all six source kinds—including query-origin expiry—and the licensed-evaluation external-anchor fixture remove job/map/decision/evidence/assistant/meeting/derived copies, provider requests/results, external replicas, lineage, locators/plain hashes/transient comparison HMACs, and the temporary request while preserving only the explicitly approved access-locked permanent admission tombstone while emitting one random-ID/count/code/timestamp attestation; replay is idempotent, the repository fixture remains unchanged, and GitHub retrieval contains no sentinel. Separate document cases prove same-intent retry remains blocked after erasure while distinct byte-identical upload intents coexist and erasing one leaves the other unchanged. Tombstone upload timeout, partial object, lost response, changed retry bytes, stale generation, failed independent read-back, and deletion attempted before the durable attestation all leave the source and request intact. TypeSafe retries plus every synchronous generative-provider/embedding path have before-reserve/after-reserve/between-retries/in-flight/late-response/crash races proving freeze drains or retains an `unknown` blocker and never misses a post-freeze copy; TypeSafe request-ID absence, ZDR drift, inaccessible account logs, or retained response blocks. Restores from before an erasure run, before anchors, of an older source version, and of a pre-anchor legacy row remain dark, derive the same stable-key tombstone, rerun the complete deletion closure, and produce a zero-match artifact before traffic; no run reaches `verified` while Supabase reports a containing backup/PITR point. Stale anchor/dependency/replica digest, widened target, active-call/batch/origin-expiry race, shared-file partial result, unclassified legacy JSON/quotation/derived field, fault at every phase, missing/expired tombstone visibility, wrong KMS key/version, restore bypass, timeout, concurrent review/promotion/retention, or approval reuse blocks/rolls back and emits no success.

#### Step 3A — MCP capability discovery shadow delivery

**Files/functions**

- `apps/web/lib/mcp/registry.ts:67-98`: keep existing lexical/synonym scoring as fallback and comparison baseline.
- Add `apps/web/lib/mcp/decision-capability-search.ts`: build a bounded `Choice` over currently enabled capabilities plus `none`; state contains only the user's capability query and non-sensitive capability names/descriptions.
- Add `buildPublicSyntheticMcpProbe()` beside it with a checked-in fixed synthetic catalog containing only literal fake capability IDs/names/descriptions such as `synthetic_lookup` and `synthetic_status`, one fixed literal query, one pinned exact-byte hash, and `eval_case_key='mcp-capability-public-probe-v1'`. It exercises the decision client seam only and never reads, hashes, names, describes, or routes against the environment's live capability registry. The builder alone creates `origin_kind='synthetic'`; it verifies the exact catalog/query UTF-8 bytes before granting `public_synthetic`, sends no caller content, and refuses any extra or changed byte. Ordinary MCP requests—including the real enabled-capability catalog, a caller supplying the same words, or a claimed synthetic flag—remain `mcp_request`, use real provenance, and receive the full conservative free-text class union; they are blocked under synthetic-only policy.
- For every MCP Jev call, create a short-lived `mcp_request` query origin and its unique `query_origin` source anchor in one transaction, link both to the decision, and pass that exact anchor/version to `begin_source_outbound` before transport. Its stable tombstone key is `query_origin || NUL || UUID-bytes` of the immutable origin ID, and restore reconciliation resolves that exact typed FK rather than any query text/hash. The static bearer token cannot identify a person, so expiry within 24 hours plus one existing ten-minute schedule interval freezes that anchor, drains/reconciles every provider lease, erases the provider request/result or proves ZDR/inaccessible expiry, then deletes the origin/anchor/decision; a missing or unversioned anchor blocks the call. Positive fake-transport/disposable-database lifecycle tests classify a synthetic sentinel with the ordinary MCP conservative class union, capture its provider-request lease, expire the origin, and prove freeze prevents a concurrent retry and no provider/database copy survives. The real public-synthetic probe separately proves transport wiring but cannot stand in for this anchored lifecycle test; no company data is sent.

Capability names are unique: pin that exact match first, and if `limit=1` return it unchanged; Jev may rank only the remaining slots and can never displace it, regardless of probability. A disabled exact match receives no privilege. For remaining slots, remove `none`, intersect with enabled capabilities, sort by probability descending, break ties with existing lexical score then stable capability ID, apply the remaining limit, and return no duplicates. Dispatch only `.github/workflows/typesafe-vercel-secret-delivery.yml`; no local CLI/API injection or deployment credential may write the key. Verify its cryptographic/introspection account proof, target-labelled fingerprint artifact, and encrypted name/target presence; after Albert's exact owner approval described in Step 0, create the active data policy with only the `vercel_production` map entry and bind the MCP setting to that policy ID, then use the separate deployment workflow to deploy the merged Step 3A SHA. The deployed Vercel bundle must prove immutable target `vercel_production`, independently attest the same loaded-key fingerprint/account/policy ID, and pass its public-synthetic probe before shadow traffic. A pre-secret or stale deployment does not count. Preview gets a distinct synthetic-only key through a separately scoped workflow only if tests require one; Production credentials are never copied into Preview.

**Acceptance criteria**

- No disabled capability can be returned, regardless of Jev output.
- `none` and low-confidence results fall back to lexical behavior.
- The sealed MCP held-out set has at least 400 cases: 100 exact-name/alias, 100 ambiguous or multi-intent, 100 `none`/disabled-capability negatives, and 100 long-tail phrasing cases, with no case reused from tuning. Exact-name/alias and disabled-capability safety are 100%; top-1 accuracy must exceed the lexical baseline by at least 5 percentage points with a two-sided 95% bootstrap interval whose lower bound is above zero; top-k recall at `limit=1`, `limit=2`, and full-list may not regress in any stratum; false positive capability returns are at most 1% overall and zero for disabled tools.
- Added decision latency must be p95 ≤750 ms and p99 ≤1,500 ms on the held-out run, fallback success must be 100%, and measured spend plus every other accepted pilot must remain within the active $5 epoch. Any missed floor stays shadow; the threshold is not weakened after seeing held-out results.
- Safety-tier confirmation and tool argument validation remain unchanged.

**Verification gate**

- `pnpm --filter @oracle/web verify:mcp` and `pnpm run verify:vercel-guards` pass.
- The shadow eval artifact is committed.
- Vercel's production environment listing proves `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` exists without revealing its value; the post-secret production deployment ID resolves to the merged Step 3A SHA, and the exact code-owned `buildPublicSyntheticMcpProbe()` path—not an arbitrary MCP request—records the expected resolved model/cost audit. Missing-key behavior remains covered by network-free tests and fails to the lexical path.
- Production remains `shadow`; the live endpoint proves the decision/audit seam while responses stay byte-identical to lexical behavior. Active behavior is reserved for 3B.
- If code lands without live proof, open exactly one leftover-proof issue for this step before ending that session.

**Natural cut:** stop after the 3A shadow artifact is accepted or recorded as rejected. Do not activate or add entity selection in the same session.

#### Step 3B/3B-C — MCP capability-search activation and closeout

Its separate exact-head-reviewed PR changes only `config/jev-activation-manifests/mcp-capability-search.json`; after merge/deploy, a fresh sealed technical APPROVE authorizes the protected activation workflow to perform the exact `shadow→active` transition and run benign, ambiguous, no-match, exact-name, and disabled-capability canaries against the live endpoint. Failure automatically returns only this use case to shadow.
- 3B-C is a separate documentation-only current-main session that verifies the immutable activation artifact, live generation/fingerprint, deployment SHA, lexical rollback readiness, cost/latency, and no safety rollback; it updates only STATUS/evidence prose.

**Acceptance criteria**

- Jev can never invent or return an entity outside the supplied registry candidates.
- Every supplied entity candidate is linked and conservatively classified before transport; deleting or changing any candidate discards/prevents reuse of the whole decision, and unknown or unapproved entity data is blocked before network access.
- Explicitly named valid entities have 100% recall in the frozen fixture before active use.
- Low-confidence candidates preserve current behavior; no negative Jev result becomes a hard retrieval exclusion.
- Because the initial active pilot still runs the current selector, it is a quality/measurement pilot, not a cost-saving replacement. Replacing that selector requires a new STATUS row and separate plan after held-out evidence proves recall and defines a safe non-Jev fallback.
- Chinese is separately scored; if it misses the bar, `zh-CN` remains on the current path.

**Verification gate**

- `pnpm --filter @oracle/ai verify:entity-aware-retrieval` passes.
- The live fixture passes only after vendor/data approval; otherwise the step remains shadow-only.
- The Jev decision run records both the shadow selection and the current selector's bounded comparator outcome/attempt metadata under `entity_query_key`; success and failure fixtures prove this without inserting a generation `model_run`. If the current selector fails, current deterministic fallback remains authoritative and the comparator status is `failed`.
- The route verifier proves a three-turn follow-up links three ordered user messages, topic reset links current only, identical text uses IDs, assistants never link, locale/scope changes alter identity, and every candidate—including negative/unselected—is linked once in deterministic order. A separate DB connection sees the committed origin/run/reservation/full subjects before transport. Missing/stale/wrong-channel contributors, revoked membership, spoofed scope/locale, missing/stale candidate, duplicate subject, and >100 candidates yield zero calls/current result; no candidates means no Jev call. Editing/deleting each linked message/entity/origin invalidates reuse.

**Files/functions**

Link the anchor/version as an input subject and pass it to `begin_source_outbound`; the decision key additionally hashes the offered candidate IDs/text. Positive fake-provider tests prove retry reuses the same anchor, freeze blocks a concurrent call, and authorized anchor erasure deletes/reconciles the provider lease, decision/input hashes, and Oracle copies while leaving the governed repository fixture unchanged.

The restored live R2 map is 23/30; 27/30 is the unchanged end-to-end acceptance threshold, not existing evidence. Do not attribute complete responsibility-record correctness to this quote-only evaluator.

**Acceptance criteria**

- On the frozen quote-selection fixture, select every ground-truth supporting quote (100% support-quote recall), select no quote that fails the exact-quote/support label (100% precision on the high-risk set), and preserve every previously correct quote-selection case. Report ambiguous/no-supported cases separately.
- Reconstructing and scoring full role/action/object responsibility records is out of scope for this evaluator. Any future production replacement must separately rerun and pass the unchanged 27/30 end-to-end R2 record gate plus production replay; quote-level success alone cannot authorize it.
- Show material cost/latency improvement against the recorded GPT-4.1 baseline.

**Verification gate**

- Existing `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:r2-responsibilities` and `verify:r2-production-replay` remain byte-identical.
- The new Jev evaluator passes without importing its outputs into the live reader.
- Advancing from evaluation to production requires a new, separately scoped plan/status row after the active R2 handoff is settled.

**Natural cut:** stop after the evaluation artifact. This plan does not authorize replacing the live R2 quote path.

### Phase D — High-volume savings and independent safety checks

#### Step 6A/6B/6C — Claim-extraction schema, prefilter shadow mode, then separately gated activation

**Step 6A — schema-only release**

- In `packages/db/src/schema.ts`, the generated migration, and reserved `packages/db/migrations/sql/104_jev_extraction_membership.sql`, add `messages.extraction_directive` (`normal|force_extract`, default `normal`), nullable capture source/time/requester, plus `content_version` and `content_hash`; add batch snapshots/status and normalized membership. Constraints require capture fields null for normal and a matching user/requester for forced capture. This migration is backward-compatible: it must not revoke or block `messages.content` on INSERT. Existing chat replies, imports, attachments, transcript/live-meeting ingestion, contradiction interventions, lull interjections, and ordinary message creation keep inserting their current columns unchanged. A security-invoker `BEFORE INSERT` trigger accepts legacy inserts, forces omitted capture fields to `normal`/null, and database-derives version 1/hash. Revoke INSERT/UPDATE only for capture audit/version/hash columns; keep manifest-required INSERT for content, role, metadata/status, and other existing fields. Revoke direct UPDATE of content (there is no current content-update writer) and reserve future edits for the named owner RPC. Fixed-path cookie-authenticated create/edit RPCs derive employee/membership; only the create RPC may set forced capture, while ordinary direct writers never can.
- Before Step 6A production migration, run compatibility fixtures through `api/chat`, `api/admin/import`, `api/documents`, `api/messages`, `teams-transcript-ingestion`, both `teams-live-recall-utterance` insert paths, `contradiction-watcher`, and `lull-interjection` under the exact web/worker login. Assert unchanged content/role/status/metadata, normal capture, version 1/hash, and no permission error. Run every existing message-status update fixture to prove protected-column revocation does not block extraction reconciliation. Repeat on a production-snapshot rehearsal and unchanged deployed code after migration.
- The edit function locks the message then every current-version membership/owner in UUID order. It rejects edits once any completed extraction/candidate/claim evidence depends on that version (the user must add a correction message). Otherwise one transaction increments content version/hash, discards pending/succeeded decision runs linked to the old version, moves old-version `skipped_by_decision` owners to `replay_queued` and clears skip snapshots, and returns the message as pending for the existing drain; reconciliation ignores memberships whose recorded version/hash does not match the message. A concurrent worker may complete before the edit lock and cause a clean conflict, or the edit wins and fences the stale worker—never a stale skip. Merge/migrate/prove these schema/functions/policies before dependent web/worker code; no new reader imports them earlier.
- Add `extraction_batch_messages(extraction_batch_id uuid FK ON DELETE CASCADE, message_id uuid FK ON DELETE CASCADE, message_content_version integer, message_content_hash text, position integer NOT NULL CHECK (position>=0), PRIMARY KEY(extraction_batch_id,message_id), UNIQUE(extraction_batch_id,position))` plus index `(message_id,message_content_version,extraction_batch_id)`. Backfill from ordered `source_message_ids` and current message versions/hashes. A compatibility trigger validates legacy JSON UUID arrays and mirrors inserts/updates until every writer migrates; JSON remains compatibility/audit data, never reconciliation authority.
- Add `skipped_by_decision` and `replay_queued` to the canonical extraction-batch status/check and every derived list. Add nullable `skip_decision_run_id` (`ON DELETE SET NULL`), pack/threshold/data-policy versions, decision key, and decided timestamp. A check requires the complete snapshot exactly for `skipped_by_decision` and every field null for `replay_queued`.
- In the same Step 6A PR, update `config/runtime-db-access.json` for every new `messages` column/function and `extraction_batch_messages` operation. Add explicit RLS/policies or function-only access, exact web/worker CRUD/EXECUTE grants, regression IDs, and fresh/snapshot effective-privilege comparisons; the global scanner must pass before migration. No later phase may rely on the Step 1 manifest without extending it for its own schema.

**Step 6B — dependent worker/web delivery**

- In `apps/workers/src/trigger/claim-extraction.ts` and `claim-extraction-batch-submit.ts`, stage one owner plus normalized memberships for every window before decision. Use the same helper in sync/batch. Shadow/canary/forced windows continue to the current model; only an active confident-negative non-canary window becomes `skipped_by_decision` with no provider request.
- In `apps/web/app/api/messages/route.ts`, change the strict request body to `{channelId,content,intent?:'message'|'capture'}` and never accept directive, actor, source, requester, timestamp, hash, or version. After `requireEmployee`, call the cookie-bound create RPC; the database revalidates participant/active channel and derives capture only from typed intent or exact anchored `/capture ` followed by non-empty text, stripping only that prefix. Capture cannot target an existing message, another employee, or another channel; near matches, quoted commands, metadata, imports, Teams, assistants, and ordinary messages remain normal. Any future edit endpoint must use the versioned edit RPC and surface its completed-evidence conflict; no direct update path is allowed.
- In `apps/web/app/channels/[channelId]/_components/channel-chat.tsx`, add a composer-level “Save as knowledge” toggle beside Send with `aria-pressed`, concise explanatory text, and a visible captured badge. Snapshot the toggle with the draft, send only `intent:'capture'`, reset draft/toggle only after success, restore both on failure, and use the API's normalized content/capture fields for optimistic display. Add capture fields to the shared `ChatMessage` shape, initial channel-page selection, API response, and Realtime row mapping so initial, optimistic, and live views agree. `/capture ...` remains an exact command path without the toggle.
- Include message content version/hash and the durable directive in input identity and normalized membership. Any window containing `force_extract` bypasses Jev before transport and reaches the unchanged extractor. An allowed edit preserves force capture, invalidates the old decision/version atomically, and requeues; a post-extraction edit is rejected. No natural-language phrase such as “remember this” grants bypass.
- Refactor `apps/workers/src/lib/message-extraction-status.ts` to accept message IDs, lock them in UUID order, and join normalized membership to **every** owner across all job runs. Exclude only `replay_queued`; never filter by current `job_run_id`. A message is complete if any current owner completed, skipped only if all current owners skipped, failed only if all non-skipped owners failed and none completed, otherwise processing. Owner creation, membership insert, processing, skip, replay, and reconciliation use the same ordered locks/transaction.
- Update admin status consumers and exhaustive tests. Track skipped batches separately in batch submission; failure cleanup may reset only pending submitted owners. An all-skipped selection succeeds without a provider batch job.
- Link each decision to its owner and every source message/version. Atomically finalize decision, subjects, skip status, and reconciled messages only after rechecking current content versions/hashes under the same ordered locks. The key hashes ordered message IDs/versions/content/directives; an edit fences a stale worker and cannot reuse a skip.
- Fence asynchronous provider completion too. `claim-extraction-batch-submit.ts` writes the normalized membership IDs plus expected message version/hash and owner generation into durable batch membership; legacy `input_json` UUID arrays become non-authoritative compatibility data. Before `claim-extraction-batch-drain.ts` parses/applies any provider result, it locks the owner and every normalized member in canonical order, reloads current message bytes/version/hash/directive/source-anchor version, and requires exact equality with submission plus the still-current batch/provider request. A mismatch atomically marks that result `stale_source_version`, applies no candidate/claim/evidence/status effect to edited content, cancels/invalidate its decision/replica linkage, and queues the current versions through the ordinary extraction path. Completion-versus-edit barrier tests pause after provider completion and before each database write, proving an old result can never land on a new message even when `input_json` still names the same UUID.
- Add `scripts/replay-jev-extraction-skips.ts`, dry-run by default. Select from durable snapshots, lock normalized memberships/messages, change the old owner to `replay_queued`, clear skip fields, and reset only messages still reconciled skipped. It never depends on a retained decision run or resets complete/processing/unlinked rows.
- Create independent Nouls for operational fact, decision, rule/exception, metric, and process/handoff detail. Do not initially change document ingestion. Add the governed mode/threshold settings, verifier, and eval artifact.
- Before the first deployed Trigger shadow call, obtain a sealed technical production approval binding the merged **Step 6B** SHA, twenty-root/2A-C workflow closure, 2K-C credential artifact, 2L-C live TypeSafe lifecycle artifact, TypeSafe item field/key ID/issuance evidence for `trigger_prod_api_key`, organization/account/posture fingerprint, Trigger project `proj_wgpzsvhmsopqhvwqaycn`, `prod`, variable name, expiry, and nonce. Dispatch only `.github/workflows/typesafe-trigger-secret-delivery.yml`; no local injection or deployment credential may write the key. Verify the cryptographic/introspection account proof, target-labelled fingerprint artifact, and secret name/target presence. Then require Albert's fresh exact owner approval for the displayed unchanged class/vendor terms plus the old-policy digest, new two-target map digest, and complete dependent-setting set. The guarded replacement transaction consumes it, inserts the two-target policy, rewrites every dependent setting to that new policy ID, and forces every dependent Vercel mode to shadow; any missing/revoked binding blocks before canaries. Redeploy Vercel and Trigger from their exact applicable merged SHAs, require each runtime bundle/target to attest only its own mapped fingerprint and current policy ID, rerun the Vercel public-synthetic canary under the replacement policy, and run the code-owned public-synthetic worker probe. Trigger shadow stays blocked and no Vercel mode can reactivate until both target-specific canaries and their fresh review evidence pass.

**Ground truth and acceptance**

- Build disjoint tuning/held-out data with boilerplate, implicit rules, negation, exceptions, natural-language capture requests, short valuable facts, irrelevant chatter, and sensitive content. Any reviewed operational claim is positive even when the old extractor returned zero. Require 100% recall on at least 50 high-value positives within 200 held-out segments and at least 30% confident coverage of true negatives, or reject activation.
- In active mode, send the first 50 weekly confident negatives then every tenth later negative through the current extractor as canaries; review disagreements and the reserved agreement sample. One missed high-value claim or more than 1% adjudicated false negatives over the latest 100 atomically returns only this use case to shadow.
- Persist comparator model run/candidates and bounded outcome. Direct uploads, durable `force_extract`, low confidence, unsupported language, provider failure, and missing key preserve the current extractor. Natural-language capture wording is a high-value positive test, not a trusted directive.

**Verification gate**

- Typecheck and the verifier pass in both dispatch modes. Handler/RPC tests cover unauthenticated/nonparticipant/inactive channel, malformed/privileged fields, exact/empty command, composer/ordinary paths, actor spoofing, and version conflict. Direct authenticated/service/web/worker SQL inserts that supply `force_extract`, requester/source/time, version, or hash fail; omitted fields become normal/version-1/database hash. Direct protected updates fail; only signed create/edit RPC can capture/edit. Barrier races cover edit versus pending/negative/provider completion. Forced pre-extraction edits preserve force/zero suppression; post-extraction edits fail. UI tests cover toggle restore, badge, Realtime/initial parity, and conflict guidance. Add real-browser visual proof at desktop and mobile widths: keyboard Tab/Space/Enter operates the toggle/send path with visible focus and `aria-pressed`; injected send failure restores the exact draft/toggle; optimistic, server response, initial load, and Realtime render the same captured badge/content; conflict guidance remains readable with no layout overflow. Imports, Teams/assistants, quoting, and near matches remain normal.
- Cross-run fixtures create owners in two/three job runs: late old-run completion beats skip/failure for the same version; old-version completion never wins after an edit; replay/edit/new extraction serialize; concurrent sync/batch order converges. Backfill/compatibility rows equal JSON and current hashes; duplicate membership/position, bad FK/UUID/hash, and orphan writes fail; `EXPLAIN` uses the message/version/owner index.
- Batch failures preserve committed skips; all-skipped work creates no provider custom ID. Recovery survives decision retention and crashes around replay commit without ambiguous status or lost membership.
- Trigger `prod` names-only secret proof, post-secret deployment tied to merged Step 6B SHA, and public-synthetic shadow audit pass. Real approved shadow telemetry measures false negatives without raw text logs. Only tracked Step 6C may perform the active switch, and 6C-C must record its live proof.

**Step 6C/6C-C — separately activate and record extraction prefilter**

- 6C starts only after the merged 6B artifact satisfies the sealed held-out and shadow thresholds with no open safety miss. In a new exact-head-reviewed PR it changes only the extraction use-case activation manifest; it does not mutate live mode, settings, or generation. Run `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:jev-extraction-activation -- --assert-head <sha> --evidence <6b-artifact>` before merge and again on the merged SHA; then deploy that SHA, obtain the sealed single-use technical APPROVE, dispatch the protected activation workflow to apply the manifest, and run one bounded public-synthetic plus one approved non-sensitive extraction flow with canary comparison and automatic shadow rollback armed. The immutable artifact binds PR/merge/deployment SHA and ID, config generation/fingerprint, 6B evidence digest, command digest, source class, decision/effect counts, cost/latency, canary result, and rollback state without source text.
- 6C-C is a separate current-main documentation-only session. It runs `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:jev-extraction-activation-live -- --artifact <path>`, checks live config/deployment/generation and no safety rollback, records the artifact/issue, and changes only STATUS/evidence prose. Failed proof atomically returns extraction prefilter to shadow and leaves 6C open; 7A cannot absorb or repair it.

Numeric ordering guarantees existing `13_extraction_constraints.sql` runs first and cannot overwrite these final definitions. **Step 7B — files/functions**

- Extend `config/runtime-db-access.json` again for each new candidate/claim admin action, shared transaction helper, decision finalizer, and erasure path before code merges; exact real-login positive and cross-role/direct-SQL negative tests are part of the 7B gate.
