The document path remains off when durable classification is absent/unknown/wider than policy.
- Refactor `packages/oracle-engines/src/extraction/promotion-executor.ts` into `executePromotionInTransaction(tx,input)` plus its convenience wrapper, and add a transaction-aware decision-fence helper that never opens a nested transaction. Refactor `apps/workers/src/lib/document-claim-auto-approval.ts` into a pure eligibility decision plus `applyDocumentClaimAutoApprovalInTransaction(tx,...)`. In `document-ingestion.ts`, one advisory-lock transaction reloads/fences the disposition after the fresh candidate/evidence snapshot and existing-claim lookup, then executes promotion, attaches decision subjects/effect, applies map/domain/entity mutations, applies or vetoes auto-approval, writes validation audit, and finalizes candidate status. No claim/candidate mutation commits before the fence, and no later helper may approve outside this transaction. Stale/mismatched evidence/config uses explicit fallback. Never perform a post-promotion check whose failure would require deleting appended evidence.
- For `insert_new_claim`, a successfully audited `allow` preserves normal insertion; a successfully audited `review` creates only `pending_review`, sets `review_held`, and vetoes document auto-approval. For duplicates, audited review leaves the existing claim/evidence/entities/domains unchanged and holds the candidate. `fallback`—including timeout, provider/audit failure, malformed response, low confidence, unsupported language, missing key, or stale disposition—preserves the **entire current promotion path**, including existing document auto-approval eligibility; it records failure/fallback but creates no hold or backlog. Only a successful audited review outcome may reduce promotion.
- Add `apps/web/app/admin/ai/candidates/_actions.ts` and extend `page.tsx` with a counted `review_held` tab plus admin-only **Accept duplicate** and **Reject candidate** actions. These actions resolve held duplicates that have no claim-review row. Accept takes the same candidate-hash advisory lock as promotion; requires `review_held`, `review_hold_kind='duplicate_claim'`, expected decision run/config fingerprint and duplicate target, plus unchanged candidate/evidence/source hashes; then re-runs source/quote existence, taxonomy, sensitivity, and current duplicate-target validation. One transaction appends evidence/entities exactly once, records human adjudication/validation, clears `review_hold_kind`, and marks the candidate `duplicate`. Reject records immutable adjudication/reason, clears `review_hold_kind`, and changes only the candidate to `rejected` while retaining the bounded duplicate target for audit. Stale/deleted/reclassified evidence, target drift, or failed validation leaves it held with no partial write. Workers never resolve or retry `review_held`.
- Extend `apps/web/app/admin/claims/_actions.ts:updateClaimStatus`, used by both claims pages, to resolve linked new-claim holds. Inside its existing transaction, lock the pending claim and every candidate with `promoted_to_claim_id=claim.id AND status='review_held' AND review_hold_kind='new_claim'`, using a shared adjudication-health helper. Approval revalidates surviving evidence, records human `supported/allow`, resolves the decision review, clears `review_hold_kind`, marks linked candidates `promoted`, records validation results, then approves the claim. Rejection records `unsupported/reject`, resolves review, clears `review_hold_kind` plus candidate `promoted_to_claim_id/promoted_at`, marks linked candidates `rejected`, records validations, then rejects the claim; the immutable decision output subject and claim-review event retain the audit link. Require the claim still be `pending_review`; double-submit is idempotent and concurrent approve/reject permits one winner. Ordinary pending claims retain current behavior. Failed approval revalidation leaves claim/candidate/decision unchanged and visibly reviewable; rejection remains available so no hold is stranded.
- Migration 105 atomically upgrades the installed erasure handler/manifest from `pre105-v1` to `post105-v2` in the same guarded transaction that adds `review_held`; the new version retains every existing state transition and adds both held variants. Clean-install and current-production-snapshot tests invoke erasure immediately before and after the upgrade, while a barrier fixture starts erasure across the migration boundary; the old handler can never observe new columns/statuses and the new handler cannot install without them. Source erasure uses the same locks before deleting any linked decision. Every affected held candidate becomes `rejected` with bounded `source_erased` reason plus a `source_erasure_cleanup` validation result: held-new clears `review_hold_kind` and its candidate promotion fields while rejecting the linked pending claim; held-duplicate clears `review_hold_kind` and leaves the existing duplicate target claim unchanged unless that claim is itself in the affected derived chain. Every affected claim version—approved or pending, with or without other evidence—is invalidated and physically deleted with its summary, embedding, translations, and downstream Brain/relationship/recommendation/map versions after the typed content-free resolution event; surviving evidence may later rebuild a new claim/artifact ID through the normal pipeline. Extend `claim_review_events` with bounded actor kind `employee|system_source_erasure`; reviewer is nullable only for the system kind, whose typed event records before/after status and reason without free text. Only after these business rows/events commit may the decision/adjudication be deleted. Failure rolls back the entire erasure.
- In the same successful finalization transaction that applies the disposition, insert the one constrained **output-phase** subject: a claim target for inserted/new-held/applied-duplicate effects, or a candidate target with `review_hold_candidate` when a duplicate candidate is held and the existing claim remains unchanged. No input subject changes after transport, and fallback/failure adds no output. All existing schema, taxonomy, evidence, sensitivity-positive, and promotion rules remain authoritative; Jev may only add review/veto.
- This step asks only support/contradiction/qualification questions for veto-or-review. Claim kind, semantic role, impact, domain, and sensitivity classification are explicitly out of scope and get no hidden secondary output from this pack.
- Keep all existing schema, taxonomy, evidence, entity, sensitivity-positive, and promotion rules authoritative.
- Add `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-claim-support.ts`, adversarial claim/quote fixtures, and `evals/jev/claim-support.md`.

**Acceptance criteria**

- No Jev result bypasses an existing rejection/quarantine/review rule.
- Existing sensitivity-positive results are OR'd with Jev; Jev cannot clear them.
- Unsupported, contradicted, materially qualified, and negated new candidates stay pending review and never auto-approve; those duplicate candidates append zero evidence/entities/domains and cannot alter an approved claim.
- Low-confidence/provider/audit outage preserves byte-for-byte current insertion/duplicate/auto-approval outcomes and creates no `review_held` rows. Recovery affects only newly evaluated work; prior fallback outcomes are not silently reopened. A bounded canary/eval may measure the missed additive review signal but cannot mutate those claims.
- The sealed claim-support held-out set has at least 500 cases, 100 each of supported, unsupported, contradicted/negated, materially qualified, and sensitivity/duplicate-review cases, disjoint from tuning. False `continue_current` on any unsupported/contradicted/negated/materially-qualified case is zero; sensitivity-positive clearance is zero; supported claims sent to extra review are at most 2%; every stratum's recall is no worse than the current validator; p95/p99 added latency is ≤750/1,500 ms; fallback is 100%; and total spend remains inside the active epoch. Any error beyond those ceilings blocks activation.

**Verification gate**

- Existing extraction, R5 quote-validator, promotion, and document auto-approval verifiers stay green in both worker paths.
- The verifier proves supported new/duplicate candidates insert/append once; successful high-risk review cases perform no unsafe duplicate mutation; every timeout/provider/audit/malformed/low-confidence outage fixture exactly matches Jev-off insertion, duplicate, and document-auto-approval results with zero holds; recovery creates no accumulated backlog. Eligible allow commits claim/evidence/effect/audit/approved status atomically; review commits pending claim plus the exact typed hold and vetoes approval; fallback preserves the exact existing auto-approval result. Fresh-install and snapshot tests inspect both replacement check expressions, then attempt every status/pointer/hold-kind combination: held-new without its pending claim/promotion fields and held-duplicate without its duplicate claim target fail, while both valid variants commit. Faults after promotion, after auto-approval, and before finalization roll back the entire transaction; a concurrent control change cannot leave an approved claim with a stale/discarded decision; no independent approval helper path remains. Evidence/hash/config drift falls back; concurrent workers cannot append twice; prompt injection changes no deterministic rule. Admin-action tests deny non-admin/direct mutation; accepted held duplicates append validated evidence once without changing approval; rejection appends nothing; repeated/concurrent actions have one auditable winner; source/quote/target/decision/config drift and sensitivity/taxonomy failure remain held with zero partial writes. New-claim tests cover creation, approval, rejection, both claims-page forms, ordinary pending claims, double-submit/concurrent races, evidence deletion/hash drift, and fault rollback across claim/candidate/decision writes. Erasure tests cover held new/duplicate rows, approved claims with zero or independent surviving evidence, simultaneous human review, and repeated erasure; no affected hold/claim/artifact version survives, an unaffected duplicate target remains byte-identical, an affected target is deleted with the same chain, surviving evidence rebuilds only later under a new ID, exactly one source-independent system outcome exists, and decision deletion never precedes resolution. UI exposes count, kind, reason, target, evidence, and both actions.
- Production activation is owned only by 7C below; 7B may collect shadow/canary evidence but cannot switch active or post a live-success claim.

**Step 7C/7C-C — separately activate and record claim support**

- 7C starts only after the merged 7B artifact satisfies the sealed held-out/shadow gates and all promotion/auto-approval/erasure regressions. A new exact-head-reviewed manifest-only PR changes only the claim-support activation manifest; it does not mutate live mode, settings, or generation. Run `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:jev-claim-support-activation -- --assert-head <sha> --evidence <7b-artifact>` before merge and on the merged SHA; deploy it, obtain the sealed single-use technical APPROVE, dispatch the protected activation workflow to apply the manifest, and run one bounded approved claim flow covering either a truthful allow or review hold without weakening deterministic validators. The immutable artifact binds PR/merge/deployment, config generation/fingerprint, 7B digest, command digest, typed claim/candidate effects, cost/latency, canary outcome, and rollback state without claim text.
- 7C-C independently runs `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:jev-claim-support-activation-live -- --artifact <path>`, checks live config/deployment/generation and deterministic-validator results, records the issue/artifact, and updates only STATUS/evidence prose. Any mismatch or safety miss returns only claim support to shadow and leaves 7C open.

**Natural cut:** Step 7A schema migration/proof, Step 7B shadow delivery, 7C activation, and 7C-C closeout are separate sessions/outcomes. Step 6C and 6C-C are likewise separate. Extraction prefilter and claim-support never share a live proof.

### Phase E — Runtime gates

#### Step 8A-1/8A-2/8A-3 — Contradiction pair integrity, shadow screening, then activation

**Step 8A-1 — schema-only release**

- Add `contradiction_dedup_archive`, `contradiction_dedup_archive_sources`, and the pair-reservation table/columns/ordinary indexes to `packages/db/src/schema.ts` and the generated migration. Reserved raw migration `106_jev_contradiction_pair_integrity.sql` contains only the deterministic populated-data repair, archive population, expression/partial uniqueness that Drizzle cannot represent, owner functions/triggers, RLS/grants, and constraints; schema/catalog drift tests reject any raw-only table or duplicate DDL.
- Add a check rejecting self-pairs and a database-authoritative unique expression index on `(LEAST(claim_a_id,claim_b_id),GREATEST(claim_a_id,claim_b_id))`. Before migration, one transactional preflight builds a deterministic loser→survivor map per unordered pair (oldest creation time, then lowest UUID) and validates **every** known reference: scalar `business_model_changes.contradiction_id`, `gaps.related_contradiction_id`, `oracle_interventions.related_contradiction_id`; `messages.metadata_json.contradictionId`; `oracle_context_packs.included_contradiction_ids[]`; and sibling `contradictions.assigned_gap_id`/`new_message_id`. Abort before mutation on malformed/non-array JSON, invalid embedded UUID, orphan assigned gap, or any unrecognized contradiction-ID storage shape. Repoint scalar/metadata references; replace/de-duplicate context arrays while preserving order; and retain every gap/message. The survivor's business/audit payload remains byte-identical **except** its two assignment columns: a non-null survivor assignment always wins; a null assignment is intentionally filled from the oldest-created then lowest-UUID valid sibling. Additional distinct sibling gap/message relationships survive through the repointed rows and immutable archive/source closure rather than being collapsed into the single survivor columns. No description, severity, confidence, retrieved-claim, decision, question, resolution, model, or timestamp field is merged.
- The same transaction inserts every loser into immutable, retention-managed `contradiction_dedup_archive` before deletion. Each archive row records migration ID, loser/survivor IDs, deterministic survivor rule, archive time, a typed snapshot of **every** contradiction column (including description, severity, confidence, retrieved claim IDs, interjection decision, suggested question, status/resolution metadata, timestamps, and model provenance), plus canonical full-row JSON and hash so a future column cannot be dropped unnoticed. Model-run identity is retained as an immutable scalar snapshot rather than a cascading FK. Store real ordered claim FKs with `NO ACTION` and a companion `contradiction_dedup_archive_sources` closure containing every contributing source-anchor FK across all claim-bearing kinds with `NO ACTION`; a query-origin anchor is included only if it actually contributed persisted claim content. This lets authorized erasure find content even after the loser contradiction is gone. RLS allows reviewed admin read only; no runtime role may insert/update/delete, and only the named owner retention/erasure functions may delete. The transaction verifies one matching archive row, complete source closure, and hash per loser after all reference repoints and before deletion, records map/counts, verifies no loser reference remains, then deletes only archived duplicate rows and creates the unique index. A populated-conflict fixture proves the survivor is unchanged, every loser value is byte-for-byte recoverable, all references are repointed, rerun is idempotent, and any archive/source-closure insert/count/hash failure rolls back the entire repair.
- In reserved `packages/db/migrations/sql/106_jev_contradiction_pair_integrity.sql`, add the canonical pair-reservation table keyed by ordered low/high claim FKs with `NO ACTION`, current claim hashes, use-case/config fingerprint, lease token/expiry, attempt state, and timestamps, together with the dedup archive/repair above. It is transient coordination, not durable business history: stale active leases become failed within 24 hours, and terminal reservations delete after 30 days because durable decision/disposition rows hold the result. The ordered claim/source erasure procedure locks and deletes related active/terminal reservations before claim deletion; direct claim deletion remains blocked. Add the migration and its constraints/indexes to the catalog. Merge, migrate, verify, and smoke unchanged contradiction/admin flows before dependent code.
- Archive retention is explicit: absent earlier source erasure, delete archive rows and source joins 365 days after migration after recording only aggregate migration counts in a source-independent control event. Any authorized deletion of either claim or any contributing source anchor locks and deletes the related archive/source joins in the same ordered erasure transaction before the parent; no copied description, question, identifier, JSON, hash, or source link survives. Direct parent deletion fails through `NO ACTION`. Race fixtures cover erasure versus active reservation/finalization, archive retention versus erasure, either claim, every contributing claim-bearing source kind including a linked query-origin anchor, rollback at each delete phase, and zero surviving copied content/dangling row.
- In the same Step 8A-1 PR, extend `config/runtime-db-access.json` with every pair-reservation CRUD/function path, worker-only RLS or owner-function policy, exact worker grants, and regression IDs; compare fresh/snapshot effective privileges before migration. Direct web/service/browser mutation is denied.

**Step 8A-2 — files/functions**

- `apps/workers/src/trigger/contradiction-watcher.ts:155-232,450-452`: keep vector shortlist; batch Jev Choice questions over pair categories `contradiction`, `compatible_exception`, `refinement`, `different_scope`, `unrelated`, plus severity Score if useful.
- In shadow, compare with current adjudicator. In active, only confidently negative pairs skip the generative call. Possible contradiction and all uncertainty continue to the existing model, which still writes explanation/question text.
- Use one canonical unordered pair key `(min(claim_id),max(claim_id))` in sweep selection, dispatch, storage, and lookup. Persist both claims and their hashes. Replace claim-wide “already has a contradiction” exclusion with a pair-aware helper: a claim stays eligible whenever at least one current ANN-shortlisted pair has neither an existing contradiction row for that exact unordered pair nor an exact current-version `skip_current` disposition. Unrelated contradictions involving either claim never suppress a new pair.
- Reuse the pair-aware helper immediately before reservation. Before any Jev, generative adjudication, or live-drafting call, compare-and-swap the canonical reservation; an A–B/B–A loser exits with zero provider calls. The winner revalidates both approved claims/hashes after reservation. Finalization locks the reservation and verifies its lease token; contradiction insertion, gap/intervention/message effects, negative disposition, and reservation completion commit atomically. An expired lease may be stolen only by compare-and-swap, and a stale worker cannot finalize. The unique index is the final write backstop. Existing contradictions/current negative dispositions suppress only their exact pair; policy/model/pack/threshold/hash drift invalidates only that pair and reevaluates once.
- Preserve `packages/oracle-engines/src/interjection.ts` decision thresholds, cooldowns, caps, and status rules.
- Add `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-contradiction-screen.ts` and `evals/jev/contradiction-screen.md`.

**Acceptance and verification**

- The sealed contradiction held-out set has at least 400 pairs: 100 severe/material positives; 100 hard qualifiers split into at least 20 each of conditional, temporal, scope, exception, and negation; and 200 true negatives. Require 100% recall on the 100 severe/material positives, at least 98% recall in every qualifier stratum, no more than 1% false-positive suppression on true negatives, zero change to created contradiction records versus baseline, and at least 25% fewer generative calls on true negatives. Added latency is p95 ≤750 ms and p99 ≤1,500 ms, fallback succeeds 100%, and spend remains inside the active epoch; any miss stays shadow.
- Regression fixture: A–B has an existing positive contradiction, A–C a current durable negative, and A–D is unseen. The sweep selects A, makes zero calls for B/C, and one for D; the second sweep makes zero after D receives a disposition. Reversed order is identical. D hash or pack/threshold/data-policy/requested/resolved-model drift reevaluates only A–D once; an unrelated C–E contradiction suppresses neither A–C nor A–D.
- A barrier-controlled A–B/B–A race produces exactly one provider call, one contradiction, and at most one gap/message/intervention. Reversed direct inserts fail the unique index. Crash fixtures before provider, after provider, and before commit recover once after lease expiry; an old token cannot finalize after takeover; unique-conflict handling loads the winner and creates no duplicate side effect.
- Migration fixtures put winner/loser IDs in every scalar/JSON/sibling location, including arrays containing both; two assigned gaps/messages remain preserved, only contradiction-row count decreases, the typed archive/source closure exactly matches the loser, and dangling-reference queries return zero. They cover non-null-survivor-wins and null-survivor-fills-from-oldest cases, assert the exact two permitted assignment changes, and prove every other survivor byte is unchanged. Malformed JSON/UUID, orphan assignment, incomplete source closure, or archive hash mismatch aborts with zero writes; rerun is idempotent. Retention/erasure fixtures delete either claim and every contributing source and prove the archive plus pair reservation leave no copied content or orphan.
- The same reuse fixture changes data-policy version, revokes the old policy, changes requested/resolved Jev version, and proves each invalidates the old disposition; no lookup may reactivate an older-policy/model result.
- Atomically reserve the first 50 confident-negative pairs each ISO week (or all when fewer exist), then every tenth later unique pair through the current adjudicator. Human-review every disagreement; one missed material contradiction or more than 1% adjudicated false negatives over the latest 100 canaries changes only this gate to `shadow`, alerts admins, and makes subsequent sweeps ignore its negative dispositions.
- Link the canary adjudicator's model run and both claims, persist its versioned category/severity as the bounded comparator outcome, then append the human verdict; do not infer rolling error from a transient log or recompute it from a moving model.
- Run worker typecheck and existing contradiction/interjection tests.
- 8A-2 deploys/proves this outcome in shadow only: negative dispositions are recorded/comparison-scored but cannot suppress a generative contradiction call. Active suppression is reserved for 8A-3.

**Natural cut:** Step 8A-1 schema/migration/data-repair proof and Step 8A-2 shadow worker delivery/live proof are separate sessions. Never deploy reservation-dependent code before the pair schema is proven, and stop after accepted shadow evidence.

#### Step 8A-3/8A-3-C — Contradiction negative-path activation and closeout

- 8A-3 starts only after accepted 8A-2 held-out/shadow evidence proves the stated material-contradiction recall and canary thresholds. Its separate exact-head-reviewed PR changes only `config/jev-activation-manifests/contradiction-negative-path.json`; after merge/deploy, a fresh sealed technical APPROVE authorizes the protected workflow to perform the exact `shadow→active` transition and run bounded seen-negative, unseen, reversed-order, race, crash, safety-miss, and fallback canaries. Failure returns only this gate to shadow and resumes all current generative calls.
- 8A-3-C independently verifies the immutable artifact, live generation/fingerprint, deployment SHA, suppression counts, current-path rollback, and human canary result, then updates only STATUS/evidence prose.

#### Step 8B-0 — Freeze the real Recall inbound contract

- Before a schema migration or dependent code change, capture Recall's current official `transcript.data` contract and redacted, size-bounded payloads from one authorized synthetic test-bot meeting. The fixture set covers ordinary delivery, retry, corrected/revised transcript text, missing participant or word timing, missing bot/transcript fields if Recall accepts such a variant, and the actual signature/content-length headers. Record which fields are vendor-guaranteed immutable, merely observed, nullable, or absent. This is a read/test-only outcome and authorizes no production message.
- Freeze a versioned parser/identity matrix from that evidence. Prefer a documented durable vendor event ID; otherwise use the complete proven immutable coordinate tuple. A valid currently accepted webhook lacking either identity form must not be rejected by the migration: classify it `exact_payload_weak`, but still require the text-independent parent `recall\0bot_id\0transcript_id` anchor/admission permit before persistence; dedupe only byte-identical event retries, preserve the unchanged current analyzer/post path while that parent is active, force the Jev gate off for it, and alert/count it. Step 8B-2 cannot enable its new hard-negative or durable-send path until 100% of the sealed fixtures and bounded live canary carry strong identity; any weak event keeps current behavior rather than being silently suppressed.
- Freeze revision semantics too: same strong identity plus same payload hash is a retry; same strong identity plus a new hash before send appends and selects a new immutable revision; a changed revision after `send_started|sent|send_unknown` is a `revision_conflict`, cannot create a second post, and enters admin review. The contract artifact pins canonicalization bytes, ordering, normalization, and signature verification. Independent review approves this artifact before Step 8B-1 starts.

**Natural cut:** stop after the contract artifact, scrubbed fixtures, and independent approval. Schema work is a different session and must implement the frozen matrix rather than assumptions from the current TypeScript types.

#### Step 8B-1 — Live-Recall delivery schema

**Schema-only release**

- Add `live_recall_channels`, `live_recall_deliveries`, `live_recall_delivery_revisions`, and every new compatibility column on `live_recall_legacy_ingress` to `packages/db/src/schema.ts` plus the generated migration. Reserved raw migration `107_jev_live_recall_delivery.sql` is limited to legacy backfill, non-representable constraints/indexes, owner functions/triggers, RLS, and grants; clean-install and production-snapshot drift tests reject any raw-only table/column or generated/raw duplicate.
- Add `live_recall_channels` as the canonical bot→Oracle-channel registry: bounded `recall_bot_id` primary key, `channel_id` unique non-null FK, timestamps, and no channel-name identity after backfill. A preflight searches the exact legacy display name `Teams live meeting <botId>` used by current code: zero matches creates one mapping later, one match backfills it, and multiple matches abort for admin reconciliation rather than guessing. The web inbox owner function advisory-locks the bot ID and creates/loads the channel plus mapping in the same transaction, so concurrent first delivery cannot create twins. Only that exact web function may create a mapping; workers may read it, admin may inspect/reconcile through a separate authenticated function, and generic browser/service/runtime mutation is revoked. Source/channel erasure handles the mapping in the same ordered lock contract. A 100-request same-bot fixture produces one mapping/channel; different bots stay isolated.
- Add `live_recall_deliveries` in `packages/db/src/schema.ts` plus reserved `packages/db/migrations/sql/107_jev_live_recall_delivery.sql` as the durable strong-identity webhook inbox and send state machine. `channel_id` references the canonical mapping; Recall identifiers are **not** FKs because no authoritative vendor tables exist. Store the bounded contract fields proven by 8B-0 without invented `unknown-*` substitutes, `identity_kind=vendor_event_id|full_coordinates`, `event_key`, and `payload_hash` only while its source anchor is active. The owner function—not caller text—derives strong keys from the frozen immutable vendor ID or coordinate bytes, excluding transcript text. `source_message_id` is nullable only in `received` and becomes required/unique once persisted. Store only the strictly parsed, size-bounded normalized envelope needed to reconstruct the utterance—not arbitrary raw webhook JSON—with source classes, received time, and explicit retention below. States are `received→analyzing→no_post|post_ready→send_started→sent` plus `retryable_pre_send|send_unknown|reconciled_sent|reconciled_not_sent|revision_conflict|unknown_expired|source_erased|failed_terminal`, with lease/version fields, frozen-snapshot/result/draft hashes, nullable bounded approved draft retained only where specified, nullable external-message ID plus receipt hash/timestamps, bounded error metadata, and timestamps. Source erasure atomically clears envelope, event/payload/result/draft/receipt hashes, external/source/channel IDs, and any other correlator before retaining only random delivery ID, state, bounded code, and timestamps. Add one-delivery-per-source and one-live-intervention-per-delivery constraints.
- Add append-only `live_recall_delivery_revisions` keyed by `(delivery_id,revision)` with unique `(delivery_id,payload_hash)`, normalized envelope, received time, superseded-revision link, processing state, and the same retention/erasure classification. The delivery head stores the selected revision and advances by compare-and-set. Same key/hash returns the existing revision; changed hash before send appends and supersedes prior work so only the newest selected revision may analyze; changed hash at/after `send_started` appends `revision_conflict`, alerts, and can never schedule or post automatically. Concurrency tests cover simultaneous retry/correction and revision arrival at every send boundary.
- Reuse and extend Step 1A's already-live `live_recall_legacy_ingress` table for valid `exact_payload_weak` events; migration 107 may add only the reviewed compatibility columns/constraints needed to coexist with strong delivery. Every row has a non-null parent Recall transcript anchor/version and cannot insert after its tombstone. It continues to store content for at most 24 hours, clear content immediately on terminal handling, retain only hash/status for 30 days, and remain unable to reference a Jev decision or enter the strong delivery/send state machine. The upgraded route owner function returns either a strong delivery+revision or the existing legacy-ingress ID, never an ambiguous shape, and production-snapshot migration tests prove all pre-107 ingress rows remain replayable/erasable.
- In the same PR extend `config/runtime-db-access.json` with mapping, strong delivery, revision, weak legacy-ingress, both task entrypoints, web inbox, admin, sweeper, retention, and erasure operations; exact web/worker/authenticated-admin RLS or owner-function policies/grants; denied generic service/browser mutation; and real-login regression IDs. Fresh/snapshot effective-privilege comparison is a migration gate.
- Retention is executable and state-specific. Normalized ingress content lives at most 24 hours and is cleared atomically when the source message is created; a still-`received` row at 24 hours becomes `failed_terminal`, clears content, and raises one alert. Approved draft text exists only in `post_ready|send_started|send_unknown`, clears immediately on `sent|no_post|reconciled_sent|reconciled_not_sent|source_erased|failed_terminal`, and may remain in `send_unknown` for at most 7 days for reconciliation; at 7 days it becomes `unknown_expired`, clears text, and writes a control event. Daily retention deletes non-erased terminal rows after their stated 90/365-day windows; a `source_erased` row has already lost every hash, external/source/channel ID, and correlator and retains only random delivery ID, state/code/timestamps for at most 30 days. Open `send_unknown` never silently deletes before its 7-day terminal transition. Step 8B-1 extends the versioned source-erasure handler to lock affected deliveries, perform that complete scrub, dismiss alerts/reviews, and invalidate linked decisions before source deletion. Fake-clock tests cover every state/TTL boundary, content/hash/identifier clearing, all source-kind erasures, concurrent sweeper/reconciliation/retention, and zero orphan/dangling rows.
- Step 8B-1 contains no import, query, or writer for the new table. Merge the schema-only PR, run clean-install and current-production-snapshot migration rehearsal, perform the guarded production migration, verify constraints/RLS/grants with the worker login, and smoke the unchanged Recall webhook/task path. An unchanged-code smoke and zero missing-relation errors are required before Step 8B-2 may merge.

**Natural cut:** stop after the schema, runtime-access manifest, migration, and unchanged-code production smoke are proven. Automatic deployment of any dependent reader/writer before that proof is forbidden.

#### Step 8B-2 — Durable Live-Recall delivery code and shadow evaluation

**Files/functions**

- Add a new Trigger task `teams-live-recall-delivery` plus `apps/workers/src/lib/live-recall-delivery.ts`; it accepts only `{deliveryId,selectedRevision}` and owns the strong durable/Jev path. Atomically upsert source message plus delivery; duplicates load/resume state rather than exit. Lease analysis for 2 minutes so expired `analyzing` is recoverable. Persist the frozen snapshot, Jev/current-analyzer results, and approved draft before transport. Under the existing channel lock, recheck kill switches, direct-mention behavior, cooldown/rate limits, then atomically reserve `send_started`; reservations and unknown sends count against limits. Commit before HTTP and call Recall once. Change `sendRecallChatMessage` from `void` to a bounded `RecallSendReceipt` containing HTTP status, response hash/timestamps, optional request ID, and optional durable external message ID parsed only when the endpoint actually returns it. HTTP success uses a fenced transaction to write `sent`, receipt, one assistant message, and one intervention. Timeout/reset/5xx or crash after `send_started` becomes `send_unknown`, alerts, and is never automatically resent. A 429 before a definitive send uses `Retry-After` capped at 10 minutes and `retryable_pre_send`; definite 4xx is terminal. Re-enable at most three Trigger attempts because state-machine resume, not blind replay, controls work.
- Keep Step 1E's ID-only `teams-live-recall-utterance` as the explicit legacy weak-identity entrypoint and preserve `runLegacyLiveRecallEnvelope`; Step 8B-2 changes no legacy task payload or logging contract. It continues to load/lease the normalized envelope, execute unchanged current behavior with Jev forcibly off, and terminally clear it. It never accepts a delivery ID/revision and never calls the durable helper. The legacy sweeper redispatches this task by ingress ID; identical weak payload retries reuse one row/lease; a corrected weak payload may create a legacy revision only while the same parent anchor is active, and after parent erasure both identical and corrected deliveries block before ingress, channel, or task creation. Regression tests rerun Step 1E's golden envelopes and require identical channel resolution, model input, cooldown/caps, and post/no-post result.
- Add an admin-only unknown-send queue under `/admin/ai/decisions` plus `reconcileLiveRecallDelivery(deliveryId,expectedVersion,outcome,observedExternalMessageId?,observedContentHash,evidenceUrl)`. Its authenticated owner RPC derives the admin via `auth.uid()` and accepts no actor assertion. Only `send_unknown` may transition. `posted` requires observed content hash equal the retained approved-draft hash plus bounded evidence; external ID is stored/required only when the contract probe proves Recall or Teams exposes one. Then atomically insert exactly one assistant message/intervention keyed to delivery, transition `reconciled_sent`, clear draft, resolve alert, and record one event. `not_found` creates no business rows, transitions `reconciled_not_sent`, clears draft, resolves alert, and records the event. Neither outcome resends. Stale version, hash mismatch, repeated/concurrent action, non-admin, or expired/source-erased row makes zero change. Browser/database tests cover both receipt shapes and outcomes, races/idempotency, expiry, audit/alert resolution, and erasure.
- Export `sweepLiveRecallDeliveriesOnce(now)` and invoke it exactly once in the reserved pre-batch maintenance block of the existing ten-minute `claim-extraction-batch-drain` schedule; add no schedule. In bounded indexed pages and within its reserved 60-second cap it compare-and-set claims expired strong `received|analyzing|post_ready|retryable_pre_send` work and redispatches `teams-live-recall-delivery` with delivery ID/revision; it separately leases and redispatches weak rows to `teams-live-recall-utterance` with legacy-ingress ID. `send_started` older than 2 minutes becomes `send_unknown` plus one alert and is never redispatched. A sweeper/dispatch error joins the drain's bounded aggregate error, marks `job_runs` failed, and makes the scheduled task reject even when there are no extraction batches. The ten-minute recovery pass leaves successful/terminal rows to the separate daily retention rules above. Add an integration fixture proving decision maintenance and both Recall sweep paths run before bounded batch draining on no-work and 10,000-backlog runs, with lease takeover, cursor progress, dispatch failure visibility, and zero new `schedules.task()` exports.
- Fix `apps/web/app/api/teams/live/recall/route.ts` with a 256 KiB pre-buffer streaming cap. Reject invalid/negative/multiple or declared-over-cap `Content-Length` before reading; otherwise read `req.body` incrementally, count actual bytes for missing/chunked/underreported lengths, cancel immediately above the cap, and return 413 with no signature/JSON/database/dispatch work. Reject a declared/actual mismatch. Only after the bounded exact bytes are available may the route verify their signature and parse against the frozen 8B-0 variant matrix. The database inbox function derives identity/revision and commits before any 202: strong identity resolves the canonical bot mapping and returns delivery+selected revision; weak identity writes/returns a legacy-ingress ID and alert/counter without requiring a channel. The route dispatches the corresponding distinct Trigger task/input shape. Successful enqueue returns 202. Failed enqueue leaves committed recoverable state and returns non-2xx so Recall retries immediately. A crash/fault before commit returns no acknowledgement and leaves no row; a crash after commit/before/during dispatch is recoverable from the matching inbox. Each worker loads its normalized envelope by its own ID and never trusts task-supplied event content.
- In `teams-live-recall-delivery.ts`, reuse extracted pure current-analyzer/draft helpers from the logic now at `apps/workers/src/trigger/teams-live-recall-utterance.ts:292-318,379-491,756-900`; only the strong task may place Noul `worth_intervention` and Choice `contradiction|gap|direct_question|none` before those helpers. It first constructs one immutable `LiveDecisionSnapshot` used by both Jev and the current analyzer. The snapshot contains the current persisted message, every ordered recent utterance ID/content hash serialized into `recentContext`, the retrieval-plan hash, every retrieved claim, each supplied claim-evidence quote hash and source message/chunk, and every supplied Brain-section ID/content hash. The legacy entrypoint calls its preserved orchestration with Jev off and never enters this strong block.
- Before transport, link the query origin, every recent/current `message_id` exactly once, every retrieved `claim_id`, each `claim_evidence_id` and its source message/chunk, and every `brain_section_id`. The decision key hashes the complete ordered snapshot, meeting/session hash, locale, permission-scope hash, and control versions. Message persistence failure, retrieval failure, incomplete/duplicate source linkage, or snapshot drift makes zero Jev calls and fails open to the current analyzer. Jev and comparator consume the same frozen snapshot; no second retrieval may produce different comparison context.
- Direct mentions, force-test paths, kill switches, confidence thresholds, cooldowns, and rate caps remain deterministic.
- A positive/uncertain result invokes the current generative model to draft; Jev never writes the Teams message.
- Atomically reserve the first 20 `none` decisions each ISO week (or all when fewer exist), then every tenth later unique decision through the existing analyzer as non-posting canaries. The current model's draft/interjection recommendation is captured for comparison but cannot post from the canary path; disagreements enter admin review.
- Link the analyzer model run and every source in its frozen snapshot, persist a bounded versioned recommendation comparator, and append human adjudication for disagreements plus the required random agreement sample. No message text or draft is copied into the decision tables.
- Add `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-live-recall-gate.ts` and `evals/jev/live-recall-gate.md` using scrubbed/synthetic utterances before any live meeting.

**Acceptance and verification**

- Direct mention always reaches current behavior.
- Timeout/low confidence fails open to the current model, not to posting.
- No extra message can be posted from Jev output alone.
- Fixtures prove Jev and comparator receive byte-identical snapshot payloads; every recent utterance, claim, evidence/source, and Brain section is linked before transport. Deleting any one before finalization blocks the negative effect, and deleting it later discards the decision/prevents reuse. Retrieval/link failure makes zero Jev calls and preserves the analyzer.
- The verifier covers every sealed 8B-0 payload variant plus exact-limit and limit+1 bodies with truthful, missing, chunked, oversized, underreported, overreported, invalid, and duplicate `Content-Length`; over-limit streams cancel before authentication/database/dispatch, and declared/actual mismatch fails. Fields that the frozen contract marks required/invalid fail before insert; valid nullable variants are accepted. Same strong identity+hash is idempotent; a pre-send changed hash appends/selects one revision and cancels stale work; a post-send changed hash becomes one non-posting `revision_conflict`; a changed coordinate is a distinct event. Exact-payload weak identity dedupes byte-identical retries, never invokes Jev or the new send path, and preserves current behavior only under its active parent transcript anchor. A corrected-text weak delivery shares that parent; before erasure it follows the bounded legacy revision behavior, while after parent erasure both original and corrected payloads hit the same permanent tombstone and produce zero ingress/channel/dispatch/message writes. It then injects failures before inbox commit, after commit before dispatch, on dispatch false/throw, before source persistence on all three worker attempts, after source persistence, analysis, `post_ready`, before/after `send_started`, after Recall 200 before database finalization, and after final commit. It also covers sweeper recovery, 100 concurrent same-bot first deliveries producing one mapping/channel, 100 concurrent duplicate/revised events, different bots, two utterances racing one channel, stale lease, Jev fallback, both send-receipt shapes, 429, timeout, 5xx, and 4xx. Every acknowledged strong utterance has recoverable inbox state; every accepted weak utterance follows unchanged behavior and is counted. Confirmed-send fixtures produce exactly one assistant record/intervention. Timeout/reset/5xx or crash after `send_started` produces one `send_unknown` plus alert, zero fabricated business rows, and no automatic resend; only authenticated reconciliation may close it.
- Deploy the merged Step 8B-2 SHA only after 8B-1 production proof, with the Jev gate `off` or shadow/non-posting. Run the real worker-login regression, public-synthetic decision probe, webhook dispatch-failure check, and delivery/sweeper verifier against production infrastructure without authorizing a live Teams post. Record the deployment and schema version; code delivery is complete only when no missing-table/grant failure occurs.

**Natural cut:** stop after durable code is deployed and its non-posting recovery path is proven. Do not combine the first live meeting post with schema migration or code delivery.

#### Step 8B-3 — Live-meeting intervention proof

- Starts only from the production-proven 8B-1 schema and 8B-2 deployment. Re-run the production/action gate and independent review for the exact merged SHA, meeting/test controls, bot, time window, kill switch, and restoration command before any live post.
- First run a code-owned API-contract probe against the exact Recall endpoint/test bot and authenticated Microsoft Graph test-chat view. Positive eligibility requires a durable Recall request/message receipt **and** exact Teams chat/message IDs, plus provider contracts and narrowly enforceable credentials that can retrieve and delete that exact post or prove its bounded inaccessible expiry. Pin the field/schema and live allow/deny evidence. HTTP 200, timestamps, response/content hashes, or human Teams observation without both stable identifiers are observational only and block Step 8B-3 before any post; the database external-ID fields may never be null on `sent`.
- Only after that prerequisite and Step 1E6-C are current may the bounded test send one uniquely tagged direct-question fixture expected to post. Positive proof binds the exact merged/deployed worker SHA and deployment ID to one coordinate event key, one delivery ending `sent`, stable Recall and Teams IDs, authenticated read-back, one linked assistant message/intervention, one final Jev/current-analyzer audit record, settled spend, and exact delete-or-expire lifecycle proof through the already-proven erasure adapter. Replay the identical webhook/task and prove all counts stay one. If either ID/lifecycle operation is unavailable, no live post occurs and the milestone remains blocked; observational evidence can never substitute.
- One human-confirmed missed direct question, material contradiction, or intervention-worthy gap—or more than 2% adjudicated false negatives over the latest 100 canaries—changes only the live gate to `shadow` and raises the existing admin alert before further negative suppression.
- The live test uses the existing bounded test controls, then restores the production clamp and verifies no post-clamp messages.
- This is a distinct session from contradiction screening and requires independent reviewer approval before the technical production action.
- Step 8B-2 installs protected default-branch workflow `.github/workflows/jev-live-recall-post.yml`, CODEOWNED manifests under `config/jev-live-recall-post-manifests/`, and package command `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:jev-live-recall-post -- --manifest <merged-manifest> --action prove`. The command accepts only the merged manifest path/digest and dispatches the workflow; it receives no production credential or free-form message. The manifest binds exact worker deployment SHA/ID, test bot/meeting, one fixed synthetic direct-question fixture hash, coordinate event key, start/end window, expected current clamp/mode/generation/policy/model/pack/threshold, 1E6-C artifact, provider contract/credential-scope proofs, kill switch, and rollback target. The workflow rechecks the sealed technical APPROVE and all bindings before credentials, flips only the one live-Recall test control, sends exactly one fixture, runs authenticated read-back/replay/delete-or-expire assertions, and in an always-run `finally` invokes the same typed transition to restore the pre-test clamp and proves no post-clamp message. Any missing, ambiguous, duplicate, failed, or rollback result makes the workflow and command exit nonzero.
- The content-free immutable 8B-3 artifact schema is `{manifestDigest,approvalDigest,workflowRunId,workflowAttempt,sourceCommit,deploymentId,deploymentSha,closureDigest,meetingFixtureDigest,eventKeyDigest,startedAt,endedAt,deliveryState,recallReceiptPresent,recallMessageIdPresent,teamsChatIdPresent,teamsMessageIdPresent,readBackCount,replayCounts,assistantCount,interventionCount,decisionRunCount,spendSettled,providerLifecycleOutcome,clampBefore,clampAfter,postClampMessageCount,rollbackActionDigest,result}`; IDs that identify the provider/meeting remain only in the sealed workflow request, while the artifact records presence/count/digests. Success requires `sent`, every required presence true, all counts exactly one except replay remains one and `postClampMessageCount=0`, settled spend, verified delete-or-expire, restored clamp, and `result=passed`. Standalone recovery uses `pnpm --filter @oracle/workers run verify:jev-live-recall-post -- --manifest <same-merged-manifest> --action restore-clamp`; it is idempotent, cannot post, and exits nonzero until the clamp and zero-post proof are current.
- 8B-3-C is a fresh documentation-only session. It retrieves the GitHub-attested artifact, re-runs its schema/signature/digest/current-deployment/clamp checks, confirms the exact workflow succeeded and the rollback proof is current, updates only STATUS/evidence prose, and merges no code/config/workflow. A failed 8B-3 remains open with the clamp restored and one owned correction issue; it never advances to closeout or another live proof.

After **each** provider call, one transaction persists its context pack, one model run, output hash, usage/attempt rows, and pack backlink before the next stage. Missing/deleted subjects roll back everything and make zero calls. After transport, a finalization transaction settles spend and terminal outcome through the activation fence. Shadow failure never changes the existing publish/fail-closed result. Any future active mode must atomically finalize the decision and insert the assistant message; message and decision cannot succeed separately. Deleting either linked model run triggers source erasure.

Jev/provider/audit failure changes nothing in the existing fail-closed pipeline and cannot publish a rejected ledger.

Failure injection at every model-attempt insert leaves no partial run/usage/attempt/backlink and starts no later call. Reviewer-audit failure preserves reconciliation audit but creates no Jev run and publishes nothing. Missing subjects roll back origin/run/links/reservation. Crash before transport becomes `discarded/process_interrupted`; crash after transport is conservatively settled/discarded and cannot publish. Finalization/message failure rolls back both; hash mismatch, post-link mutation, duplicate provider-call audit, or rendered-byte mismatch cannot authorize reuse. Bounded orphan cleanup removes only abandoned staged packs/pending audits and preserves completed runs.

This plan does **not** authorize replacing or bypassing the current semantic reviewer; any cost-saving replacement needs its own STATUS row/plan after evidence.

Steps 9A/9B preserve citation identity and the new reconciliation guarantees while measuring Jev only against the actual current pipeline.

### Trust-boundary adversarial-cases table

Every row names a required automated test. This table applies before any production activation.

| External input / boundary | Hostile or failure case | Required proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 migration boundary | Malformed/duplicate order, partial historical effect, wrong target, lock contention, Drizzle/raw crash, proof/key/checkpoint tampering, secret/log leak, interrupted snapshot, stale version artifact, unallowlisted sequence/catalog change, or retired command/seed bypass | `pnpm verify:m0` runs the named pure, baseline, fresh, proof-chain, target/secret, and rehearsal-fault suites; candidate CI and the strict merged-SHA deployed artifact must both pass |
| TypeSafe API key | Missing, malformed, revoked, or accidentally included in an error | `typesafe-decision-contract`: preflight/failure is redacted; fallback runs; no key substring in logs |
| TypeSafe response | Wrong answer type, missing/duplicate question ID, non-finite probability, probability outside inclusive 0–1, or answer probabilities whose raw IEEE-754 sum has `abs(sum - 1) > 0.000001` | Contract test performs no normalization, rounding, clipping, or missing-answer fill; it accepts exactly the inclusive ±0.000001 sum boundary, rejects the next representable values beyond it plus NaN/Infinity, records `invalid_response`, and preserves current behavior |
| Model alias/version | `jev-latest` moves or response reports unexpected resolved version | Contract test requires pinned request and logs resolved version; version mismatch prevents active effect |
| Service availability | Timeout, connection reset, 429, 529 | Mocked transport test proves bounded behavior, visible failure record, and current-path fallback |
| Cancellation/deadline | An application-owned retry attempt or late response outlives the caller's latency budget | SDK `maxRetries:0` contract plus fake-clock tests prove one total deadline aborts every separately leased application attempt and a late result cannot affect behavior |
| Request size/count | State+longest question or complete serialized request exceeds conservative byte caps, or question/option counts exceed app limits | Boundary/multi-byte/JSON-overhead tests refuse before network call and record `policy_blocked` |
| Choice cardinality | More than 255 candidates | Builder test shortlists deterministically or refuses; it never truncates silently |
| Prompt injection in state | Source text says to ignore criteria or choose a specific answer | Per-pack injection fixtures; result cannot bypass deterministic candidate/permission gates |
| Adversarial option text | Candidate label contains instructions, HTML, or control text | Pack encodes candidates as data with stable IDs; verifier proves only supplied IDs can return |
| No valid option | None of the candidates applies | Every Choice pack that can miss includes `none/no_supported_candidate`; test proves fallback |
| Ambiguity | Two near-equal candidates or diffuse probability | Threshold test routes to fallback/review; no forced autonomous choice |
| Negation and qualification | “Not required”, “except seasonal”, conditional/temporal scopes | Domain-specific fixtures prove the pack distinguishes support, contradiction, exception, and unrelated |
| Arithmetic/date content | Exact counts, deadlines, date order, rate caps | Test proves arithmetic/date/limit logic remains code-only and is not sourced from Jev Score |
| Unsupported language | Chinese or mixed-language input below evaluated support | Language-policy test keeps current path; no hard exclusion or skip |
| Sensitive/HR content | Employee complaint, private HR matter, personnel name | Data-class gate blocks network unless explicitly approved; sensitivity-positive current gate remains authoritative |
| Licensed source | R2 or uploaded licensed text sent before vendor approval | Data-class test blocks call; synthetic fixture path still works |
| Duplicate/replayed result | Same request retried or late response arrives after state changes | State/input hash test prevents applying stale judgment to changed state; duplicates remain auditable |
| Stale disposition identity | Policy is revoked or model/pack/threshold/source version changes | Lookup test requires exact policy, requested/resolved model, pack, threshold, decision-key, and source hashes; old disposition never reactivates |
| Logging | Raw employee/source text or full response appears in general logs | Log-capture test asserts redaction; decision tables keep hashes, bounded answers, subject links, and policy metadata—not raw state |
| Audit persistence | Pending run, subject-link, final decision, or combined business-effect write fails before/after provider success | Fault-injection test proves Jev effect is discarded, current behavior runs, and no partial record is labeled successful |
| Data-class downgrade/policy drift | Caller labels employee/licensed/mixed content as public, omits provenance, uses a pack wider than policy, or references a revoked policy | Provenance/policy test recomputes the union, adds `unknown_restricted` when incomplete, requires `actual ⊆ pack ⊆ active version`, records that version, and blocks before network access |
| Pasted free-text provenance | Employee/MCP text embeds licensed, internal, HR, or customer material that its channel identity cannot prove | Content-independent mapping applies the full possible union plus `unknown_restricted`; calls remain blocked unless every class is approved, and any future narrowing needs its own reviewed deterministic plan |
| Process crash | Worker/web process dies after pending insert or provider response | Fake-clock/restart test discards stale pending rows, applies no effect, and permits one clean retry |
| Overlapping extraction windows | One window is Jev-negative while another owner extracts or fails | Owner-reconciliation matrix proves a completed owner wins and only all-negative ownership becomes skipped |
| Cross-run extraction ownership | Reconciliation sees only the current job or scans JSON, so an older completion is ignored | Normalized indexed membership joins every owner across job runs under ordered locks; cross-run/replay races converge |
| Capture-directive spoofing | Caller text/metadata marks a message forced and bypasses policy, or a real force request is suppressed | Only authenticated server-owned exact command/UI actions persist the enum; near matches/direct writes fail and flagged windows make zero suppression calls |
| Post-promotion support veto | Jev rejects after unsupported evidence was already appended to a new/approved claim | Disposition is fenced inside promotion before any claim/evidence/domain/entity mutation; duplicate-review fixtures append nothing |
| Claim-wide contradiction exclusion | One existing contradiction prevents unseen pairs for the same claim from screening | Canonical pair-aware selection/redispatch tests preserve A-D while suppressing only exact A-B/A-C pairs |
| Production drift | New business language makes a formerly safe negative gate miss valuable content | Deterministic canary/human-label test crosses the limit, atomically changes only that flag to `shadow`, and alerts admins |
| Source erasure/retention | A message, document, meeting transcript, external-system copy, manual-admin evidence, query origin, claim/evidence, synchronous model/embedding call, provider batch/job/file/result, Storage/backup/cache, Recall/Teams message, Trigger/Vercel history, job JSON/error, assistant reply, meeting metadata/summary, workflow-map quotation, candidate, claim summary/embedding/translation, entity, Brain section, relationship, model run, reservation, or dedup archive is deleted/expired | The saga freezes the canonical anchor under the outbound-lease lock, drains/records every in-flight call, keeps its exact manifest only in a temporary managed secret, proves live external deletion/inaccessible expiry and GitHub zero-disclosure, locks the full database/derived closure, deletes every contaminated artifact version and downstream chain, resolves versioned business state, deletes source/lineage in FK-safe order, destroys the temporary request, and retains only random IDs plus counts/codes/timestamps. A nonreversible retained tombstone makes every pre-erasure restore run reconciliation before traffic, and final success waits until no backup/PITR point can contain the source; unknown calls/copies, identifying proof metadata, unlinked content, direct deletion, restore bypass, and partial failure remain blocked/retryable and never report success |
| Long-lived extraction skip | Decision audit expires while a `skipped_by_decision` batch/message still needs recovery | Batch-level version/key snapshot survives run retention; replay test deletes the run first and still restores only the exact eligible window |
| Direct database access | Browser/admin/service-role SQL bypasses immutable policy, spend, audit, or mode controls | RLS plus revoked DML and named fixed-path owner functions enforce typed transitions; direct privileged mutation tests fail |
| Release ordering | Auto-deployed code queries new tables, retains migration-owner credentials, or accepts company data before erasure works | Steps 1A–1D prove schema/roles and all manifest regressions, then cut Trigger and Vercel separately; Step 1E proves ID-only dispatch/logging, database-derived closure, external/platform-history replica inventory, and physical erasure before any non-synthetic pilot; exact identity/privilege/business/erasure probes pass |
| Runtime secret drift | Key exists only in 1Password or one host, or a deployment predates the env update | Separate Vercel Production and Trigger `prod` names-only checks, post-update redeploy IDs tied to merged SHAs, and one public-synthetic target-path probe each |
| Trigger capacity | Decision maintenance accidentally adds one or more schedules to the existing 9/10 project | Static export-count test proves no new `schedules.task()` and the worker deployment proves the project remains at 9/10 schedules with its reserved headroom |
| Spend cap | Parallel/restarted calls or run retention race past or silently refill the approved balance | Permanent spend-epoch row serializes reservations; tests delete all child runs and prove the microusd cap remains spent/blocked until a fresh exact-owner approval is atomically consumed for a replacement epoch |
| Active config drift | Model/pack/threshold/policy changes while a use case remains active | Locked change forces shadow, clears old evidence, increments generation, discards in-flight results, and requires new exact-fingerprint held-out/review approval |
| Shared scheduler failure/starvation | Provider batches fail, polling hangs, overlapping cron runs collide, or continuous arrivals starve older work while the ten-minute Trigger run reports success or never reaches recovery | Fixed pre-batch budgets and cancellable poll deadlines preserve the 90-second shutdown reserve; a non-extendable 9m45 task lease permits next-tick recovery; oldest-due ordering, a 20-minute age gate, and a 20-arrivals/25-cap capacity envelope prevent silent starvation; any aggregate, timeout, or capacity failure marks the job failed and rejects the scheduled run |
| Cost calculation | Missing token usage or changed price | Test records cost unknown rather than zero; pricing constant/version is explicit and documented |
| Feature flag | Missing, malformed, or unexpected value | Config test defaults to `off`; active behavior requires exact `active` |
| Deterministic gate conflict | Jev says approve/match but exact evidence, permission, status, or type check fails | Integration test proves deterministic rejection wins every time |
| Live Teams direct mention | Jev says “none” on an explicit Oracle invocation | Live-gate test proves direct mention bypasses Jev suppression |
| MCP disabled capability | Jev selects a disabled or unauthorized tool | Registry intersection test removes it and falls back safely |
| MCP exact-name regression | Jev assigns higher probability to a different tool than an enabled exact-name query | Deterministic preflight pins the exact enabled match first; limit-1/limit-2 tests prove Jev cannot displace it |
| Synthetic probe spoofing | An arbitrary MCP caller copies the probe text or supplies a synthetic flag to bypass the data policy | Only the code-owned builder plus exact fixed fake-catalog/query bytes can create the synthetic origin/class; no live capability metadata participates, and near-matches/caller assertions are blocked before transport |
| Entity metadata provenance | Labels/aliases are sent without linking, classification, or invalidation when the registry changes | Every serialized candidate has a typed entity subject and conservative class union; mutation changes input identity and deletion discards the whole decision |
| Retrieval false negative | Jev rates valid approved evidence low | Shadow/rerank-only test proves candidate remains available until recall acceptance is met |

## 10. Tests required

### New foundation tests

- Each M0 milestone owns one runnable green gate using only merged dependencies. M0R's gate includes governance plus the credential-free infrastructure-approval verifier; M0G/G-C creates/records exact-SHA empty cloud boundaries. M0I1/I1-C journals and transfers six replicas; I2/I2-C proves migration/KMS; I3/I3-C reviewer; I4/I4-C deployment; I5/I5-C settings. M0U/U-C is read-only PostgreSQL 17 proof. M0S/S-C owns constant suppression. M0V0/V0-C alone installs/proves the manifest-aware Vercel setting; V1/V1-C deploys/proves web; V2/V2-C deploys/proves worker. M0X0/X0-C owns only enable metadata; X1/X1-C enabled web; X2/X2-C enabled worker. M0F/F-C alone owns database activation. Every implementation and closeout has its exact direct command in the gate matrix; `pnpm verify:m0` aggregates only already merged suites and rejects unowned files, commands, or combined outcomes.
- `packages/ai/src/__verify__/typesafe-decision-contract.ts`
  - validates all three primitives and normalized responses;
  - rejects malformed answers/cardinality/state/data-class violations, mixed unapproved classes, unknown provenance, and caller downgrade attempts;
  - tests 100/101-question boundaries, 255/256 Choice options, Score levels 1/2/10/11 (only 2–10 pass), exact serialized 32,000/64,000-byte edges, JSON overhead, many short questions, and multi-byte Chinese text before any mocked transport call;
  - verifies redaction, pinned model, resolved version, tokens/cost/latency, and failure taxonomy;
  - uses an injectable fake transport and requires no live key.
- `packages/db` migration/schema tests verify the dedicated decision tables, constraints, indexes, RLS, immutable successful outcomes, query-origin/subject links, and current-version disposition lookup; migration catalog verification proves every new hand-written SQL file is listed in `packages/db/migrations/sql/README.md`.
- Runtime-access tests scan `config/runtime-db-access.json` for every central/raw/service client call and transitive deployed operation, compare exact fresh/snapshot-clone grants for both runtime roles, and execute every manifest regression ID on real PostgreSQL. Platform cutover probes verify actual Vercel/Trigger identities/privileges and migration-credential absence.
- `packages/ai/src/__verify__/typesafe-decision-live.ts`
  - opt-in only; uses a synthetic public fixture;
  - exits non-zero on provider failure or contract drift;
  - prints only summary metrics, never key/state bodies.
- Add scripts `verify:typesafe-decision-contract` and `verify:typesafe-decision-live` to `packages/ai/package.json`; only the no-key contract verifier belongs in routine CI initially.
- Add root `verify:jev-contract` to `package.json` and an explicit network-free “Jev decision contract” step to `.github/workflows/pr-check.yml`. When claim support/contradiction/live gates land, also add named CI steps for existing `@oracle/engines verify:r5`, `verify:r5.5`, `verify:r6`, `verify:r7`, and `verify:r11.1`; they are mandatory safety regressions and are not currently CI-wired. Every later network-free verifier is wired into the affected package's existing required suite or added as its own named PR-check step in the same PR; an eval script that CI never calls is unfinished.

### Per-use-case tests

- MCP: extend `apps/web/lib/mcp/__verify__/mcp-registry.ts`.
- Entity selection: extend package registry/fallback coverage and add required DB-backed `apps/web/lib/__verify__/chat-entity-selection-route.ts`; live vendor fixture remains opt-in.
- Responsibility selection: add isolated verifier without editing the live R2 prompt/contract.
- Extraction prefilter: `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-extraction-prefilter.ts`.
- Claim support: `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-claim-support.ts` plus existing R5/promotion/auto-approval suites.
- Contradiction: `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-contradiction-screen.ts` plus existing interjection tests.
- Live Recall: keep worker state-machine coverage in `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-live-recall-gate.ts`, and add required web-route verifier `apps/web/lib/__verify__/recall-webhook-delivery.ts` plus `@oracle/web verify:recall-webhook-delivery` in the required PR check. It invokes the actual Next route with streamed `Request` bodies and fake dispatch/database seams, proving the byte ceiling is enforced incrementally, the signature covers untouched raw bytes, JSON parsing occurs only after verification, strong versus weak envelope dispatch is exact, raw payloads never log, duplicate/revision races are idempotent, and only the owner function persists the inbox. Worker-only verification cannot complete Step 8B-2.
- Retrieval reranking: `apps/web/lib/__verify__/jev-business-answer-rerank.ts`, wired into `@oracle/web verify:business-answer`.
- Reconciled-answer shadow audit: `apps/web/lib/__verify__/jev-chat-grounding.ts` covers the real reconciliation/review topology; attachments bypass.
- Reconciliation audit schema: Step 9B-1 proves nullable `model_runs.output_hash`; the shared writer covers every chat provider-call class with canonical structured/exact-text/null-on-untrusted hashes. Step 9B-2 failure/orphan tests prove per-attempt atomic audit, accepted reconciliation/review persistence before Jev preflight, complete subjects committed before transport, conservative crash settlement, and atomic future decision/message publication.
- Extraction recovery: the prefilter verifier exercises dry-run and applied `scripts/replay-jev-extraction-skips.ts` against isolated fixture rows in both dispatch modes.
- Decision review lifecycle: add an admin-page verifier for open/resolved/dismissed states, idempotent actions, redaction, and non-admin denial.
- Decision health: add a fake-clock/database verifier that races at least 100 parallel calls for the **same** identity and 100 distinct keys; prove one reservation/counter increment for the duplicate race, exact first-N weekly minimum (or all lower traffic), every-tenth post-minimum sampling, atomic agreement-review quota, rolling denominators, human-adjudication updates, fenced `active`→`shadow`, alert emission, and isolation from unrelated flags. Change each fingerprint component midweek and prove every new configuration starts its own first-N cohort.
- Pending reconciliation/retention: export `runDecisionAuditMaintenanceOnce` for a fake-clock fixture that seeds unrelated stale keys, invokes the shared ten-minute drain hook, proves all deadline+grace rows discard in bounded pages, proves fresh rows remain, and covers query-origin expiry plus every retention/erasure age boundary and orphan cleanup.
- Schedule-capacity/failure verification counts `schedules.task()` exports before/after, requires the total to remain nine, and proves `claim-extraction-batch-drain` reserves pre-batch decision/origin/Recall/retention maintenance without weakening bounded provider progress or the 90-second shutdown reserve. Add `apps/workers/src/__verify__/jev-decision-maintenance-drain.ts` and a package script. Cover zero batches with maintenance success/failure, healthy and transiently failed batches, both the 10,000-backlog/400-tick failure profile and the steady 20-arrival acceptance profile, concurrent invocations on actual ten-minute alignment, kill at 599 seconds, stale lease takeover, never-returning Anthropic/OpenAI/Vertex polling, and forced phase/task deadlines in fake-clock and real-PostgreSQL runs. Every admitted run invokes each enabled maintenance family first within its 60/60/30-second budget, advances all cursors, selects due jobs strictly oldest-first, processes no more than 25 provider batches/300 seconds, stops new work by 510 seconds, proves the 20-arrivals/20-minute capacity gate, preserves retryable failures with bounded metadata, and reports aggregate/finalization/capacity failure by failing `job_runs` and rejecting/non-zero. Record a successful worker deployment/capacity result before completion.
- Each accepted pilot adds a versioned Markdown result under `evals/jev/` with dataset provenance, exact command, model version, question-pack version, threshold curve, errors, latency, tokens, total cost, and cost per correct decision.
- Every activation artifact identifies disjoint tuning and sealed held-out dataset versions, satisfies the larger of the use-case-specific counts above or a generic floor of 400 total with at least 50 examples in every decision-bearing class and 100 negatives, predeclares the label rubric, threshold, latency/cost budgets, and zero-tolerance safety classes before sealing, and reports independent double-labeling of at least 20% and 80 cases. Inter-rater agreement must be Cohen's kappa ≥0.80 overall and 100% consensus after adjudication on every zero-tolerance material case. Threshold comes from tuning only; the untouched held-out score must meet all fixed accuracy/error, p95 ≤750 ms, p99 ≤1,500 ms, 100% fallback, regression, and active-spend ceilings. Re-running after a failed held-out gate requires a newly versioned held-out set; never tune against failed acceptance cases, lower a floor, merge strata, or call the rerun independent.
