## 11. Constraints, standing rules, and gotchas

- Start every implementation step in its own current-upstream worktree and declare its task class with `ai-task-gates`.
- One session owns one unproven live behavior. Do not bundle MCP, extraction, contradiction, or Teams production proofs.
- Never push directly to `main`; open, review, and merge the PR. Documentation-only PRs may use the documented owner override after verifying every changed file is prose.
- Before first commit, verify `Albert Hazan <u2giants@users.noreply.github.com>` with `git var GIT_COMMITTER_IDENT`.
- Stage only owned files. Do not touch the dirty shared checkout or active R2 worktree.
- Treat every reviewer-instruction, gate-policy, and module-resolution surface as part of M0R's independently owned production closure even when it is not a direct runtime import: root/nested `AGENTS.md`, any `AGENTS.override.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, reviewer prompt/template/config files, `.ai-devops/task-gates.json`, root `tsconfig.base.json`, every extending `tsconfig*.json`, Node/pnpm loader hooks, package `exports`/`imports`, and bundler/runtime alias config. The manifest hashes them and CODEOWNERS matches them explicitly; the recursive scanner resolves privileged imports through those exact inputs and fails on an unowned resolver. The production reviewer launches from a clean neutral directory outside the snapshot, mounts repository content read-only as untrusted data, and receives only the pinned M0Q top-level brief. M0Q plants adversarial instruction/source text demanding APPROVE; the reviewer must flag but never obey it. M0R fixtures change every instruction/policy/resolver surface, redirect a privileged import through aliases/loaders, and prove the closure digest, independent last-push approval, gate-strength check, and merge refusal all fire.
- The only valid production-review invocation is `ai-codex-review final-check --base <merged-parent> --assert-head <merged-sha> --sealed-brief PRODUCTION_APPROVAL_BRIEF.json --untrusted-snapshot-root`; any earlier abbreviated example omitting neutral-root isolation is non-authoritative and must fail the M0E contract test.
- M0D3 owns the authoritative revocation schema/script; M0E adds protected default-branch `.github/workflows/production-approval-revoke.yml`. The append-only registry uses sorted unique v1 entries `{artifactDigest, runId, workflowRunId, workflowAttempt, revokedAt, effectiveAt, reasonCode, sourceApprovalDigest, sourceReviewRun, sourcePlanDigest}`; Git blob/commit/ETag are fetched evidence. Entries cannot be edited/deleted. `revoke-production-approval.mjs --plan` creates two content-addressed outputs after sealed APPROVE. First, the revocation workflow validates that approval and uses only its ephemeral `GITHUB_TOKEN` with `actions: write` and all other permissions `none` to cancel the exact run/attempt; it does not checkout source, cannot read/write contents or PRs, and emits only status. Second, a separate current-upstream worktree under the ordinary repository author identity applies the precomputed JSON patch, pushes a branch, and opens the sensitive-path PR through CODEOWNER/last-push/check rules; it never receives the workflow token. Contract tests prove cancellation cannot touch contents/PRs and the PR step cannot cancel runs. Executors have only Actions/contents read and before every action/renewal/abort/commit fetch no-cache registry commit/blob/ETag/digest plus exact run/attempt status; listed, canceled, incomplete, or unavailable state fails. Cancellation is immediate; merged registry is durable; until either is observed revocation is not claimed. Renewal/abort are separate sealed-brief approvals bound to original request/artifact, proof head, remaining action, and current state. Crash/race tests cover cancel/PR order, API outage, stale ETag, duplicates, canceled reruns, mistaken revocation, and fail-closed recovery without unrevoking history.
- Authoritative human-managed secret records live in 1Password vault `vibe_coding`; use `secrets-to-1password` for creation/update and never expose values. M0I's six Secret Manager entries—two for migration, one for reviewer, two for deployment, and one for Vercel project settings—are narrow delivery replicas with fingerprinted rotation, not a second editable source; both Cloud KMS keys are non-exportable and never enter 1Password, GitHub, the database, or a runner.
- `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is server-side only. Never put it in client bundles, browser env, logs, issue/PR text, or command arguments.
- Keep TypeSafe SDK logging explicitly `off` in every environment; warning/error/debug SDK logging is forbidden because SDK error/debug paths may expose request state. Oracle records only its own bounded redacted error taxonomy.
- Jev accepts text only. Images/audio/video must follow existing transcription/parsing paths before any bounded text judgment.
- Pin `jev-1.13.0`; upgrades require rerunning every active pack's evaluation and thresholds.
- Keep arithmetic, dates, counts, limits, price calculation, lifecycle, permissions, and exact matching in code.
- Use `none` outcomes and uncertainty fallbacks. Never force a choice from incomplete candidates.
- Do not infer multilingual safety from English results. Evaluate `zh-CN` separately or leave it on the current path.
- Existing provider batch behavior is unrelated. Do not route Jev through generative Batch APIs or the model catalog.
- The active R2 handoff remains open. Do not alter its prompt/production behavior while doing the isolated Jev quote-selection evaluation.
- No shared-database structural change is planned. If one emerges, stop and route it through the applicable database governance instead of improvising.
- Before any production trigger or deployment action, run the required task-gate check and obtain independent reviewer approval where the standing production rule requires it.
- Scheduled/worker calls must fail visibly in telemetry; a Jev outage may preserve current product behavior but must not look like a successful Jev decision.

## 13. Definition of done, risks, rollback, and open questions

### Definition of done

- [ ] Every STATUS row through M0U-C has its own merged commit where applicable, exact-head approval, direct green command, and STATUS transition. M0R includes the independently reviewed, credential-free exact-digest infrastructure-approval issuer in its twenty-root closure. M0G creates only exact-SHA empty cloud boundaries. M0I1/I1-C through M0I5/I5-C separately deliver/journal, prove migration/KMS, reviewer, deployment, and settings boundaries; only I5-C unlocks read-only M0U. No M0I pair runs database SQL, deployment, or setting mutation.
- [ ] M0W merges identity-probe-only workflows. M0R protects them, registers/tests the twenty-root closure and independent GitHub-review attestation issuer, and M0R-C records the live rule/issuer proof before M0G.
- [ ] M0G/G-C establishes five exact-M0G-SHA identities and empty resources. M0I1 writes/read-backs its journal before any capability or secret-bearing bootstrap, completes or rolls back all six rows, and I1-C proves zero live bootstrap. M0I2–M0I5 each merge one workflow, obtain one independent exact-digest approval, receive one external Cloud-Shell old-SHA→new-SHA IAM transition, prove only that identity, and close separately. Old/unapproved SHAs, PRs, forks, siblings, and workload IAM mutation are denied at cloud trust.
- [ ] M0E merges both the rehearsal route and complete activation route while the repository guard remains false; tests prove early activation fails before authority or connection. Its approved rehearsal produces the schema-valid, current-SHA artifact with pinned runner identity, three complete real-production full-root timing runs inside the stated margin, KMS MAC, public GitHub attestation, and cleanup proof; M0E-C independently verifies/records that artifact before M0V starts.

### Rollback

- M0 is forward-only repository infrastructure, not a credential boundary. After its control bootstrap reaches production, supported automation uses the guarded runner; old root/package/direct entrypoints in current code fail closed, while historical binaries and Supabase-owner tools remain an explicitly unsupported administrator risk until Steps 1A–1D cut credentials over. Operational recovery pins the last verified guarded-runner image/commit, restores service with that version, and advances with a new reviewed numbered control/raw correction plus a chained proof. Tests attempt a current-code rollback path, old alias, ledger deletion, checksum downgrade, partial control-schema downgrade, catalog break-glass drift, and between-run application DML: current repository paths make zero writes, control/catalog drift invalidates proof, and legitimate row changes do not falsely fork the chain. Database restore is disaster recovery under a separately reviewed production action, never a shortcut to replay migrations.
- Every use case has `off|shadow|active`; first set the affected use case to `off` and redeploy only if code-path changes require it.
- A Jev service failure before any audited active outcome preserves the pre-Jev behavior. Active negative gates can create durable dispositions, so rollback must reconcile those records rather than claim that no repair is needed.
- Extraction rollback runs `scripts/replay-jev-extraction-skips.ts` in dry-run, reviews the exact durable batch pack/threshold/data-policy snapshot, window, and linked message IDs, then applies the bounded reset to `pending` and verifies the existing extractor completes them. Retained decision rows remain immutable audit evidence, but replay never depends on their surviving the 365-day retention window.
- Contradiction rollback turns the gate off; the watcher ignores Jev negative dispositions when mode is off. A pack/threshold version change naturally reconsiders pairs because only a matching current-version disposition suppresses repeat evaluation.
- The foundation migration is additive. Removing Jev code does not drop its audit tables; table removal, if ever desired after retention review, is a separate migration.
- If an active pilot causes quality regression, capture the failing request hash/question-pack version/result, turn only that pilot off, and preserve the evidence for evaluation. Do not disable unrelated Oracle capabilities.

### Principal risks

- Vendor retention/residency/subprocessor terms may not be acceptable for employee or licensed data.
- False-negative extraction/contradiction gates could silently suppress knowledge or intervention.
- Confidence may be well calibrated in aggregate but wrong on a specific Oracle subdomain.
- Jev is primarily English; Chinese and mixed-language content may underperform.
- Prompt injection or adversarial source text can influence the decision.
- Large/noisy state can cause context rot and erase the expected quality advantage.
- Provider launch pricing, limits, model behavior, and availability may change.
- A cheap additional verifier can still increase total latency if placed on synchronous chat paths.
- Too many small packs/flags can become maintenance burden; reject low-value pilots instead of keeping permanent experiments.

### Open questions and decision criteria

1. **Will TypeSafe provide enterprise ZDR and acceptable DPA/security terms?** If no, restrict the integration to synthetic/public/low-sensitivity state or stop the company-data pilots.
2. **Does a $5 capped evaluation prove real value?** Continue only when cost per correct decision and total workflow cost improve without missing the quality gate.
3. **Which pilots survive?** Acceptance is per use case, not ideological. A pilot that fails accuracy, privacy, latency, or maintenance value is documented and removed.
4. **Should Jev ever make a hard negative decision?** Only after its use-case-specific frozen fixture proves required recall and an uncertainty/failure fallback exists. Until then, rerank, veto, or shadow only.

## Mandatory plan self-audit

1. **Could a brand-new AI session execute this plan without asking a question? — Yes.** Sections 1–4 define the business outcome, application, trigger, and boundaries; §5–§8 carry the current state, evidence, rejected approaches, locked/open decisions; §9 gives ordered file/function-level steps and verification gates; §§10–13 specify tests, rules, access, landing, risks, rollback, and owner decisions.
2. **Does the plan carry the investigation's background, nuance, and rejected approaches? — Yes.** Sections 5–7 preserve the audited code seams, active R2 collision risk, privacy boundary, model limitations, and every important rejected architecture. The adversarial table converts trust-boundary risks into named tests.
3. **Is the ultimate goal clear enough to steer a correct judgment if a step is wrong? — Yes.** Section 1 makes cost reduction subordinate to knowledge recall, evidence quality, privacy, and employee trust, and explicitly says the goal overrides a conflicting step.

Checklist result: all 13 required sections are present; the plan is self-contained; every build step names concrete files/functions and a verification gate; trust-boundary cases name tests; locked/open decisions and out-of-scope items are explicit; test commands, secrets locations, deployment/proof requirements, and plan/handoff backlinks are present. **Self-audit passed on 2026-09-20.**
